1.5.
Notation

Our notation largely matches the one introduced in
[
ACC+18
, §1.2]
.
If
F
is a perfect field, we let
\overline{F}
denote an algebraic
closure of
F
and
G_{F}
denote the absolute Galois group
\Gal(\overline{F}/F)
.

If
F
is a number field, we let
S_{p}(F)
be the set of
places of
F
above
p
. If
S
is a finite set of finite places of a
number field
F
, we let

G_{F,S}
denote the Galois group of the maximal extension
of
F
that is unramified outside
S
. For a prime
\ell
, we let

\epsilon_{\ell}
denote the
\ell
-adic cyclotomic character
and
\bar{\epsilon}_{\ell}
denote its reduction modulo
\ell
.

If
\pi
is an irreducible admissible representation of
\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}}_{F})

and
\lambda\in({\mathbb{Z}}^{n})^{\Hom(F,{\mathbb{C}})}
is dominant for the standard
upper triangular Borel subgroup, we say that
\pi
is regular algebraic
of weight
\lambda
if the infinitesimal character of
\pi_{\infty}
is the
same as that of
V_{\lambda}^{\vee}
, where
V_{\lambda}
is the
algebraic representation of
\mathrm{Res}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}\GL_{n}
of highest
weight
\lambda
. See §
2.1.11
for a discussion
of highest weight representations.

If
K
is a finite extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
for some prime
p
,
we write
I_{K}
for the inertia subgroup of
G_{K}
,
\mathrm{Frob}_{K}\in G_{K}/I_{K}

for the geometric Frobenius and
W_{K}
for the Weil group.
We write
{\operatorname{Art}}_{K}:K^{\times}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}W_{K}^{\mathrm{ab}}
for the Artin map of
local class field theory, normalized to take uniformizers to
geometric Frobenius elements. We let
\mathrm{rec}_{K}

denote the local Langlands correspondence of
[
HT01
]
,
which sends an irreducible smooth (admissible) representation
\pi

of
\GL_{n}(K)
over
{\mathbb{C}}
to a Frobenius semi-simple Weil–Deligne representation

\mathrm{rec}_{K}(\pi)
of
W_{K}
, also over
{\mathbb{C}}
. We
also write
\mathrm{rec}_{K}^{T}
for the arithmetic normalization
of the local Langlands correspondence, as defined for example
in
[
CT14
, §2.1]
; this normalization is defined for
coefficients in any field which is abstractly isomorphic to

{\mathbb{C}}
, such as
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
. We define labelled Hodge–Tate weights of
p
-adic representations of
G_{K}
as in
[
ACC+18
, §1.2]
. In particular,
\epsilon_{p}
has Hodge–Tate weight
-1
.

If
G
is a locally profinite group and
K
is an open
subgroup, we write
{\mathcal{H}}(G,K)
for the
{\mathbb{Z}}
-algebra of
compactly supported bi-
K
-invariant functions
f:G\to{\mathbb{Z}}
,
cf.
[
NT16
, Lemma 2.3]
.

We let
E/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
be a
p
-adic field
which will be our coefficient field, with ring of integers
{\mathcal{O}}
, uniformiser

\varpi
and finite residue field
k:={\mathcal{O}}/\varpi
. We let
\operatorname{CNL}_{{\mathcal{O}}}

denote the category of complete, local, Noetherian
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebras with
residue field
k
.

2.
The cohomology of locally symmetric spaces

2.1.
Preliminaries

In this section, we gather some preliminaries, and we
largely follow
[
ACC+18
, §2]
without giving complete details.

2.1.1.
Locally symmetric spaces

Let
F
be a number field
and
\mathrm{G}
be a connected linear algebraic group over
F
,
with a model over
{\mathcal{O}}_{F}
that we will still denote by
\mathrm{G}
.
We will denote by
X^{\mathrm{G}}
the
symmetric space

for
\mathrm{Res}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathrm{G}
, which is
a homogeneous space for
\mathrm{G}(F\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}}{\mathbb{R}})

as in
[
BS73
, §2]
and
[
NT16
, Definition 3.1]
(and which is determined by
\mathrm{G}

up to isomorphism of homogeneous spaces).

Let
K_{\mathrm{G}}\subset\mathrm{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,f})
be a
good
compact
open subgroup in the sense of
[
ACC+18
, §2.1]
: namely
it is
neat
and of the form
\prod_{v}K_{\mathrm{G},v}
, where

v
runs over the finite places of
F
.
We consider the double quotient

X^{\mathrm{G}}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}}:=\mathrm{G}(F)\backslash X^{\mathrm{G}}\times\mathrm{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,f})/K_{\mathrm{G}},

which is a smooth, orientable Riemannian manifold. We
also consider the partial Borel–Serre compactification

\overline{X}^{\mathrm{G}}
of
X^{\mathrm{G}}
as
in
[
BS73
, §7.1]
and form the double quotient

\overline{X}^{\mathrm{G}}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}}:=\mathrm{G}(F)\backslash\overline{X}^{\mathrm{G}}\times\mathrm{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,f})/K_{\mathrm{G}},

which is a compact, smooth manifold with corners
with interior
X^{\mathrm{G}}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}}
. We note that the spaces
X^{\mathrm{G}}
are always connected; when
\mathrm{G}({\mathbb{R}})
is not connected, it is sometimes better to work with
X^{\mathrm{G}}\times\pi_{0}(\mathrm{G}({\mathbb{R}}))
(equivalently, replacing the isotropy subgroup in the definition of the symmetric space with its identity connected component). Since
\mathrm{G}({\mathbb{R}})
will be connected in all the cases of interest to us, this will not concern us. Finally, we consider the boundaries
\partial X^{\mathrm{G}}:=\overline{X}^{\mathrm{G}}\setminus X^{\mathrm{G}}

and
\partial X^{\mathrm{G}}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}}:=\overline{X}^{\mathrm{G}}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}}\setminus X^{\mathrm{G}}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}}
.

We define
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{G}}:=\varprojlim_{K_{\mathrm{G}}}X^{\mathrm{G}}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}}
,
endowed with the projective limit topology,
where
K_{\mathrm{G}}\subset\mathrm{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,f})

runs over good compact open subgroups.
We also consider the analogous spaces
\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}

and
\partial\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{G}}
. All these spaces are
equipped with a continuous action of
\mathrm{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,f})
, which
is equipped with the locally profinite topology.
Note also that the spaces
\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}

and
\partial\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{G}}
are compact Hausdorff,
being projective limits of compact Hausdorff spaces. We denote
by
j:\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{G}}\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}
the
natural open immersion.
As a consequence
of
[
CGH+20
, Lemma 6.2.1]
and
[
NT16
, Lemma 2.31]
,
we see that the actions of any good subgroup
K_{\mathrm{G}}
on

\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}
and
\partial\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{G}}

are free in the sense of
[
NT16
, Definition 2.23]
. These limits have been considered previously by Rohlfs
[
Roh96
]
. It follows from the properness of the action of arithmetic groups on the symmetric space and its compactification (cf.
[
Roh96
, Proposition 1.9]
) that we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{G}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{G}(F)\backslash{X}^{\mathrm{G}}\times\mathrm{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,f}),~\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}=\mathrm{G}(F)\backslash\overline{X}^{\mathrm{G}}\times\mathrm{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,f}),

\displaystyle\text{and }\partial\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{G}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{G}(F)\backslash\partial X^{\mathrm{G}}\times\mathrm{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,f}),

with topologies induced by the locally profinite topology on the adelic groups. We prefer to work with these topological spaces, since they seem more natural than those used in
[
NT16
,
ACC+18
]
which equip the adelic groups with the discrete topology. We compare these set-ups (‘topological’ and ’discrete’) in the next subsection.

2.1.2.
Hecke operators and coefficient systems

If
S
is a finite set of finite places of
F

we set
\mathrm{G}^{S}:=\mathrm{G}({\mathbb{A}}^{S}_{F,f})
and
\mathrm{G}_{S}:=\mathrm{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,S})
, and similarly
K_{\mathrm{G}}^{S}=\prod_{v\not\in S}K_{\mathrm{G},v}
and
K_{\mathrm{G},S}=\prod_{v\in S}K_{\mathrm{G},v}
.
We write
{\mathcal{H}}(\mathrm{G}^{S},K_{\mathrm{G}}^{S})
for the global Hecke algebra
over
{\mathbb{Z}}
which is the restricted tensor product of the
local Hecke algebras
{\mathcal{H}}(\mathrm{G}(F_{v}),K_{\mathrm{G},v})

for
v
a finite place of
F
not contained in
S
.

Let
R
be a commutative ring and let
{\mathcal{V}}
be a smooth

R[K_{\mathrm{G},S}]
-module, which is finite free as an
R
-module.
We now explain how to obtain from it a local system
{\mathcal{V}}

of
R
-modules on
X^{\mathrm{G}}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}}
and how to equip
the usual and compactly supported cohomology groups

R\Gamma_{(c)}(X^{\mathrm{G}}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}},{\mathcal{V}})

with an action of the Hecke algebra
{\mathcal{H}}(\mathrm{G}^{S},K_{\mathrm{G}}^{S})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}R
,
by adapting the formalism of
[
NT16
]
to our topological setting.

Firstly, note that the
R[K_{\mathrm{G},S}]
-module
{\mathcal{V}}

defines a
\mathrm{G}^{S}\times K_{\mathrm{G},S}
-equivariant local system,
which we denote by
{\mathcal{V}}
as well,
on both
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{G}}
and

\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}
. Indeed, we first inflate
{\mathcal{V}}
to a smooth

R[\mathrm{G}^{S}\times K_{\mathrm{G},S}]
-module, which is equivalent to a

\mathrm{G}^{S}\times K_{\mathrm{G},S}
-equivariant sheaf on a point by
[
NT16
, Lemma 2.26]
, and then
we pull back this sheaf to
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{G}}
and
\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}
,
respectively.
From now on, we consider the
\mathrm{G}^{S}\times K_{\mathrm{G},S}
-equivariant sheaves

{\mathcal{V}}
and
j_{!}{\mathcal{V}}
on
\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}
.
By
[
Sch98
, §1, Lemma 1]
3
3

3

It is
assumed in
loc. cit.
that the coefficients have characteristic
0
, but this is not
used in the proof.
, the category of
\mathrm{G}^{S}\times K_{\mathrm{G},S}
-equivariant sheaves
on
\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}
has enough injectives. By
[
NT16
, Lemma 2.25]
,
since
\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}
is compact,
the global sections of a
\mathrm{G}^{S}\times K_{\mathrm{G},S}
-equivariant sheaf
on
\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}
form a smooth

R[\mathrm{G}^{S}\times K_{\mathrm{G},S}]
-module. We therefore have a
well-defined derived functor
R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},\ )
,
and we obtain

R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},{\mathcal{V}})\ \mathrm{and}\ R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},j_{!}{\mathcal{V}})

in the bounded below derived category of smooth

R[\mathrm{G}^{S}\times K_{\mathrm{G},S}]
-modules. We apply the
functor
R\Gamma(K_{\mathrm{G}},\ )
4
4

4

This is the derived
functor of
K_{\mathrm{G}}
-invariants considered with its profinite topology, so
it computes the
continuous
group cohomology of
K_{\mathrm{G}}
.
,
which gives rise to objects

R\Gamma(K_{\mathrm{G}},R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},{\mathcal{V}}))\ \mathrm{and}\ R\Gamma(K_{\mathrm{G}},R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},j_{!}{\mathcal{V}}))

in the bounded below derived category of

{\mathcal{H}}(\mathrm{G}^{S},K_{\mathrm{G}}^{S})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}R
-modules.

On the other hand, we can also view
{\mathcal{V}}
and
j_{!}{\mathcal{V}}

as
K_{\mathrm{G}}
-equivariant sheaves on
\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}
,
using the forgetful functor. Recall that the action of
K_{\mathrm{G}}
on
\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}

is free and that the quotient can be identified with
\overline{X}^{\mathrm{G}}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}}
;
let
\pi:\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}\to\overline{X}^{\mathrm{G}}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}}
denote
the projection map.
The
descent
functor
{\mathcal{F}}\to\left(\pi_{*}{\mathcal{F}}\right)^{K_{\mathrm{G}}}
gives an equivalence between the category
of
K_{\mathrm{G}}
-equivariant sheaves on
\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}

and the category of sheaves on
\overline{X}^{\mathrm{G}}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}}
by
[
NT16
, Lemma 2.24]
.
We denote the corresponding sheaves on
\overline{X}^{\mathrm{G}}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}}
by
{\mathcal{V}}

and
j_{!}{\mathcal{V}}
as well.

Proposition 2.1.3
.

The following diagram of derived functors is commutative

\textstyle{\mathrm{DSh}^{+}_{\mathrm{G}^{S}\times K_{\mathrm{G},S}}(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},\ )}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{forget}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{D}^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{G}^{S}\times K_{\mathrm{G},S},R)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R\Gamma(K_{\mathrm{G}},\ )}
\textstyle{\mathrm{D}^{+}({\mathcal{H}}(\mathrm{G}^{S},K^{S})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}R)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{forget}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DSh}^{+}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}}(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{descent}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DSh}^{+}(\overline{X}^{\mathrm{G}}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R\Gamma\left(\overline{X}^{\mathrm{G}}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}},\ \right)}
\textstyle{\mathrm{D}^{+}(R)}

Proof.

This is a topological version of
[
NT16
, Prop. 2.18]
.
The corresponding diagram of underived functors commutes up to natural isomorphism.
The forgetful functor from
\mathrm{G}^{S}\times K_{\mathrm{G},S}
-equivariant
sheaves to
K_{\mathrm{G}}
-equivariant sheaves is exact and preserves injectives by
[
Sch98
, §3, Corollary 3]
.
The descent functor is also exact and preserves injectives, since it is an equivalence of categories.
The functor
\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},\ )
preserves injectives by
[
NT16
, Lemma 2.28]
.
∎

Note that we have a canonical isomorphism

R\Gamma\left(\overline{X}^{\mathrm{G}}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}},{\mathcal{V}}\right)\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}R\Gamma\left(X^{\mathrm{G}}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}},{\mathcal{V}}\right)

induced by the pullback map
j^{*}
, because
j
is a homotopy equivalence,
and that
R\Gamma(\overline{X}^{\mathrm{G}}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}},j_{!}{\mathcal{V}})

precisely computes
R\Gamma_{c}(X^{\mathrm{G}}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}},{\mathcal{V}})
. This shows
how to construct morphisms

{\mathcal{H}}(\mathrm{G}^{S},K^{S}_{\mathrm{G}})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}R\to\End_{\mathrm{D}^{+}(R)}\left(R\Gamma_{(c)}(X^{\mathrm{G}}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}},{\mathcal{V}})\right).

The same formalism
also applies to
R\Gamma(\partial X^{\mathrm{G}}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}},{\mathcal{V}})
.

Lemma 2.1.4
.

The functor
R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},\ ):\mathrm{DSh}^{+}_{\mathrm{G}^{S}\times K_{\mathrm{G},S}}(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}})\to\mathrm{D}^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{G}^{S}\times K_{\mathrm{G},S},R)
has bounded cohomological dimension.

Proof.

We can check this after applying the forgetful functor to
K_{\mathrm{G}}
-equivariant sheaves. If
{\mathcal{F}}\in\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}}(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}})
, then
[
NT16
, Lemma 2.35]
implies that
R^{i}\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},{\mathcal{F}})
vanishes for
i>\dim(X^{\mathrm{G}}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}})
.
∎

We now compare our set-up with that of
[
NT16
]
. We set
\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}^{\mathrm{dis}}=\mathrm{G}(F)\backslash\overline{X}^{\mathrm{G}}\times\mathrm{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,f})^{\mathrm{dis}}
, where the superscript indicates that we are considering
\mathrm{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,f})
with the discrete topology. If we have a good compact open subgroup
K_{\mathrm{G}}\subset\mathrm{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,f})
, then
(K_{\mathrm{G}})^{\mathrm{dis}}
acts freely on
\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}^{\mathrm{dis}}
with quotient equal to
\overline{X}^{\mathrm{G}}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}}
. Using
[
NT16
, Lemma 2.19]
to make the Hecke action on cohomology explicit, we see that, whether we use the topological or discrete set-up, we will obtain the same Hecke actions on the cohomology of
\overline{X}^{\mathrm{G}}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}}
. We will prove something a little stronger than this, to convince the reader that the two different set-ups really are naturally equivalent.

There is a natural map
\pi_{\mathrm{dis}}:\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}^{\mathrm{dis}}\to\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}
which induces an exact functor

\pi_{\mathrm{dis}}^{*}:\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{G}^{S}\times K_{\mathrm{G},S}}(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{(\mathrm{G}^{S}\times K_{\mathrm{G},S})^{\mathrm{dis}}}(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}^{\mathrm{dis}}).

Using descent to
\overline{X}^{\mathrm{G}}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}}
for both the topological and discrete categories, we see that pullback by
\pi_{\mathrm{dis}}
induces an equivalence

\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}}(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}})=\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}^{\mathrm{dis}}}(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}^{\mathrm{dis}})
.

Lemma 2.1.5
.

We have a natural isomorphism of functors from
\mathrm{DSh}^{+}_{\mathrm{G}^{S}\times K_{\mathrm{G},S}}(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}})
to
\mathrm{D}^{+}({\mathcal{H}}(\mathrm{G}^{S},K^{S})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}R)
:

R\Gamma(K_{\mathrm{G}},-)\circ R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},-)\cong R\Gamma(K_{\mathrm{G}}^{\mathrm{dis}},-)\circ R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}^{\mathrm{dis}},-)\circ\pi^{*}_{\mathrm{dis}}.

Proof.

It follows from
[
NT16
, Lemma 2.19]
that we can identify the underived functors
\Gamma(K_{\mathrm{G}},-)\circ\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},-)=\Gamma(K_{\mathrm{G}}^{\mathrm{dis}},-)\circ\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}^{\mathrm{dis}},-)\circ\pi^{*}_{\mathrm{dis}}.
From this, we deduce that there is a natural transformation

R\Gamma(K_{\mathrm{G}},-)\circ R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},-)\to R\Gamma(K_{\mathrm{G}}^{\mathrm{dis}},-)\circ R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}^{\mathrm{dis}},-)\circ\pi^{*}_{\mathrm{dis}}.

We can check that this is an isomorphism after composing with the forgetful map to
\mathrm{D}^{+}(R)
, and this follows from comparing Proposition
2.1.3
and
[
NT16
, Proposition 2.18]
. ∎

We now recall an important finiteness result:

Lemma 2.1.6
.

Let
K_{\mathrm{G}}
be a good subgroup, and let
K^{\prime}_{\mathrm{G}}\subset K_{\mathrm{G}}

be a normal subgroup which is also good.
Let
R
be a Noetherian ring, and let
{\mathcal{V}}
be a smooth

R[K_{\mathrm{G}}]
-module, finite free as
R
-module.
Then
R\Gamma_{(c)}(X^{\mathrm{G}}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}^{\prime}},{\mathcal{V}})
are
perfect objects of

\mathrm{D}^{+}(K_{\mathrm{G}}/K_{\mathrm{G}}^{\prime},R)
; in other words, they
are isomorphic in this category to bounded complexes of projective

R[K_{\mathrm{G}}/K_{\mathrm{G}}^{\prime}]
-modules.

Proof.

The case of usual cohomology is essentially
[
ACC+18
, Lemma 2.1.7]
:
we choose a finite triangulation of
\overline{X}^{\mathrm{G}}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}}
and pull this back
to a
K_{\mathrm{G}}
-invariant triangulation of

\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}
, then consider the corresponding complex
of simplicial chains
C_{\bullet}
. We notice that

\Hom_{{\mathbb{Z}}[K^{\prime}_{\mathrm{G}}]}\left(C_{\bullet},{\mathcal{V}}\right)
is isomorphic
in
\mathrm{D}^{+}(K_{\mathrm{G}}/K_{\mathrm{G}}^{\prime},R)
to

R\Gamma(\overline{X}^{\mathrm{G}}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}^{\prime}},{\mathcal{V}})
.
The case of cohomology with compact support can be
done in a similar way, by choosing our triangulation of
\overline{X}^{\mathrm{G}}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}}

in such a way that a triangulation of
\partial X^{\mathrm{G}}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}}
is a
simplicial subcomplex. Pulling back to
\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}
we
obtain simplicial complexes
\partial C_{\bullet}\to C_{\bullet}
. Letting

C^{\mathrm{BM}}_{\bullet}
denote the cone of this map, we observe that

\Hom_{{\mathbb{Z}}[K^{\prime}_{\mathrm{G}}]}\left(C^{\mathrm{BM}}_{\bullet},{\mathcal{V}}\right)
is isomorphic
in
\mathrm{D}^{+}(K_{\mathrm{G}}/K_{\mathrm{G}}^{\prime},R)
to

R\Gamma_{c}(X^{\mathrm{G}}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}^{\prime}},{\mathcal{V}})
.
∎

Assume now that
R={\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}
for some
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
.
If
S\subseteq S_{p}(F)
is a set of places of
F
above
p
, and if

{\mathcal{V}}
is a smooth
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}[K_{S_{p}\setminus S}]
-module, we define the

completed cohomology
at
S
of level
K^{S}_{\mathrm{G}}

to be

R\Gamma(K^{S}_{\mathrm{G}},R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},{\mathcal{V}}))\in\mathrm{D}^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{G}_{S},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}).

Similarly, we define the
completed cohomology with compact support
at
S

of level
K^{S}_{\mathrm{G}}
to be

R\Gamma(K^{S}_{\mathrm{G}},R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},j_{!}{\mathcal{V}}))\in\mathrm{D}^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{G}_{S},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}).

For a finite set of finite places
T\supseteq S_{p}(F)
of
F
,
a variant of the above formalism equips these objects with
actions of
{\mathcal{H}}(\mathrm{G}^{T},K^{T}_{\mathrm{G}})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}
.
The same formalism applies to

R\Gamma(K^{S}_{\mathrm{G}},R\Gamma(\partial\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{G}},{\mathcal{V}}))\in\mathrm{D}^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{G}_{S},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}).

The following lemma offers a justification for the term
completed cohomology
.

Lemma 2.1.7
.

For any
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
, we
have
{\mathcal{H}}(\mathrm{G}^{T},K^{T}_{\mathrm{G}})
-equivariant isomorphisms
of admissible smooth
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}[\mathrm{G}_{S}]
-modules

(2.1.1)

H^{i}\left(R\Gamma(K^{S}_{\mathrm{G}},R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},{\mathcal{V}}))\right)\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}\varinjlim_{K_{\mathrm{G},S}}H^{i}\left(X^{\mathrm{G}}_{K^{S}_{\mathrm{G}}K_{\mathrm{G},S}},{\mathcal{V}}\right)

and

(2.1.2)

H^{i}\left(R\Gamma(K^{S}_{\mathrm{G}},R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},j_{!}{\mathcal{V}}))\right)\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}\varinjlim_{K_{\mathrm{G},S}}H^{i}_{c}\left(X^{\mathrm{G}}_{K^{S}_{\mathrm{G}}K_{\mathrm{G},S}},{\mathcal{V}}\right).

Proof.

In the category
of compact Hausdorff spaces, we have

\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}/K^{S}_{\mathrm{G}}=\varprojlim_{K_{\mathrm{G},S}}\overline{X}^{\mathrm{G}}_{K^{S}_{\mathrm{G}}K_{\mathrm{G},S}}.

This shows that
K^{S}_{\mathrm{G}}
acts freely on
\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}
,
so we can functorially rewrite the LHS of (
2.1.1
) and (
2.1.2
)
in terms of the cohomology
of either
{\mathcal{V}}
or
j_{!}{\mathcal{V}}
on the quotient
\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}/K^{S}_{\mathrm{G}}
.
We can functorially rewrite the terms on the RHS in terms of the cohomology
of either
{\mathcal{V}}
or
j_{!}{\mathcal{V}}
on
\overline{X}^{\mathrm{G}}_{K^{S}_{\mathrm{G}}K_{\mathrm{G},S}}
.
The result now follows from
[
NT16
, Lemma 2.34]
. Finally, admissibility
of the cohomology groups follows from Lemma
2.1.6
.
∎

Our next result will imply an important property of completed
cohomology: it is, in some sense, independent of the weight

{\mathcal{V}}
. It will be useful to work in a little more generality, so we
assume that
{\mathcal{V}}
is a smooth
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}[K_{\mathrm{G},S_{p}\backslash S}\times\Delta_{S}]
-module,
flat over
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}
, for an open submonoid
\Delta_{S}\subset\mathrm{G}_{S}

which contains an open subgroup
U_{S}
of
\mathrm{G}_{S}
.
As above, we associate to
{\mathcal{V}}
a
G^{T}\times K_{\mathrm{G},T\backslash S}\times U_{S}
-equivariant sheaf on
\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}
by pulling back from a point.

Lemma 2.1.8
.

We have canonical isomorphisms

R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\otimes{\mathcal{V}}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},{\mathcal{V}})

and

R\Gamma(\partial\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\otimes{\mathcal{V}}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}R\Gamma(\partial\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},{\mathcal{V}})

in
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(G^{T}\times K_{\mathrm{G},T\backslash S}\times U_{S},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
.

Proof.

We explain the case of
\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}
,
the case of
\partial\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}
is the same. Let

f:\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}\to*
be the
\mathrm{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,f})
-equivariant
projection to a point. We set
H:=G^{T}\times K_{\mathrm{G},T\backslash S}\times U_{S}
. There is a pair of adjoint functors
(f^{*},Rf_{*})
between

D^{+}(\mathrm{Sh}_{H}(*),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\simeq D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(H,{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})

and
D^{+}(\mathrm{Sh}_{H}(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
.
There is also a natural isomorphism

f^{*}(Rf_{*}({\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\otimes{\mathcal{V}})\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}f^{*}Rf_{*}({\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\otimes f^{*}{\mathcal{V}}

and hence by adjunction a natural map

f^{*}(Rf_{*}({\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\otimes{\mathcal{V}})\to f^{*}{\mathcal{V}}.

We therefore have a morphism

(2.1.3)

R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\otimes{\mathcal{V}}=Rf_{*}({\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\otimes{\mathcal{V}}\longrightarrow Rf_{*}f^{*}{\mathcal{V}}=R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},{\mathcal{V}})

in
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(H,{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
. It is enough to show that this
is an isomorphism after forgetting the equivariant structure. By
[
Sch98
, §1, Corollary 3]
,
if we forget the equivariant structure for the sheaves on
\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}
,
the resulting derived functors compute cohomology with compact support.
Since
\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}
is a compact Hausdorff space,
we can apply
[
KS94
, Prop. 2.6.6]
, which implies
that the morphism in (
2.1.3
) is an isomorphism (since
{\mathcal{V}}
is flat over
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}
, the assumption in
loc. cit.
that the coefficient ring has finite weak global dimension is not necessary).
∎

We can use this lemma to
define
an object

R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},{\mathcal{V}}):=R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\otimes{\mathcal{V}}\in D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(G^{T}\times K_{\mathrm{G},T\backslash S}\times\Delta_{S},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}).

It is independent of the choice of
U_{S}
, by
[
Sch98
, §1, Corollary 3]
. When
K_{\mathrm{G},S}
is a compact open subgroup of
\Delta_{S}
, we obtain
R\Gamma(K_{\mathrm{G}},R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},{\mathcal{V}}))\in D^{+}({\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
with an action of
{\mathcal{H}}(\mathrm{G}^{T},K_{\mathrm{G}}^{T})\otimes{\mathcal{H}}(\Delta_{S},K_{\mathrm{G},S})
.

In §
4.1
, we will need a variant of this lemma with a coefficient system in a derived category. When we apply this lemma, we will just have group actions, not monoids, so we now assume
{\mathcal{V}}\in D^{b}_{\mathrm{sm}}(K_{\mathrm{G},S_{p}\backslash S},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
. After inflation and pullback from a point, we get a corresponding object
{\mathcal{V}}\in D^{b}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{G}^{T}\times K_{\mathrm{G},T}}(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}))
. To state the lemma, we need the derived tensor product functor
R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}-
. Although
\Mod_{\mathrm{sm}}(G^{T}\times K_{\mathrm{G},T},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
does not have enough projectives, every object has a surjection from a
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}
-flat object. Indeed, smoothness implies that there is a surjection from a direct sum of copies of compact inductions of trivial representations of compact open subgroups on
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}
6
6

6

This is the usual proof of ‘enough projectives’ over a characteristic
0
field. The problem here is that the trivial representation with coefficients in
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}
of a non-trivial compact
p
-adic group is not projective.
. This gives enough acyclic objects to compute derived tensor products on
D^{-}_{\mathrm{sm}}(G^{T}\times K_{\mathrm{G},T},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
. Since the functor
R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},-)
on
D^{+}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{G}^{T}\times K_{\mathrm{G},T}}(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}))
has bounded cohomological dimension (Lemma
2.1.4
), it takes bounded objects to bounded objects.

Lemma 2.1.9
.

Let
{\mathcal{V}}\in D^{b}_{\mathrm{sm}}(K_{\mathrm{G},S_{p}\backslash S},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
. We have a canonical isomorphism

R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathcal{V}}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},{\mathcal{V}})

in
D^{b}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{G}^{T}\times K_{\mathrm{G},T},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
.

Proof.

As in the proof of Lemma
2.1.8
, we set
H=\mathrm{G}^{T}\times K_{\mathrm{G},T}
and consider
f:\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}}\to\ast
the map to the point. By Lemma
2.1.4
, we have a pair of adjoint functors
(f^{*},Rf_{*})
between unbounded derived categories
D_{\mathrm{sm}}(H,{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
and
D(\mathrm{Sh}_{H}(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}))
. Since
f^{*}
is exact, it is easy to see that we have a natural isomorphism

f^{*}(Rf_{*}({\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathcal{V}})\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}f^{*}Rf_{*}({\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}f^{*}{\mathcal{V}}

and we then obtain a map

p_{\mathcal{V}}:R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathcal{V}}\to R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},{\mathcal{V}})

by adjunction. The fact that this is an isomorphism follows from the case where
{\mathcal{V}}
is a
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}
-flat module. More precisely, we can replace
{\mathcal{V}}
by a bounded above complex
{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}
of
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}
-flat objects in
\Mod_{\mathrm{sm}}(H,{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
, and replace
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}
by a bounded complex
{\mathcal{I}}^{\bullet}
of
Rf_{*}
-acyclic objects in
\mathrm{Sh}_{H}(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
. Using
[
KS94
, Prop. 2.6.6]
again, we see that each sheaf
{\mathcal{I}}^{i}\otimes{\mathcal{F}}^{j}
is
Rf_{*}
-acyclic and the natural map

f_{*}{\mathcal{I}}^{i}\otimes{\mathcal{F}}^{j}\to f_{*}({\mathcal{I}}^{i}\otimes{\mathcal{F}}^{j})

is an isomorphism. The total complexes of the double complexes
f_{*}{\mathcal{I}}^{\bullet}\otimes{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}\to f_{*}({\mathcal{I}}^{\bullet}\otimes{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet})
respectively compute the source and target of
p_{\mathcal{V}}
, so we see that
p_{\mathcal{V}}
is an isomorphism in
D^{b}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{G}^{T}\times K_{\mathrm{G},T},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
.
∎

Remark 2.1.10
.

With Lemma
2.1.9
in hand, we can prove that
R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
has bounded Tor-dimension, and then extend our projection formula to handle
{\mathcal{V}}
in the unbounded derived category. See for example
[
Fu15
, Corollary 6.5.6]
for the classical projection formula in
l
-adic cohomology.

Assume now that
R={\mathcal{O}}
, and that
{\mathcal{V}}
is an

{\mathcal{O}}[K_{\mathrm{G},S}]
-module, which is finite free as an
{\mathcal{O}}
-module and such that
{\mathcal{V}}/\varpi^{m}

is a smooth
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}[K_{\mathrm{G},S}]
-module for each
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
.
We then consider

R\Gamma(\overline{X}^{\mathrm{G}}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}},{\mathcal{V}}):=\varprojlim_{m}R\Gamma(\overline{X}^{\mathrm{G}}_{K_{\mathrm{G}}},{\mathcal{V}}/\varpi^{m})

in
\mathrm{D}^{+}({\mathcal{O}})
,
where the projective limit should be understood as a homotopy limit.
We also consider the analogue with coefficient system
j_{!}{\mathcal{V}}
.
These limits can be endowed with an action of the Hecke algebra

{\mathcal{H}}(\mathrm{G}^{S},K_{\mathrm{G}}^{S})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathcal{O}}
7
7

7

As
the proof of Lemma
2.1.6
shows, we
have explicit perfect complexes that compute these derived functors, so
we could simply take a projective limit on the level of complexes. To
endow the projective limit with a Hecke action, we can instead
consider adelic complexes that compute these derived
functors as in
[
CGJ23
, §5.1]
. Combining the fact that derived limits commute with cohomology
[
Sta13
,
Tag 08U1
]
and Lemma
2.1.5
we can show that we will obtain the same Hecke actions as in
[
NT16
]
.
.

Continue to assume
R={\mathcal{O}}
. If
S\subseteq S_{p}(F)
is a set of places of
F
above
p
, let

{\mathcal{V}}
be an
{\mathcal{O}}[K_{S_{p}\setminus S}]
-module which is finite
free as an
{\mathcal{O}}
-module and such that
{\mathcal{V}}/\varpi^{m}
is a smooth

{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}[K_{S_{p}\setminus S}]
-module. We consider

R\Gamma(K^{S}_{\mathrm{G}},R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},{\mathcal{V}})):=\varprojlim_{m}R\Gamma(K^{S}_{\mathrm{G}},R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{G}},{\mathcal{V}}/\varpi^{m}))

in
\mathrm{D}^{+}(\mathrm{G}_{S},{\mathcal{O}})
, where again the projective
limit should be understood as a homotopy limit. There is also
the analogue with coefficient system
j_{!}{\mathcal{V}}
. When
T\supseteq S_{p}(F)

is a finite set of finite places of
F
, these limits can also
be endowed with an action of the Hecke algebra

{\mathcal{H}}(\mathrm{G}^{T},K_{\mathrm{G}}^{T})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathcal{O}}
.

We now assume that
\mathrm{G}
is reductive, and let

\mathrm{P}=\mathrm{M}\mathrm{N}
be a parabolic subgroup with
Levi subgroup
\mathrm{M}
. Let
K_{\mathrm{G}}\subset\mathrm{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,f})
be
a good subgroup. In this situation, we define
K_{\mathrm{P}}=K_{\mathrm{G}}\cap\mathrm{P}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,f})
,

K_{\mathrm{N}}=K_{\mathrm{G}}\cap\mathrm{N}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,f})
, and define
K_{\mathrm{M}}

to be the image of
K_{\mathrm{P}}
in
\mathrm{M}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,f})
.
We say that
K_{\mathrm{G}}
is
decomposed
with respect to
\mathrm{P}=\mathrm{M}\mathrm{N}
if we have
K_{\mathrm{P}}=K_{\mathrm{M}}\ltimes K_{\mathrm{N}}
;
equivalently, if
K_{\mathrm{M}}=K_{\mathrm{G}}\cap\mathrm{M}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,f})
.

Assume now that
K_{\mathrm{G}}
is decomposed with respect to
\mathrm{P}=\mathrm{M}\mathrm{N}
, and let
S
be a finite set of finite places of

F
such that for all
v\not\in S
,
K_{\mathrm{G},v}
is a hyperspecial
maximal compact subgroup of
\mathrm{G}(F_{v})
. In this case,
we can define homomorphisms

r_{\mathrm{P}}:{\mathcal{H}}(\mathrm{G}^{S},K_{\mathrm{G}}^{S})\to{\mathcal{H}}(\mathrm{P}^{S},K_{\mathrm{P}}^{S})\text{ and }r_{\mathrm{M}}:{\mathcal{H}}(\mathrm{P}^{S},K_{\mathrm{P}}^{S})\to{\mathcal{H}}(\mathrm{M}^{S},K_{\mathrm{M}}^{S}),

given respectively by “restriction to
\mathrm{P}
” and
“integration along
\mathrm{N}
”; see
[
NT16
, §2.2.3]

and
[
NT16
, §2.2.4]
respectively
for the definitions of these maps, along with the proofs
that they are indeed algebra homomorphisms,
and that
r_{\mathrm{M}}
preserves integrality. We
use
{\mathcal{S}}:=r_{\mathrm{M}}\circ r_{\mathrm{P}}
to denote the
unnormalised Satake transform.

Finally, we remark that the above formalism also
applies to the case of the Hecke algebra of a monoid,
see
[
ACC+18
, §2.1.8]
.

2.1.11.
The general linear group and the quasi-split unitary group

From now on, we fix an integer
n\geq 2
and we let

F
be an imaginary CM field containing
the maximal totally real subfield
F^{+}
. Let
c\in\Gal(F/F^{+})
denote complex conjugation.
We set
\overline{S}_{p}:=S_{p}(F^{+})
and
S_{p}:=S_{p}(F)
.
We let
\Psi_{n}
be the matrix with

1
’s on the anti-diagonal and
0
’s elsewhere, and we let

J_{n}=\left(\begin{matrix}0&\Psi_{n}\\
-\Psi_{n}&0\end{matrix}\right).

We let
\widetilde{G}/{\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}}
be the group scheme
defined by

\widetilde{G}(R)=\{g\in\GL_{2n}(R\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}}}{\mathcal{O}}_{F})\mid\ ^{t}gJ_{n}g^{c}=J_{n}\}

for any
{\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}}
-algebra
R
. The generic fibre of

\widetilde{G}
over
F^{+}
is a quasi-split unitary group,
which becomes isomorphic to
\GL_{2n}/F
after base change
from
F^{+}
to
F
. In particular, if
\bar{v}
is a place of

F^{+}
that splits in
F
, a choice of place
v\mid\bar{v}

of
F
determines a canonical isomorphism
\iota_{v}:G(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}\GL_{2n}(F_{v})
.

We let
P\subset\widetilde{G}
denote the Siegel parabolic
consisting of block upper-triangular matrices with blocks
of size
n\times n
. We let
P=U\rtimes G
be a Levi
decomposition such that we can identify

G
with
\mathrm{Res}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F}/{\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}}}\GL_{n}
8
8

8

We use the same identification
as in
[
ACC+18
, §2.2.1]
, namely
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&0\\
0&D\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in G(R)\mapsto D\in\GL_{n}(R\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}}}{\mathcal{O}}_{F})

for an
{\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}}
-algebra
R
.
.
To simplify the notation, from now on we write

\widetilde{X}
for
X^{\widetilde{G}}
and

X
for
X^{G}
. We also write
\widetilde{K}

and
K
for good subgroups of
\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})

and of
G({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})=\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,f})
. Note that
the locally symmetric spaces
\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}}
are complex
manifolds of (complex) dimension
d:=n^{2}[F^{+}:{\mathbb{Q}}]
, whereas
the locally symmetric spaces
X_{K}
are real manifolds
of (real) dimension
d-1
.

We now describe some explicit (integral and rational)
coefficient systems for these symmetric spaces. These
will depend on a choice of a prime
p
and on a choice
of a dominant weight for either
G
or
\widetilde{G}
. We fix a coefficient
field
E/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
which is assumed to be sufficiently large,
so that it contains the image of every embedding

\Hom(F,\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p})
. Let
T\subset\widetilde{B}\subset\widetilde{G}

be the maximal torus of diagonal matrices and the upper triangular Borel
subgroup, respectively. Set
B:=\widetilde{B}\cap G
, this can be identified
with the upper triangular Borel subgroup in
G
.

We first treat the case of
G
. We identify the character group of

(\mathrm{Res}_{F^{+}/{\mathbb{Q}}}T)_{E}
with
({\mathbb{Z}}^{n})^{\Hom(F,E)}

in the usual way. A weight
(\lambda_{\tau,i})\in({\mathbb{Z}}^{n})^{\Hom(F,E)}

with
\tau\in\Hom(F,E)
and
i\in 1,\dots,n

is dominant for
(\mathrm{Res}_{F^{+}/{\mathbb{Q}}}B)_{E}
if it satisfies

\lambda_{\tau,1}\geq\lambda_{\tau,2}\geq\dots\geq\lambda_{\tau,n}

for each
\tau\in\Hom(F,E)
. We denote by
({\mathbb{Z}}^{n}_{+})^{\Hom(F,E)}
the
subset of dominant weights. The expression ‘
{\lambda}
is a dominant weight for
G
’ will indicate that a weight
{\lambda}\in X^{\ast}((\mathrm{Res}_{F^{+}/{\mathbb{Q}}}T)_{E})
is dominant for
(\mathrm{Res}_{F^{+}/{\mathbb{Q}}}B)_{E}
.

Assume now that
\lambda\in({\mathbb{Z}}^{n}_{+})^{\Hom(F,E)}
.
We define the
G({\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+},p})=\prod_{{v}\in{S}_{p}}G({\mathcal{O}}_{F_{v}})
-representation
{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}

to be the integral dual Weyl module of highest weight
\lambda
with coefficients in
{\mathcal{O}}
,
obtained from the Borel–Weil construction.
More precisely, if
we let
\mathrm{B}_{n}\subset\GL_{n}
denote the standard Borel
consisting of upper-triangular matrices and
w_{0,n}
denote
the longest element in the Weyl group of
\GL_{n}
, we consider
the algebraic induction

(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathrm{B}_{n}}^{\GL_{n}}w_{0,n}\lambda_{\tau})_{/{\mathcal{O}}}:=\{f\in{\mathcal{O}}[\GL_{n}]\mid f(bg)=(w_{0,n}\lambda_{\tau})(b)f(g),

\forall\ {\mathcal{O}}\to R,b\in\mathrm{B}_{n}(R),g\in\GL_{n}(R)\},

and we set
{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\tau}}
to be the finite free

{\mathcal{O}}
-module obtained by evaluating this on
{\mathcal{O}}
and

V_{\lambda_{\tau}}:={\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\tau}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}}E
. When
\tau
induces the place
v
of
F
, these modules come with an action of
\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{F_{v}})
and
\GL_{n}(F_{v})
respectively.

Finally, we set
{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}:=\otimes_{\tau,{\mathcal{O}}}{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\tau}}

and
V_{\lambda}:={\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}}E
. Then

V_{\lambda}
is the absolutely irreducible algebraic representation of
(\mathrm{Res}_{F^{+}/{\mathbb{Q}}}G)_{E}

of highest weight
\lambda
and it is finite-dimensional over
E
; the
lattice
{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}\subset V_{\lambda}
is
G({\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+},p})
-stable.
For every
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
,
{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}/\varpi^{m}
,
is a smooth
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}[G({\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+},p})]
-module
that is finite free as an
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}
-module. Therefore,
the formalism of the previous section applies to
{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}
.

We now treat the case of
\widetilde{G}
. Assume that each
place in
\overline{S}_{p}
splits from
F^{+}
to
F
and that
we have a partition of the form

S_{p}=\widetilde{S}_{p}\sqcup\widetilde{S}^{c}_{p}
, with
\tilde{v}\in\widetilde{S}_{p}

the place lying above a place
\bar{v}\in\overline{S}_{p}
. This induces
a partition on
\Hom(F,E)
, by choosing the embedding
\tilde{\tau}:F\hookrightarrow E

above a given embedding
\tau:F^{+}\hookrightarrow E
that induces a place
in
\widetilde{S}_{p}
. In turn, this induces an identification

(\mathrm{Res}_{F^{+}/{\mathbb{Q}}}\widetilde{G})_{E}=\prod_{\Hom(F^{+},E)}\GL_{2n,E}

and therefore an identification of the character group
of
(\mathrm{Res}_{F^{+}/{\mathbb{Q}}}T)_{E}
with
({\mathbb{Z}}^{2n})^{\Hom(F^{+},E)}
.
More precisely, this identifies a weight
\lambda=(\lambda_{\tilde{\tau},i})

with a weight
\tilde{\lambda}=(\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau,i})
where

(2.1.4)

\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}=(-\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}c,n},\dots,,-\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}c,1},\lambda_{\tilde{\tau},1},\dots,\lambda_{\tilde{\tau},n}).

The set of weights that are dominant for
(\mathrm{Res}_{F^{+}/{\mathbb{Q}}}\widetilde{B})_{E}

are the ones in the subset
({\mathbb{Z}}^{2n}_{+})^{\Hom(F^{+},E)}
. For such weights,
we can therefore define the integral dual Weyl module of highest weight
\tilde{\lambda}
,
{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}\subset V_{\tilde{\lambda}}
, a
\widetilde{G}({\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+},p})
-stable
{\mathcal{O}}
-lattice in the highest weight
\tilde{\lambda}
representation of
(\mathrm{Res}_{F^{+}/{\mathbb{Q}}}\widetilde{G})_{E}
.
For every
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
,
{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m}
,
is a smooth
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}[\prod_{\bar{v}\in\overline{S}_{p}}\widetilde{G}({\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}_{\bar{v}}})]
-module
that is finite free as an
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}
-module. Therefore,
the formalism of the previous section also
applies to
{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}
. We say ‘
\tilde{\lambda}
is a dominant weight for
\widetilde{G}
’ to indicate that a weight
\tilde{\lambda}\in X^{\ast}((\mathrm{Res}_{F^{+}/{\mathbb{Q}}}T)_{E})
is dominant for
(\mathrm{Res}_{F^{+}/{\mathbb{Q}}}\widetilde{B})_{E}
.

We now define appropriate quotients of the Hecke algebras
acting on the cohomology groups with these coefficient systems. Again,
we treat
G
first. Let
S\supseteq S_{p}
be a finite set of finite
places of
F
and let
K\subset\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,f})
be a good subgroup
such that
K_{v}=\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{F_{v}})
for
v\not\in S
and

K_{v}\subseteq\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{F_{v}})
for
v\in S_{p}
. For any

\lambda\in({\mathbb{Z}}^{n}_{+})^{\Hom(F,E)}
, the complex
R\Gamma(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda})

is well-defined as an object of
D^{+}({\mathcal{O}})
(up to unique isomorphism)
and equipped with a Hecke action. We set

\mathbb{T}^{S}:={\mathcal{H}}(G^{S},K^{S})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathcal{O}}

and

\mathbb{T}^{S}(K,\lambda):=\mathrm{Im}\left(\mathbb{T}^{S}\to\mathrm{End}_{D^{+}({\mathcal{O}})}(R\Gamma(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}))\right).

In the case of
\widetilde{G}
, let
S\supseteq S_{p}
be a finite set of
finite places of
F
satisfying
S=S^{c}
. Let
\overline{S}
denote
the set of finite places of
F^{+}
below
S
. Let
\widetilde{K}\subset\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})

be a good compact open subgroup such that
\widetilde{K}_{\bar{v}}=\widetilde{G}({\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}_{\bar{v}}})

for
\bar{v}\not\in\overline{S}
and
\widetilde{K}_{\bar{v}}\subseteq\widetilde{G}({\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}_{\bar{v}}})

for
\bar{v}\in\overline{S}_{p}
. To simplify notation, we write
\widetilde{G}^{S}=\widetilde{G}^{\overline{S}}
etc. For any
\tilde{\lambda}\in({\mathbb{Z}}^{2n}_{+})^{\Hom(F^{+},E)}
,
the complex
R\Gamma(X_{\widetilde{K}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}})

is well-defined as an object of
D^{+}({\mathcal{O}})
(up to unique isomorphism)
and equipped with a Hecke action. We consider the abstract Hecke
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra

\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{S}:={\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{G}^{S},\widetilde{K}^{S})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathcal{O}}
and
its quotient
\mathbb{T}^{S}(\widetilde{K},\tilde{\lambda})
acting faithfully on
R\Gamma(X_{\widetilde{K}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}})
.

As a consequence of Lemma
2.1.6
, we
see that both
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K,\lambda)
and
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{S}(\widetilde{K},\tilde{\lambda})

are finite
{\mathcal{O}}
-modules.
There are obvious versions of all of this with
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}
-coefficients
and for compactly supported cohomology and for the cohomology of
the boundary
\partial X_{\widetilde{K}}
of the Borel–Serre compactification
of
X_{\widetilde{K}}
.

We will make use of particular elements of some Weyl groups, besides the longest element
w_{0,n}
in the Weyl group of
\GL_{n}
which we have already mentioned. For
\mathrm{G}=G
or
\widetilde{G}
, we will write
w_{0}^{\mathrm{G}}
for the longest element in the Weyl group
W((\mathrm{Res}_{F^{+}/{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathrm{G})_{E},(\mathrm{Res}_{F^{+}/{\mathbb{Q}}}T)_{E})
. We set
w^{P}_{0}=w_{0}^{G}w_{0}^{\widetilde{G}}
. It is the longest element in the set
W^{P}
of minimal length coset representatives for

W((\mathrm{Res}_{F^{+}/{\mathbb{Q}}}\widetilde{G})_{E},(\mathrm{Res}_{F^{+}/{\mathbb{Q}}}T)_{E})/W((\mathrm{Res}_{F^{+}/{\mathbb{Q}}}G)_{E},(\mathrm{Res}_{F^{+}/{\mathbb{Q}}}T)_{E}).

In our development of
P
-ordinary Hida theory, it will be important to compare coefficient systems for
\widetilde{G}
and
G
.

Let
\mathrm{P}_{n,n}\subset\GL_{2n}
be the parabolic subgroup
of block-upper triangular matrices with Levi quotient
\GL_{n}\times\GL_{n}
.
By the transitivity of algebraic induction,
{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}}
and
V_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}}
can be identified
with the evaluation on
{\mathcal{O}}
and
E
respectively of the algebraic induction

\left(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathrm{P}_{n,n}}^{\GL_{2n}}{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{-w_{0,n}\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}c}}\right)_{/{\mathcal{O}}}.

Lemma 2.1.12
.

The natural
\mathrm{P}_{n,n}({\mathcal{O}})
-equivariant
morphism

{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}}\to{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{-w_{0,n}\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}c}}

given by evaluation of functions at the identity is surjective.

Proof.

By transitivity of parabolic induction, we can identify

{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}}
with the evaluation on
{\mathcal{O}}

of

\left(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathrm{B}_{2n}}^{\GL_{2n}}w_{0,2n}\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}\right)_{/{\mathcal{O}}}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}\left(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathrm{P}_{n,n}}^{\GL_{2n}}\circ\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathrm{B}_{n}\times B_{n}}^{\GL_{n}\times\GL_{n}}w_{0,2n}\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}\right)_{/{\mathcal{O}}},

where, by
[
Jan03
, §I.3.5]
,
the map is given by
f\mapsto\tilde{f}(g)(h)=f(hg)
for all
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebras
R
,

h\in\GL_{n}(R)\times\GL_{n}(R)
, and
g\in\GL_{2n}(R)
. By Nakayama,
it is enough to check surjectivity after base change to
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}
, in which
case the evaluation at identity map can be rewritten in geometric terms
as the restriction map

H^{0}(X,{\mathcal{L}})\to H^{0}(X^{\prime},{\mathcal{L}}),

where
X=\mathrm{B}_{2n}\backslash\GL_{2n}
is the full flag variety
for
\GL_{2n}
,
X^{\prime}\subset X
is the Schubert variety
for the longest Weyl group element in
\GL_{n}\times\GL_{n}
and

{\mathcal{L}}
is the line bundle on
X
determined by
w_{0,2n}\tilde{\lambda}
.
The result now follows from the main theorem of
[
And85
]

applied to
\mathrm{SL}_{2n}/\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}
.
∎

2.1.13.
Explicit Hecke operators

Fix once and for all a choice
\varpi_{\bar{v}}
of uniformiser of
F^{+}_{\bar{v}}
for every finite place
\bar{v}
of
F^{+}
. When
\bar{v}
is unramified in
F
we set
\varpi_{v}=\varpi_{\bar{v}}
for
v|\bar{v}
.

We define some explicit Hecke operators at unramified primes first. If
v
is a
finite place of
F
and
1\leq i\leq n
is an integer then
we write
T_{v,i}\in{\mathcal{H}}(\GL_{n}(F_{v}),\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{F_{v}}))

for the double coset operator

T_{v,i}=[\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{F_{v}})\mathrm{diag}(\varpi_{v},\dots,\varpi_{v},1,\dots,1)\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{F_{v}})],

where
\varpi_{v}
appears
i
times on the diagonal.
This is the same as the operator denoted by
T_{M,v,i}

in
[
NT16
, Prop.-Def. 5.3]
.
We define a polynomial

(2.1.5)

\begin{split}P_{v}(X)=X^{n}&-T_{v,1}X^{n-1}+\dots+(-1)^{i}q_{v}^{i(i-1)/2}T_{v,i}X^{n-i}+\dots\\
&+q_{v}^{n(n-1)/2}T_{v,n}\in{\mathcal{H}}(\GL_{n}(F_{v}),\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{F_{v}}))[X].\end{split}

It corresponds to the characteristic polynomial of a Frobenius
element on
\mathrm{rec}^{T}_{F_{v}}(\pi_{v})
, where
\pi_{v}
is an
unramified representation of
\GL_{n}(F_{v})
.

If
\overline{v}
is a place of
F^{+}
unramified in
F
, and

v
is a place of
F
above
\overline{v}
, and
1\leq i\leq 2n

is an integer, then we write
\widetilde{T}_{v,i}\in{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{G}(F^{+}_{\overline{v}}),\widetilde{G}({\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}_{\overline{v}}}))\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Z}}[q_{\overline{v}}^{-1}]
for the operator denoted

T_{G,v,i}
in
[
NT16
, Prop.-Def. 5.2]
. We define a polynomial

(2.1.6)

\begin{split}\widetilde{P}_{v}(X)=X^{2n}&-\widetilde{T}_{v,1}X^{2n-1}+\dots+(-1)^{j}q_{v}^{j(j-1)/2}\widetilde{T}_{v,j}+\dots\\
&+q_{v}^{n(2n-1)}\widetilde{T}_{v,2n}\in{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{G}(F^{+}_{\overline{v}}),\widetilde{G}({\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}_{\overline{v}}}))\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Z}}[q_{\overline{v}}^{-1}][X].\end{split}

It corresponds to the characteristic polynomial of a Frobenius element on

\mathrm{rec}^{T}_{F_{v}}(\pi_{v})
, where
\pi_{v}
is the base change of an
unramified representation
\sigma_{\overline{v}}
of
the group
\widetilde{G}(F^{+}_{\overline{v}})
.

We now describe the behaviour of these Hecke operators
under the unnormalised Satake transform with respect to the Siegel parabolic.
We use the following convention: if
f(X)
is a polynomial of degree
d
,
with constant term a unit
a_{0}
, we set
f^{\vee}(X):=a_{0}^{-1}X^{d}f(X^{-1})
.

Proposition 2.1.14
.

Let
v
be a place of
F
, unramified over the place
\overline{v}
of
F^{+}
. Let

{\mathcal{S}}:{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{G}(F^{+}_{\overline{v}}),\widetilde{G}({\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}_{\overline{v}}}))\to{\mathcal{H}}(G(F^{+}_{\overline{v}}),G({\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}_{\overline{v}}}))

denote the homomorphism defined at the end of §
2.1.1
.
Then we have

{\mathcal{S}}(\widetilde{P}_{v}(X))=P_{v}(X)q_{v}^{n(2n-1)}P_{v^{c}}^{\vee}(q_{v}^{1-2n}X).

Proof.

See
[
NT16
, §5.1]
.
∎

We now discuss some Hecke operators at (possibly ramified) places
in
\overline{S}_{p}
. Assume that each prime
\bar{v}
of
F^{+}
above
p
splits
in
F
.
Let
\bar{v}\in\overline{S}_{p}
, and recall that
\tilde{v}
is a chosen prime
of
F
above it. For integers
c\geq b\geq 0
, we define subgroups

{\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{v}}(b,c)\subset\widetilde{G}({\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}_{\bar{v}}})=\GL_{2n}({\mathcal{O}}_{F_{\tilde{v}}})

which reduce to
block upper-triangular matrices (with two
n\times n
blocks) modulo

\varpi_{\tilde{v}}^{c}
and to block unipotent matrices modulo
\varpi_{\tilde{v}}^{b}
.
We set
{\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{v}}={\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{v}}(0,1)
, which is identified with the standard parahoric subgroup
{\mathcal{P}}_{n,n}
of
\GL_{2n}
. For each parabolic subgroup
Q_{\bar{v}}
of
P_{\bar{v}}
which contains
\widetilde{B}_{\bar{v}}
we have an associated parahoric subgroup
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\subset{\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{v}}
. We note that these subgroups all admit an Iwahori decomposition
with respect to
P_{\bar{v}}
, and therefore the formalism of
[
ACC+18
, §2.1.9]
applies when we consider the Hecke algebras of monoids.

Write

\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},n}:=\mathrm{diag}(\varpi_{\tilde{v}},\dots,\varpi_{\tilde{v}},1,\dots,1)\in\GL_{2n}(F_{\tilde{v}})
,
where
\varpi_{\tilde{v}}
appears exactly
n
times on the diagonal.
If
c\geq 1
, we write
\widetilde{U}_{\tilde{v},n}\in{\mathcal{H}}\left(\widetilde{G}({\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}_{\bar{v}}}),{\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{v}}(b,c)\right)
for the
double coset operator

\widetilde{U}_{\tilde{v},n}=[{\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{v}}(b,c)\iota_{\tilde{v}}^{-1}\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},n}{\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{v}}(b,c)]
. Also write

\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},2n}:=\mathrm{diag}(\varpi_{\tilde{v}},\dots,\varpi_{\tilde{v}})\in\GL_{2n}(F_{\tilde{v}})

and denote by
\widetilde{U}_{\tilde{v},2n}\in{\mathcal{H}}\left(\widetilde{G}({\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}_{\bar{v}}}),{\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{v}}(b,c)\right)

the corresponding double coset operator. Note
that these depend on both the choice of uniformiser
\varpi_{\tilde{v}}
and
on the chosen level. We write
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{v}}\subset\widetilde{G}(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})

for the subset

\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{v}}:=\iota_{\tilde{v}}^{-1}\left(\sqcup_{\mu_{1}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{+}}\sqcup_{\mu_{2}\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathcal{P}}_{n,n}(\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},n})^{\mu_{1}}(\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},2n})^{\mu_{2}}{\mathcal{P}}_{n,n}\right),

which is independent of the choice of
\tilde{v}\mid\bar{v}
.

Considering cohomology at level
{\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{v}}
and the ordinary subspace for the Hecke operator
\widetilde{U}_{\tilde{v},n}
will be most important for us. However, we will work a little more generally to allow us to keep track of additional Hecke operators at
\bar{v}
and prove a local–global compatibility result for ordinary as well as crystalline representations.

So, more generally, we suppose we have a parabolic subgroup
\widetilde{B}_{\bar{v}}\subset Q_{\bar{v}}\subset P_{\bar{v}}
corresponding to a subset
I\subset\Delta
of the simple roots, and with Levi decomposition
Q_{\bar{v}}=M_{Q_{\bar{v}}}N_{Q_{\bar{v}}}
compatible with the decomposition
P_{\bar{v}}=G_{\bar{v}}U_{\bar{v}}
. We consider the monoid of cocharacters

X_{Q_{\bar{v}}}:=\{\nu\in X_{*}(Z(M_{Q_{\bar{v}}})):\langle\nu,\delta\rangle\geq 0\text{ for all }\delta\in\Delta-I\}.

In fact, this is simply the subset of
\widetilde{B}_{\bar{v}}
-dominant cocharacters in
X_{*}(Z(M_{Q_{\bar{v}}}))
. We then define a subset
\widetilde{\Delta}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}}\subset\widetilde{G}(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})
containing the parahoric subgroup
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\subset{\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{v}}
by

\widetilde{\Delta}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}}:=\coprod_{\nu\in X_{Q_{\bar{v}}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\nu(\varpi_{\bar{v}}){\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}.

We have
\widetilde{\Delta}^{{\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}}=\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{v}}
.

We set
\Delta^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}},+}_{\bar{v}}:=\widetilde{\Delta}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}}\cap G_{F^{+}_{\bar{v}}}
and
\Delta^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}}:=\Delta^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}},+}_{\bar{v}}[\iota_{\tilde{v}}^{-1}(\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},n}^{-1})]
(the submonoid of
G(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})
generated by
\Delta^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}},+}_{\bar{v}}
and its central element
\iota_{\tilde{v}}^{-1}(\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},n}^{-1})
).

Lemma 2.1.15
.

(1)

For
\nu\in X_{Q_{\bar{v}}}
, the element
\nu(\varpi_{\bar{v}})
is
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}
-positive
; i.e. we have

\displaystyle\nu(\varpi_{\bar{v}})\left(N_{Q_{\bar{v}}}\cap{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\right)\nu(\varpi_{\bar{v}})^{-1}

\displaystyle\subset N_{Q_{\bar{v}}}\cap{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}

\displaystyle\text{ and }\nu(\varpi_{\bar{v}})^{-1}\left(\overline{N}_{Q_{\bar{v}}}\cap{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\right)\nu(\varpi_{\bar{v}})

\displaystyle\subset\overline{N}_{Q_{\bar{v}}}\cap{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}.

(2)

\widetilde{\Delta}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}}
is a monoid under multiplication.

(3)

The map
[(M_{Q_{\bar{v}}}\cap{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}})\nu(\varpi_{\bar{v}})(M_{Q_{\bar{v}}}\cap{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}})]\mapsto[{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\nu(\varpi_{\bar{v}}){\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}]
defines a ring isomorphism of Hecke algebras

{\mathcal{H}}(M_{Q_{\bar{v}}}\cap\widetilde{\Delta}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}},M_{Q_{\bar{v}}}\cap{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}})\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{\Delta}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}},{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}})

which also factors through an isomorphism to
{\mathcal{H}}(\Delta^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}},+}_{\bar{v}},G(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})\cap{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}})
.

Proof.

The first part can be checked directly, or using root groups. The second part follows from the first, using the Iwahori decomposition of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}
. The third part is
[
BK98
, Corollary 6.12]
.
∎

Remark 2.1.16
.

Our monoids are usually strictly contained in those defined in
[
ACC+18
, §2.1.9]
. We only need to consider Hecke operators supported on double cosets of central elements in the Levi subgroup, which in particular implies (as shown in the preceding lemma) that the Hecke algebra
{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{\Delta}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}},{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}})
is commutative.

Fix
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}

and
\tau\in\Hom(F^{+}_{\bar{v}},E)
. Let
\tilde{\lambda}\in\left({\mathbb{Z}}^{2n}_{+}\right)^{\Hom(F^{+},E)}
.
We define a character
\tilde{\alpha}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}}:\widetilde{\Delta}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}}\to E^{\times}

by setting

\tilde{\alpha}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}}\left(\nu(\varpi_{\bar{v}})\right)=\tau(\varpi_{\bar{v}})^{\langle\nu,w_{0}^{\widetilde{G}}\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}\rangle}

and setting the character to be trivial on
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}
.

Lemma 2.1.17
.

Fix
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}

and
\tau\in\Hom(F^{+}_{\bar{v}},E)
.
Define an action of
{\mathcal{O}}[\widetilde{\Delta}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}}]
on

V_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}}
by

(2.1.7)

g\cdot_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}x:=\tilde{\alpha}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}}(g)^{-1}g\cdot x,

where
g\cdot x
is the usual action of
g\in\widetilde{\Delta}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}}\subset\widetilde{G}(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})

on
x\in V_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}}
. The lattice

{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}}
is
stable under the
\cdot_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}
-action of
{\mathcal{O}}[\widetilde{\Delta}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}}]
.

Proof.

This follows from the fact that the re-scaled action of
\widetilde{\Delta}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}}
stabilizes each weight space in
{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}}
, which has lowest weight
w_{0,2n}\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}
. Cf.
[
Ger19
, Definition 2.8]
.
∎

Suppose we have a subset
\bar{S}\subseteq\bar{S}_{p}
and standard parabolic subgroups
Q_{\bar{v}}\subset P_{\bar{v}}
for each
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}
. Then we set
\widetilde{\Delta}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}}}_{\bar{S}}:=\prod_{\bar{v}\in\bar{S}}\widetilde{\Delta}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}}
, with similar notation for
\Delta
. Let
\tilde{\lambda}\in\left({\mathbb{Z}}^{2n}_{+}\right)^{\Hom(F^{+},E)}
. If we omit the superscript
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}}
, we take
Q_{\bar{v}}=P_{\bar{v}}
for all
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}
.

As a consequence of Lemma
2.1.17
, we have constructed a twisted action of
{\mathcal{O}}[\widetilde{\Delta}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}}}_{\bar{S}}]
on
V_{\tilde{\lambda}}
which stabilizes the lattice
{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}
. This action is obtained from the usual action by rescaling with the inverse of the character

\tilde{\alpha}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}:=\prod_{\bar{v}\in\bar{S}}\prod_{\tau\in\Hom(F^{+}_{\bar{v}},E)}\tilde{\alpha}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}}.

We construct a similar rescaled action for the Levi subgroup
G
. Suppose
\lambda\in\left({\mathbb{Z}}^{n}\right)^{\Hom(F,E)}
is a dominant weight for
G
. Recall that we have identified
\lambda
with a (not necessarily dominant) weight
\tilde{\lambda}
of
\widetilde{G}
. We define a character
\alpha_{\lambda}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}:\Delta^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}}\to E^{\times}
using the formula

\alpha_{\lambda}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}(\nu(\varpi_{\bar{v}}))=\prod_{\tau\in\Hom(F^{+}_{\bar{v}},E)}\tau(\varpi_{\bar{v}})^{\langle\nu,w_{0}^{G}\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}\rangle}

and a rescaled action of
\Delta^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}
on
{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}
by
g\cdot_{\lambda}x=\alpha_{\lambda}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}(x)^{-1}g\cdot x
. Note that the rescaling means that
\iota^{-1}_{\tilde{v}}(\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},n})\cdot_{\lambda}x=x
.

Let
T\supseteq S_{p}
be a finite set of finite places of
F
that
satisfies
T=T^{c}
. The formalism
of
[
ACC+18
, §2.1.8]
implies then that, for each good subgroup

\widetilde{K}\subset\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})
such that
\widetilde{K}_{\bar{v}}={\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{v}}(b,c)
with
c\geq 1

for each
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}\subseteq\bar{S}_{p}
, there is a canonical homomorphism

(2.1.8)

{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{G}^{T},\widetilde{K}^{T})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{S}},\widetilde{K}_{\bar{S}})\to\mathrm{End}_{D^{+}({\mathcal{O}})}\left(R\Gamma\left(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}\right)\right)

and in particular all the Hecke operators
\widetilde{U}_{\tilde{v},n}

and
\widetilde{U}_{\tilde{v},2n}
for
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}

act as endomorphisms of
R\Gamma\left(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}\right)
.
In fact, the Hecke operators
\widetilde{U}_{\tilde{v},2n}
for
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}

act as automorphisms of
R\Gamma\left(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}\right)

because the elements
\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},2n}
are central.

We will also consider
\widetilde{K}
with
\widetilde{K}_{\bar{v}}={\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}
for each
\bar{v}\in\overline{S}
, and then we have a Hecke action

(2.1.9)

{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{G}^{T},\widetilde{K}^{T})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{\Delta}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}}}_{\bar{S}},\widetilde{K}_{\bar{S}})\to\mathrm{End}_{D^{+}({\mathcal{O}})}\left(R\Gamma\left(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}\right)\right).

Similarly, for the Levi subgroup
G
, let
K\subset G({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})
be a good subgroup with
K_{\bar{v}}={\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\cap G(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})
for
\bar{v}\in S
.
Let
\{\lambda_{\tau}\}_{\tau\in\Hom(F^{+}_{\bar{v}},E)}
be sets of dominant weights for
G
at primes
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}
, giving rise to an

{\mathcal{O}}[K_{\bar{S}}]
-module
{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}}}
. Let
{\mathcal{V}}
be an
{\mathcal{O}}[K_{\bar{S}_{p}\setminus\bar{S}}]
-module,
which is finite free as an
{\mathcal{O}}
-module and such that
{\mathcal{V}}/\varpi^{m}
is a smooth
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}[K_{\bar{S}_{p}\setminus\bar{S}}]
-module
for every
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
.
We get a Hecke action

(2.1.10)

{\mathcal{H}}(G^{T},K^{T})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathcal{H}}({\Delta}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}}}_{\bar{S}},K_{\bar{S}})\to\mathrm{End}_{D^{+}({\mathcal{O}})}\left(R\Gamma\left(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}}{\mathcal{V}}_{{\lambda_{\bar{S}}}}\right)\right).

This Hecke action generalizes in the natural way
to the case when
{\mathcal{V}}
is a complex of
{\mathcal{O}}[K_{\bar{S}_{p}\setminus\bar{S}}]
-modules as above.
For
v|\bar{v}
we will be interested in the (invertible) Hecke operator
U_{v}
corresponding to the central element
\iota_{v}^{-1}(\tilde{u}_{{v},n}^{-1}\tilde{u}_{v,2n})
. Under our identification of
G
with
\mathrm{Res}_{F/F^{+}}\GL_{n}
, this element is
\mathrm{diag}(\varpi_{v},\ldots,\varpi_{v})
.

2.1.18.
Automorphic Galois representations and
middle degree cohomology

We start by recalling
some well-known results about Galois representations
associated to automorphic representations, and more generally
to systems of Hecke eigenvalues occurring in the cohomology
of locally symmetric spaces with integral coefficients.

Theorem 2.1.19
.

Assume
that
F
contains an imaginary quadratic field and that
\pi
is
a cuspidal automorphic representation of
\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+}})
that
is
\xi
-cohomological
9
9

9

As in
[
Shi14
]
,
\xi
-cohomological means that
\pi_{\infty}\otimes\xi
has non-zero
(\mathfrak{g},K_{\infty})
-cohomology.
for some irreducible algebraic representation

\xi
of
(\mathrm{Res}_{F^{+}/{\mathbb{Q}}}\widetilde{G})_{{\mathbb{C}}}
. For any isomorphism
\iota:\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}{\mathbb{C}}
,
there exists a continuous, semisimple Galois representation

r_{\iota}(\pi):G_{F}\to\GL_{2n}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p})

satisfying the following conditions:

(1)

For each prime
\ell\not=p
which is unramified in
F

and above which
\pi
is unramified, and for each prime
v\mid\ell
of
F
,

r_{\iota}(\pi)|_{G_{F_{v}}}
is unramified and the characteristic
polynomial of
r_{\iota}(\pi)(\mathrm{Frob}_{v})
is equal to the image of
\widetilde{P}_{v}(X)

in
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}[X]
corresponding to the base change of
\iota^{-1}(\pi_{v})
.

(2)

For each prime
v\mid p
of
F
,
r_{\iota}(\pi)
is de Rham, and for each

\tau:F\hookrightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
, we have

\mathrm{HT}_{\tau}(r_{\iota}(\pi))=\{\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau,1}+2n-1,\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau,2}+2n-2,\dots,\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau,2n}\},

where
\tilde{\lambda}\in({\mathbb{Z}}^{2n}_{+})^{\Hom(F,\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p})}
is the highest weight
of the representation
\iota^{-1}(\xi\otimes\xi)^{\vee}
of
(\mathrm{Res}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}\GL_{2n})_{\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}}
.
10
10

10

For each
\overline{\tau}:F^{+}\to{\mathbb{C}}
,
\xi
gives a representation of
\widetilde{G}_{\overline{\tau}}
and hence for
\tau,\tau c
extending
\overline{\tau}
to
F
we have a represententation
\xi\otimes\xi
of
\widetilde{G}_{\overline{\tau}}\times\widetilde{G}_{\overline{\tau}}=(\GL_{2n,F})_{\tau}\times(\GL_{2n,F})_{\tau c}
. Note that
r_{\iota}^{\vee,c}(1-2n)\cong r_{\iota}(\pi)
so
\mathrm{HT}_{\tau}(r_{\iota}(\pi))
and
\mathrm{HT}_{\tau c}(r_{\iota}(\pi))
can be read off from each other.

(3)

If
F_{0}\subset F
is an imaginary quadratic field and
\ell
is a prime
which splits in
F_{0}
(including possibly
\ell=p
), then for each prime
v\mid\ell
of
F
lying above
a prime
\bar{v}
of
F^{+}
, there is an isomorphism

\mathrm{WD}\left(r_{\iota}(\pi)|_{G_{F_{v}}}\right)^{F-ss}\simeq\mathrm{rec}^{T}_{F_{v}}(\pi_{\bar{v}}\circ\iota_{v}).

Proof.

This is
[
ACC+18
, Theorem 2.3.3]
. We mention that it
relies on the base change result of
[
Shi14
]
and on the existence and properties
of the Galois representations associated to regular algebraic, conjugate self-dual
cuspidal automorphic representations of
\GL_{m}
.
∎

Theorem 2.1.20
.

Let
\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathbb{T}^{T}(K,\lambda)

be a maximal ideal.
Suppose
F
contains an imaginary quadratic field, the finite set of finite places
T
of
F
is stable under complex conjugation, and the following condition is satisfied:

•

Let
v\notin T
be a finite place, with residue characteristic
\ell
. Then either

T
contains no
\ell
-adic places and
\ell
is unramified in
F
, or there exists an imaginary quadratic subfield of
F
in which
\ell
splits.
11
11

11

This condition
can always be realised after enlarging
T
and is used to ensure that the results of
[
Sch15
]

that we appeal to are unconditional.
.

Then there exists a continuous, semi-simple Galois representation

\bar{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}:G_{F,T}\to\GL_{n}\left(\mathbb{T}^{T}(K,\lambda)/\mathfrak{m}\right)

such that, for each finite place
v\not\in T
of
F
, the characteristic polynomial
of
\bar{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}(\mathrm{Frob}_{v})
is equal to the image of
P_{v}(X)
in

\left(\mathbb{T}^{T}(K,\lambda)/\mathfrak{m}\right)[X]
.

Proof.

This is
[
ACC+18
, Theorem 2.3.5]
: it essentially follows
from
[
Sch15
, Corollary 5.4.3]
.
∎

Lemma 2.1.21
.

Let
\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathbb{T}^{T}(K,\lambda)
be a maximal ideal as in Theorem
2.1.20
.
Suppose
k=k(\mathfrak{m})
. Let
v
be a
p
-adic place of
F
. The Hecke operator
U_{v}

has a unique eigenvalue on
H^{*}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}/\varpi)_{\mathfrak{m}}
, equal to
\bar{\epsilon}_{p}^{\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}({\operatorname{Art}}_{F_{v}}(\varpi_{v}))\cdot\det\bar{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}({\operatorname{Art}}_{F_{v}}(\varpi_{v}))
.

Proof.

Our proof of this will be global, computing the action of
U_{v}
in terms of a central Hecke operator at a suitable unramified prime. We write
Z_{n}
for the centre of
\GL_{n,F}
. We have a right action of
Z_{n}({\mathbb{A}}_{F})
on
X_{K}
(by right multiplication of the finite adelic part on
\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,f})
and of the archimedean part on
X^{\GL_{n,F}}
). The rescaled action of
u_{v}
on
{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}
allows us to define an action of
Z_{n}({\mathbb{A}}_{F})
on
{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}
which factors through the
p
-adic part and is compatible with its existing
K
action. We obtain an action of
Z_{n}({\mathbb{A}}_{F})
on
H^{*}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}/\varpi)_{\mathfrak{m}}
which factors through the quotient
Z_{n}({\mathbb{A}}_{F})/F_{\infty}^{\times}(Z_{n}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,f})\cap K)
for continuity reasons
12
12

12

In fact we do not even need continuity of the
F_{\infty}^{\times}
action. The cohomology groups are finite, so the action of
F_{\infty}^{\times}
gives a homomorphism from a product of copies of
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
to a finite group. This is necessarily trivial, since
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
has no finite index subgroups.
.

We also have a continuous character
\psi_{\mathfrak{m}}:{\mathbb{A}}_{F}^{\times}=Z_{n}({\mathbb{A}}_{F})\to k^{\times}
determined by

\psi_{\mathfrak{m}}=\bar{\epsilon}_{p}^{\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}\det\bar{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}\circ{\operatorname{Art}}_{F}.

Since
F_{\infty}^{\times}
is connected, and
\bar{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}
is unramified away from
T
,
\psi_{\mathfrak{m}}
factors through the quotient
F^{\times}\backslash Z_{n}({\mathbb{A}}_{F})/F_{\infty}^{\times}K_{Z}
for a compact open subgroup
K_{Z}=\prod_{w}K_{Z,w}
of
Z_{n}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,f})
with
K_{Z,w}={\mathcal{O}}_{F_{w}}^{\times}
for
w\notin T
. Shrinking
K_{Z}
if necessary, we assume that
K_{Z}\subset Z_{n}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,f})\cap K
. Note that for a finite place
w\notin T
,
\psi_{\mathfrak{m}}(\mathrm{Frob}_{w})
is equal to
T_{w,n}\mod\mathfrak{m}
.

By Chebotarev density, we can find a place
w\notin T
such that the uniformiser
\varpi_{w}
and
\varpi_{v}
map to the same element in the ray class group
F^{\times}\backslash Z_{n}({\mathbb{A}}_{F})/F_{\infty}^{\times}K_{Z}
.

The action of
Z_{n}({\mathbb{A}}_{F})
on
X_{K}
factors through the quotient
Z_{n}({\mathbb{A}}_{F})/(F_{\infty}^{\times}\cap K_{\infty}{\mathbb{R}}^{\times})(Z_{n}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,f})\cap K)
. We can choose
z_{\infty}\in F_{\infty}^{\times}
such that
z_{\infty}\varpi_{w}
and
\varpi_{v}
map to the same element in this quotient. Now we can compare the action of the two elements
z_{\infty}\varpi_{w}
and
\varpi_{v}
on cohomology. By construction, they act in the same way on
X_{K}
. They both act trivially on
{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}
. So they act the same on
H^{*}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}/\varpi)_{\mathfrak{m}}
. Since the action of
F_{\infty}^{\times}
on cohomology is trivial, we deduce that
\varpi_{w}
and
\varpi_{v}
have the same action on
H^{*}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}/\varpi)_{\mathfrak{m}}
. The unique eigenvalue of
\varpi_{w}
(i.e. of the Hecke operator
T_{w,n}
) on
H^{*}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}/\varpi)_{\mathfrak{m}}
is
\psi_{\mathfrak{m}}(\varpi_{w})
. Our choice of
w
means this is equal to
\psi_{\mathfrak{m}}(\varpi_{v})
, and we are done.
∎

Remark 2.1.22
.

A more conceptual proof for Lemma
2.1.21
can be given by arguing with (mod
\varpi
) completed cohomology at level
K^{p}
, localized at
\mathfrak{m}
. We can then assume
\lambda
is trivial, in which case completed cohomology is equipped with a continuous action of
F^{\times}\backslash{\mathbb{A}}_{F}^{\times}/F_{\infty}^{\times}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,f}\cap K^{p})
with unique system of eigenvalues corresponding to the character
\bar{\epsilon}_{p}^{\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}\cdot\det\bar{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}
.

Definition 2.1.23
.

We say that a maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathbb{T}^{T}(K,\lambda)

is
non-Eisenstein
if
\bar{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}
is absolutely irreducible.

Our convention is that when we ask for a maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}
to be non-Eisenstein, we are implicitly imposing the assumptions of Theorem
2.1.20
.

Theorem 2.1.24
.

Let
\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathbb{T}^{T}(K,\lambda)
be a non-Eisenstein maximal ideal. There exist an integer
N\geq 1
,
which depends only on
n
and
[F:{\mathbb{Q}}]
, an ideal
I\subset\mathbb{T}^{T}(K,\lambda)

satisfying
I^{N}=0
, and a continuous homomorphism

\rho_{\mathfrak{m}}:G_{F,T}\to\GL_{n}\left(\mathbb{T}^{T}(K,\lambda)/I\right)

such that, for each finite place
v\not\in T
of
F
, the characteristic polynomial
of
\rho_{\mathfrak{m}}(\mathrm{Frob}_{v})
is equal to the image of
P_{v}(X)
in

\left(\mathbb{T}^{T}(K,\lambda)/I\right)[X]
.

Proof.

This is
[
Sch15
, Corollary 5.4.4]
.
∎

Let
\bar{S}\subseteq\bar{S}_{p}
and let

\widetilde{K}\subset\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})
be a compact open subgroup, which is
decomposed with respect to the Levi decomposition
P=G\ltimes U
,
and which satisfies
\widetilde{K}_{\bar{v}}={\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{v}}(b,c)
or
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}
for all

\bar{v}\in\bar{S}
.
We set
K:=\widetilde{K}\cap G({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})
and
K_{U}:=\widetilde{K}\cap U({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})
.
Let
\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathbb{T}^{T}(K,\lambda)
be a non-Eisenstein maximal
ideal and let
\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}\subset\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{T}
denote its pullback
under the unnormalised Satake transform
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{T}\to\mathbb{T}^{T}
.
Recall that the boundary
\partial\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}}
of the Borel–Serre
compactification of
\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}}
has a
\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})
-equivariant
stratification indexed by the rational parabolic subgroups of
\widetilde{G}
which contain
\widetilde{B}
.
See
[
NT16
, §3.1.2]
, especially
[
NT16
, Lemma 3.10]
for more details.
For such a standard parabolic
subgroup
Q
, we denote by
\widetilde{X}^{Q}_{\widetilde{K}}
the stratum labeled
by
Q
. This stratum can be written as a double quotient:

\widetilde{X}^{Q}_{\widetilde{K}}=Q(F^{+})\backslash X^{Q}\times\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})/K

By applying the formalism in §
2.1.2
,
there is, for any
\tilde{\lambda}\in({\mathbb{Z}}^{2n}_{+})^{\Hom(F^{+},E)}

a homomorphism

\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{T}\to\mathrm{End}_{D^{+}({\mathcal{O}})}\left(R\Gamma\left(\widetilde{X}^{Q}_{\widetilde{K}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}\right)\right).

Therefore, we can define the localisation

R\Gamma\left(\widetilde{X}^{Q}_{\widetilde{K}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}\right)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
.

Theorem 2.1.25
.

Let
\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathbb{\mathbb{T}}^{T}(K,\lambda)
be a non-Eisenstein maximal ideal and let

\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}:={\mathcal{S}}^{*}(\mathfrak{m})\subset\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{T}

.
Pullback along the natural inclusion induces a

\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{T}
