:

R\Gamma\left(\partial\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}\right)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}R\Gamma\left(\widetilde{X}^{P}_{\widetilde{K}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}\right)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}.

Proof.

This is
[
ACC+18
, Thm. 2.4.2]
.
∎

Theorem 2.1.26
.

Let
\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}\subset\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{T}(\widetilde{K},\tilde{\lambda})

be a maximal ideal.
Suppose
F
contains an imaginary quadratic field,
the finite set of finite places
T
of
F
is stable under
complex conjugation, and the following condition is satisfied:

•

Let
v\notin T
be a finite place, with residue characteristic
\ell
. Then either

T
contains no
\ell
-adic places and
\ell
is unramified in
F
, or there exists an imaginary quadratic subfield of
F
in which
\ell
splits.

Then there exists a continuous, semi-simple Galois representation

\bar{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}:G_{F,T}\to\GL_{2n}\left(\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{T}(\widetilde{K},\tilde{\lambda})/\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}\right)

such that, for each finite place
v\not\in T
of
F
, the characteristic polynomial
of
\bar{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}(\mathrm{Frob}_{v})
is equal to the image of
\widetilde{P}_{v}(X)
in

\left(\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{T}(\widetilde{K},\tilde{\lambda})/\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}\right)[X]
.

Proof.

We will use the Hecke algebra

\mathbb{T}^{T}_{cl}
defined in
[
CGH+20
, §6.5]
.
By reducing to
{\mathbb{F}}
-coefficients and increasing the level
to sufficiently small compact opens
\widetilde{K}^{\prime}_{\bar{v}}
at all
primes
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}_{p}
, we can trivialise the local system

{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}}{\mathbb{F}}
. The proof of
[
CGH+20
, Theorem 6.5.3]

shows that the map

\mathbb{T}^{T}_{cl}\to\mathrm{End}_{D^{+}\left(\prod_{\bar{v}\in\bar{S}_{p}}\widetilde{G}({\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}_{\bar{v}}})/\widetilde{K}^{\prime}_{\bar{v}},{\mathbb{F}}\right)}\left(R\Gamma(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}^{\prime}},{\mathbb{F}})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}}}\left({\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}}{\mathbb{F}}\right)\right)

is continuous for the discrete topology on the target. By the Hochschild–Serre spectral
sequence, this implies
that the map

\mathbb{T}^{T}_{cl}\to\mathrm{End}_{D^{+}({\mathbb{F}})}\left(R\Gamma\left(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}}{\mathbb{F}}\right)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}\right)

is also continuous for the discrete topology on the target.
The existence of a determinant
valued in
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{T}(\widetilde{K},\tilde{\lambda})/\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}
now
follows
from
[
CGH+20
, Lemma 6.5.2]
, which is the version of
[
Sch15
, Corollary 5.1.11]

for usual cohomology and which can be made unconditional by using
Theorem
2.1.19

as an input. This
determinant corresponds to a semi-simple
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}
-valued representation by
[
Che14
, Theorem A]
.
This representation can be realised over
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{T}(\widetilde{K},\tilde{\lambda})/\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}

by the same argument as in the proof of
[
ACC+18
, Theorem 2.3.5]
.
∎

We now introduce the key technical condition that our residual
representations must satisfy in order to appeal to the main
result of
[
CS24
]
.

Definition 2.1.27
.

A continuous representation
\bar{\rho}:G_{F}\to\GL_{m}({\mathbb{F}})
is
decomposed generic
13
13

13

This is slightly weaker than the condition called

decomposed generic
in
[
CS17
]
.
See
[
CS24
, Remark 1.4]

and
[
CS24
, Corollary 5.1.3]
for an explanation.
if there exists a prime
\ell\not=p

such that the following are satisfied:

(1)

the prime
\ell
splits completely in
F
;

(2)

for every prime
v\mid\ell
of
F
, the representation

\bar{\rho}|_{G_{F_{v}}}
is unramified and the eigenvalues

\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{m}
of
\bar{\rho}(\mathrm{Frob}_{v})
satisfy

\alpha_{i}/\alpha_{j}\not=\ell
for
i\not=j
.

We note that if a representation
\bar{\rho}
is decomposed generic,
then by the Chebotarev density theorem there exist infinitely
many primes
\ell\not=p
as in Definition
2.1.27
,
cf.
[
ACC+18
, Lemma 4.3.2]
.

Theorem 2.1.28
.

Keep the same assumptions on
F
as in Theorem
2.1.26
.
Let
\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}\subset\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{T}(\widetilde{K},\tilde{\lambda})

be a maximal ideal such that the associated Galois representation

\bar{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
constructed in Theorem
2.1.26

is decomposed generic, in the sense of Definition
2.1.27
. Recall
that
d=\dim_{{\mathbb{C}}}\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}}
. Then
we have
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{T}
-equivariant morphisms

H^{d}\left(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}[1/p]\right)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}\hookleftarrow H^{d}\left(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}\right)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}\twoheadrightarrow H^{d}\left(\partial\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}\right)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}.

Proof.

This follows from
[
CS24
, Thm. 1.1]

as in the proof of
[
ACC+18
, Thm. 4.3.3]
. Moreover, we can remove the technical hypotheses that
[F^{+}:{\mathbb{Q}}]>1
and
\bar{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
has length at most two by appealing to Koshikawa’s work
[
Kos21
, Theorem 1.4]
.
∎

2.2.
P
-ordinary Hida theory

In this section, we develop a

P
-ordinary version of Hida theory for group
\widetilde{G}
and
the Betti cohomology of the locally
symmetric spaces
\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}}
,
by extending the theory developed in
[
TU99
]
for

\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
and the Betti cohomology of
Siegel modular threefolds. We relate
this construction to the
P
-ordinary part of completed cohomology.

For this entire section, fix a subset
\bar{S}\subset\bar{S}_{p}
,
where we will take the
P
-ordinary (or, slightly more generally,
Q
-ordinary)
part of the cohomology of the
\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}}
.

2.2.1.
P
-ordinary Hida theory at finite level

In this section, we will only consider good subgroups

\widetilde{K}\subset\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,f})
such that the tame level
\widetilde{K}^{p}
is fixed
and such that, for all
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}
,

\widetilde{K}_{\bar{v}}={\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{v}}(b,c)
for
some integers
c\geq b\geq 0
. We denote
such a good subgroup by
\widetilde{K}(b,c)
and assume
from now on that
c\geq 1
. Also set
{\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{S}}(b,c):=\prod_{\bar{v}\in\bar{S}}{\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{v}}(b,c)
.

Recall from (
2.1.8
) that we have well-defined actions of the Hecke algebras

{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{S}},\widetilde{K}_{\bar{S}})

on the complexes
R\Gamma(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}(b,c)},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}})
.
These actions are compatible
with the natural pullback maps as
b,c
vary.
We define the
P
-ordinary part
R\Gamma(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}(b,c)},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}})^{\ord}

of the complex
R\Gamma(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}(b,c)},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}})

to be the maximal direct summand on which all the
\widetilde{U}_{\tilde{v},n}
act invertibly. This is a well-defined object
of
D^{+}\left({\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{S}}(0,c)/{\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{S}}(b,c),{\mathcal{O}}\right)
by
Lemma
2.1.6
and by
the theory of ordinary parts, cf.
[
KT17
, §2.4]
. Moreover,
it inherits an action of the abstract Hecke algebra

\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{T}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}(\bigotimes_{\bar{v}\in\bar{S}}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{v}},\widetilde{K}_{\bar{v}})[\widetilde{U}_{\tilde{v},n}^{-1}])
.

We similarly have well-defined actions of the Hecke algebras
{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{S}},\widetilde{K}_{\bar{S}})

on the complexes
R\Gamma(\partial\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}(b,c)},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}})
.
Therefore, we can also define
the
P
-ordinary part
R\Gamma(\partial\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}(b,c)},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}})^{\ord}

of the complex
R\Gamma(\partial\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}(b,c)},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}})
,
which is an object of
D^{+}\left({\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{S}}(0,c)/{\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{S}}(b,c),{\mathcal{O}}\right)
equipped
with an action of

\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{T}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}(\bigotimes_{\bar{v}\in\bar{S}}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{v}},\widetilde{K}_{\bar{v}})[\widetilde{U}_{\tilde{v},n}^{-1}])
.

2.2.2.
The
P
-ordinary part of a smooth representation

In this section, we will define various functors that
will allow us to study
P
-ordinary Hida theory using
completed cohomology. These will be variants of
the functors considered in
[
ACC+18
, §5.2.1]
, with essentially the same
properties, and we
will appeal to the basic results in
loc. cit.
throughout. We will introduce a variant with more general parahoric level in §
2.2.11
— we find it clearer to introduce the simplest version of the theory first, which is already sufficient for our results on local-global compatibility in the crystalline case.

For an integer
b\geq 0
, we set

K_{\bar{v}}(b):=\ker\left(G({\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}_{\bar{v}}})\to G({\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}_{\bar{v}}}/\varpi^{b}_{\bar{v}})\right),K_{\bar{S}}(b):=\prod_{\bar{v}}K_{\bar{v}}(b),K_{\bar{S}}:=K_{\bar{S}}(0).

We also set
U^{0}_{\bar{S}}:=\prod_{\bar{v}\in\bar{S}}U({\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}_{\bar{v}}})
.
Let
\Delta^{+}_{\bar{S}}\subset G_{\bar{S}}
denote the monoid
generated by
K_{\bar{S}}
and by
\{\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},n}\mid\bar{v}\in\bar{S}\}

and
\Delta_{\bar{S}}\subset G_{\bar{S}}
the subgroup obtained by adjoining the inverses of the elements
\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},n}
. We set
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{S},P}:=\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{S}}\cap\prod_{\bar{v}\in\bar{S}}P(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})
and note that we have
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{S},P}=\Delta^{+}_{\bar{S}}\ltimes U^{0}_{\bar{S}}
.

We let

\Gamma(U^{0}_{\bar{S}},\ ):\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{S}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\to\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\Delta^{+}_{\bar{S}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}).

denote the functor of
U^{0}_{\bar{S}}
-invariants.

For
V\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{S}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
, we define the action of
an element
g\in\Delta^{+}_{\bar{S}}

on
v\in\Gamma(U^{0}_{\bar{S}},V)
by the formula

(2.2.1)

v\mapsto g\cdot v:=\sum_{n\in U^{0}_{\bar{S}}/gU^{0}_{\bar{S}}g^{-1}}ngv,

cf.
[
Eme10a
, §3]
.
We obtain a derived functor

R\Gamma(U^{0}_{\bar{S}},\ ):D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{S}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\to D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\Delta^{+}_{\bar{S}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}).

Since
U^{0}_{\bar{S}}
is compact, an injective object in
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{S}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
remains
\Gamma(U^{0}_{\bar{S}},\ )
-acyclic on restriction to
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{S},P}
. So this derived functor factors through the restriction functor to
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{S},P},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
.

We also define a functor

\mathrm{ord}:\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\Delta^{+}_{\bar{S}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\to\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\Delta_{\bar{S}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})

that is the composition of the localisation functors

\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}[\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},n}]}{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}[(\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},n})^{\pm 1}]

for all
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}
. Note that

\ord
is an exact functor because localisation is an
exact functor, and it preserves injectives by the same
argument as in
[
ACC+18
, Lemma 5.2.7]
.

Definition 2.2.3
.

We have a functor
of
P
-ordinary parts

D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{S}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\to D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\Delta_{\bar{S}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}),\pi\mapsto R\Gamma(U^{0}_{\bar{S}},\pi)^{\ord}

obtained by composing the functor
R\Gamma(U^{0}_{\bar{S}},\ )
with the
functor
\mathrm{ord}
.

For
b\geq 0
, we also have a functor

\Gamma(U^{0}_{\bar{S}}\rtimes K_{\bar{S}}(b),\ ):\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{S}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\to\mathrm{Mod}(\Delta^{+}_{\bar{S}}/K_{\bar{S}}(b),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}),

where the action of
g\in\Delta^{+}_{\bar{S}}/K_{\bar{S}}(b)
is
given by the same formula (
2.2.1
). We
denote the corresponding derived functor by

R\Gamma(U^{0}_{\bar{S}}\rtimes K_{\bar{S}}(b),\ )
.
We also define the functor

\mathrm{ord}_{b}:\mathrm{Mod}(\Delta^{+}_{\bar{S}}/K_{\bar{S}}(b),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\to\mathrm{Mod}(\Delta_{\bar{S}}/K_{\bar{S}}(b),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})

by localisation. Note that

\mathrm{ord}_{b}
is also an exact functor that preserves injectives.
Finally, for
c\geq b\geq 0
and
c\geq 1
, we also have a functor

\Gamma({\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{S}}(b,c),\ ):\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{S}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\to\mathrm{Mod}(\Delta^{+}_{\bar{S}}/K_{\bar{S}}(b),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}),

where the action of
g\in\Delta^{+}_{\bar{S}}/K_{\bar{S}}(b)
is
given by the same formula (
2.2.1
). Note here that on the right hand side we are considering the natural action of the Hecke algebra

{\mathcal{H}}({\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{S}}(0,c)\Delta^{+}_{\bar{S}}{\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{S}}(0,c),{\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{S}}(b,c))={\mathcal{O}}[\Delta_{\bar{S}}^{+}/K_{\bar{S}}(b)].

Lemma 2.2.4
.

There is
a natural isomorphism

\ord_{b}\circ\Gamma(K_{\bar{S}}(b),\ )\simeq\Gamma(K_{\bar{S}}(b),\ )\circ\ord

of functors
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\Delta^{+}_{\bar{S}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\to\mathrm{Mod}(\Delta_{\bar{S}}/K_{\bar{S}}(b),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
, which extends to an isomorphism of derived functors

\ord_{b}\circ R\Gamma(K_{\bar{S}}(b),\ )\simeq R\Gamma(K_{\bar{S}}(b),\ )\circ\ord.

Proof.

The same argument as for
[
ACC+18
, Lemma 5.2.6]
works for the un-derived statement. Since
\mathrm{ord}
is exact and preserves injectives and
\ord_{b}
is exact, the statement for derived functors follows.
∎

Lemma 2.2.5
.
14
14

14

Compare with
[
ACC+18
, Lemma 5.2.8]

For all
c\geq b\geq 0
with
c\geq 1
,
there is a natural isomorphism

\ord_{b}\circ\Gamma(U^{0}_{\bar{S}}\rtimes K_{\bar{S}}(b),\ )\simeq\ord_{b}\circ\Gamma({\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{S}}(b,c),\ )

of functors

\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{S}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\to\mathrm{Mod}(\Delta_{\bar{S}}/K_{\bar{S}}(b),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}).

Proof.

Let
V\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{S}}),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
.
We first claim that the natural inclusion
\Gamma({\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{S}}(b,c),V)\hookrightarrow\Gamma(U^{0}_{\bar{S}}\rtimes K_{\bar{S}}(b),V)

is a morphism of
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}[\Delta^{+}_{\bar{S}}]
-modules. Indeed,
by the formula (
2.2.1
), it is enough to check that, for all

g\in\Delta^{+}_{\bar{S}}/K_{\bar{S}}(b)
,
the map

U^{0}_{\bar{S}}/gU^{0}_{\bar{S}}g^{-1}\to{\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{S}}(b,c)/g{\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{S}}(b,c)g^{-1}

is bijective, which holds by the Iwahori decomposition of
{\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{S}}(b,c)
with respect to
P
.
By the exactness of
\ord_{b}
, we obtain an injection
\ord_{b}\Gamma({\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{S}}(b,c),V)\hookrightarrow\ord_{b}\Gamma(U^{0}_{\bar{S}}\rtimes K_{\bar{S}}(b),V)
.

We are left to show that this injection is an equality. Let
\tilde{u}_{\bar{S}}:=\prod_{\bar{v}\in\bar{S}}\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},n}

and
\widetilde{U}_{\bar{S}}:=\prod_{\bar{v}\in\bar{S}}\widetilde{U}_{\tilde{v},n}
. The result will follow if we show that,
for any
v\in\Gamma(U^{0}_{\bar{S}}\rtimes K_{\bar{S}}(b),V)
, there exists
N\geq 0
such that

(\tilde{u}_{\bar{S}})^{N}v\in V^{{\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{S}}(b,c)}
. Since
V
is smooth, there exists
c^{\prime}\geq c

such that
v\in V^{{\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{S}}(b,c^{\prime})}
. However, if
c^{\prime}\geq 2
, then
\widetilde{U}_{\bar{S}}v\in V^{{\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{S}}(b,c^{\prime}-1)}
by
[
Eme10a
, Lemma 3.3.2]
. We conclude
by induction.
∎

Lemma 2.2.6
.

Let
\pi\in D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{S}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
. Then for any
c\geq b\geq 0
with
c\geq 1
there is a natural
isomorphism

R\Gamma(K_{\bar{S}}(b),\ord R\Gamma(U^{0}_{\bar{S}},\pi))\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}\ord_{b}R\Gamma({\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{S}}(b,c),\pi)

in
D^{+}(\Delta_{\bar{S}}/K_{\bar{S}}(b),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
.

Proof.

This is proved in the same way as
[
ACC+18
, Lemma
5.2.9]
, except we appeal to Lemma
14
above
instead of Lemma 5.2.8 of
op. cit.

∎

2.2.7.
Independence of level

Recall that the finite free
{\mathcal{O}}
-module

{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}
is equipped with an action
of
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{S}}
.
We consider the completed cohomology at
\bar{S}
with coefficients
in
{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m}
:

\pi(\widetilde{K}^{\bar{S}},\tilde{\lambda},m):=R\Gamma\left(\widetilde{K}^{\bar{S}},R\Gamma\left(\mathfrak{\overline{X}}_{\widetilde{G}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m}\right)\right).

We are using the discussion following Lemma
2.1.8
to regard
R\Gamma\left(\mathfrak{\overline{X}}_{\widetilde{G}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m}\right)
as an object of
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\widetilde{G}^{T}\times\widetilde{K}_{T\backslash\bar{S}}\times\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{S}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
. Then
\pi(\widetilde{K}^{\bar{S}},\tilde{\lambda},m)
is an object of
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{S}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})

equipped with an action of
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{T}
.
We note that, for any
c\geq b\geq 0
,
{\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{S}}(b,c)\subset\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{S}}

and we have a canonical
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{T}
-equivariant
isomorphism

R\Gamma\left({\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{S}}(b,c),\pi(\widetilde{K}^{\bar{S}},\tilde{\lambda},m)\right)\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}R\Gamma\left(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}(b,c)},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m}\right)

in
D^{+}\left({\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{S}}(0,c)/{\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{S}}(b,c),{\mathcal{O}}\right/\varpi^{m})
.

We define
\pi^{\ord}(\widetilde{K}^{\bar{S}},\tilde{\lambda},m)

to be the
P
-ordinary part of
\pi(\widetilde{K}^{\bar{S}},\tilde{\lambda},m)

as in Definition
2.2.3
.
This is an object in
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\Delta_{\bar{S}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})

equipped with an action of
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{T}
.

Proposition 2.2.8
.

For
any integers
m\geq 1
and
c\geq b\geq 0
with
c\geq 1
,
there is a natural
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{T}
-equivariant
isomorphism

R\Gamma\left(K_{\bar{S}}(b),\pi^{\ord}(\widetilde{K}^{\bar{S}},\tilde{\lambda},m)\right)\simeq R\Gamma\left(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}(b,c)},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m}\right)^{\ord}

in
D^{+}(K_{\bar{S}}/K_{\bar{S}}(b),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
.

Proof.

This is proved in the same way as
[
ACC+18
, Prop. 5.2.15]
, given
Lemma
2.2.6
as an input.
Again, the key point is that the two definitions of
P
-ordinary parts

(via Hida’s idempotent as in
[
KT17
, §2.4]
or via localisation as in §
2.2.2
) agree on
finite
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}
-modules, and that the cohomology groups
of
R\Gamma\left(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}(b,c)},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m}\right)

are finite
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}
-modules by Lemma
2.1.6
.
∎

Corollary 2.2.9

(Independence of level
15
15

15

Compare with
[
ACC+18
, Cor. 5.2.16]
)
.

For
any integers
m\geq 1
and
c\geq b\geq 0
with
c\geq 1
, the natural

\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{T}
-equivariant
morphism

R\Gamma\left(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}(b,\mathrm{max}\{1,b\})},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m}\right)^{\ord}\to R\Gamma\left(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}(b,c)},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m}\right)^{\ord}

is an
isomorphism in
D^{+}(K_{\bar{S}}/K_{\bar{S}}(b),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
.

This follows immediately from Proposition
2.2.8
, upon noting that the LHS of the main isomorphism
is independent of
c\geq\mathrm{max}\{1,b\}
.
∎

The same results hold for the cohomology of
the Borel–Serre boundary, with the same proof.

Proposition 2.2.10
.

For
any integers
m\geq 1
and

with
c\geq 1
,
there is a natural
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{T}

isomorphism

R\Gamma\left(K_{\bar{S}}(b),\pi_{\partial}^{\ord}(\widetilde{K}^{\bar{S}},\tilde{\lambda},m)\right)\simeq R\Gamma\left(\partial\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}(b,c)},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m}\right)^{\ord}

in
D^{+}(K_{\bar{S}}/K_{\bar{S}}(b),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
.

2.2.11.
A variant at parahoric level

We now suppose we have standard parabolic subgroups
Q_{\bar{v}}\subset P_{\bar{v}}
for each
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}
, with corresponding parahoric subgroup
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\subset\widetilde{G}({\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}_{\bar{v}}})
. We will still be interested in
P
-ordinary parts — in other words, we only invert the Hecke operator for the element
\tilde{u}_{\overline{S}}
, as in the previous subsection. Our degree shifting arguments will apply to this
P
-ordinary part. Later on, we will take
Q
-ordinary parts after specialising to finite level
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\overline{S}}
, but this means that we need to keep track of additional Hecke operators on our cohomology groups.

Recall that we have defined monoids
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{S}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}}},{\Delta}_{\bar{S}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}},+},{\Delta}_{\bar{S}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}}}
. We will also make use of
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{S},P}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}}}:=\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{S}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}}}\cap\prod_{\bar{v}\in\bar{S}}P(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})
. Note that
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{S},P}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}}}={\Delta}_{\bar{S}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}},+}\ltimes U^{0}_{\bar{S}}
. We set
K_{\bar{v}}:={\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\cap G(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})
for each
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}
.

Using the formula (
2.2.1
), we define a functor

R\Gamma(U^{0}_{\bar{S}},-):D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{S}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\to D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}({\Delta}_{\bar{S}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}},+},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})

which factors through
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{S},P}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
, and the localisation, inverting
\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},n}
for all
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}
,

\ord:D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}({\Delta}_{\bar{S}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}},+},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\to D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}({\Delta}_{\bar{S}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})

with composition denoted by
\pi\mapsto R\Gamma(U^{0}_{\bar{S}},\pi)^{\ord}
.

Note that
K_{\bar{S}}
is not in general normal in
{\Delta}_{\bar{S}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}},+}
, but it is in the submonoid
K_{\bar{S}}[\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},n}|\bar{v}\in\bar{S}]
. This means we can define a functor

\ord_{0}R\Gamma\left(K_{\bar{S}},-\right):D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}({\Delta}_{\bar{S}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}},+},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\to D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(K_{\bar{S}}[\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},n}^{\pm 1}|\bar{v}\in\bar{S}]/K_{\bar{S}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})

as above, and equip an object in the image of this functor with an action of the Hecke algebra
{\mathcal{H}}({\Delta}_{\bar{S}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}}},K_{\bar{S}})
. We can also compute this functor as
R\Gamma(K_{\bar{S}},-)\circ\ord
, as in Lemma
2.2.4
.
We have an analogue of Lemma
2.2.6
:

Lemma 2.2.12
.

Let
\pi\in D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{S}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
. There is a natural isomorphism

R\Gamma(K_{\bar{S}},\ord R\Gamma(U^{0}_{\bar{S}},\pi))\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}\ord_{0}R\Gamma\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}},\pi\right)

in
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(K_{\bar{S}}[\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},n}^{\pm 1}|\bar{v}\in\bar{S}]/K_{\bar{S}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
under which the action of
[K_{\bar{v}}\nu(\varpi_{\bar{v}})K_{\bar{v}}]\in{\mathcal{H}}({\Delta}_{\bar{S}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}}},K_{\bar{S}})
on the left hand side matches with the action of
[{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\nu(\varpi_{\bar{v}}){\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}]
on the right hand side.

Proof.

The proof is essentially the same as Lemma
2.2.6
. The description of the Hecke action follows from the decomposition
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{S},P}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}}}={\Delta}_{\bar{S}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}},+}\ltimes U^{0}_{\bar{S}}
and the fact that the formula defining the double coset operator
[{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\cap P(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})\nu(\varpi_{\bar{v}}){\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\cap P(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})]
also defines
[{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\nu(\varpi_{\bar{v}}){\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}]
.

More precisely, if
V\in\Mod_{\mathrm{sm}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{S}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
, we consider the natural inclusion

\Gamma({\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}},V)\hookrightarrow\Gamma(U^{0}_{\bar{S}}\ltimes K_{\bar{S}},V).

For
\delta\in{\Delta}_{\bar{v}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}},+}
, the action of
[{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\delta{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}]
on the left hand side is given by
\sum_{\gamma\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}/({\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\cap\delta{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\delta^{-1})}\gamma\delta
. The action on the right hand side is given by
\sum_{k\in K_{\bar{v}}/(K_{\bar{v}}\cap\delta K_{\bar{v}}\delta^{-1})}\sum_{n\in U_{\bar{v}}^{0}/k\delta U_{\bar{v}}^{0}(k\delta)^{-1}}nk\delta
. The Iwahori decomposition of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}
with respect to
P
shows that the map
(k,n)\mapsto nk
gives a well-defined bijection

\{(k,n):k\in K_{\bar{v}}/(K_{\bar{v}}\cap\delta K_{\bar{v}}\delta^{-1}),n\in U_{\bar{v}}^{0}/k\delta U_{\bar{v}}^{0}(k\delta)^{-1}\}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}/({\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\cap\delta{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\delta^{-1}).

It remains to show that the inclusion

\ord_{0}\Gamma({\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}},V)\hookrightarrow\ord_{0}\Gamma(U^{0}_{\bar{S}}\ltimes K_{\bar{S}},V)

is bijective. As in the proof of Lemma
14
, if
v
is in the right hand side, there exists
c\geq 1
such that
v\in V^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}}\cap{\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{S}}(0,c)}
. We can apply
[
Eme10a
, Lemma 3.3.2]
again to show that
\widetilde{U}_{\bar{S}}^{N}v\in V^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}}}
for some
N\geq 0
.
∎

Using this lemma, we get natural analogues of our independence of level statements. In this context, we will have
\pi^{\ord}(\widetilde{K}^{\bar{S}},\tilde{\lambda},m)\in D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\widetilde{\Delta}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}}}_{\bar{S}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
and the same for boundary cohomology.

2.2.13.
Independence of weight

We retain our current set-up, with parabolic subgroups
Q_{\bar{v}}
for
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}
and a parahoric level subgroup
K_{\bar{S}}
. Assume that we have a dominant weight
\lambda
for
G
. For a subset
\bar{S}\subseteq\bar{S}_{p}
, set

{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}}}:=\bigotimes_{{v}\in{S}}\bigotimes_{\tau\in\Hom(F_{{v}},E),{\mathcal{O}}}{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{{\tau}}}.

This is, a priori, a finite free
{\mathcal{O}}
-module with an action of
K_{\bar{S}}
. We have explained how to extend the inflate
the
K_{\bar{S}}
-action
to an action of
\Delta^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}}}_{\bar{S}}
(in particular,
\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},n}
acts trivially for each
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}
).

Lemma 2.2.14
.

Using our identification of
K_{\bar{S}}
with the block diagonal Levi subgroup of
\prod_{\bar{v}\in\bar{S}}\mathrm{P}_{n,n}({\mathcal{O}}_{F_{\tilde{v}}})
, we can identify

{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}}}=\bigotimes_{{\bar{v}}\in{\bar{S}}}\bigotimes_{\tau\in\Hom(F^{+}_{\bar{v}},E),{\mathcal{O}}}{\mathcal{V}}_{-w_{0,n}\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}c}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}}}

with the action on both factors
{\mathcal{V}}_{-w_{0,n}\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}c}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}}}
defined using the embedding
\tilde{\tau}
.

Proof.

Recall that for each
\bar{v}\in\overline{S}
and place
\tilde{v}|\bar{v}
of
F
we have an isomorphism
\iota_{\tilde{v}}:\widetilde{G}(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})\cong\GL_{2n}(F_{\tilde{v}})
identifying the Levi subgroup
G(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})=\GL_{n}(F_{\tilde{v}})\times\GL_{n}(F_{\tilde{v}^{c}})
with block diagonal matrices in
\mathrm{P}_{n,n}(F_{\tilde{v}})
via
(A_{\tilde{v}},A_{\tilde{v}^{c}})\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}(\Psi_{n}\ ^{t}A_{\tilde{v}^{c}}^{-1}\Psi_{n})^{c}&0\\
0&A_{\tilde{v}}\end{pmatrix}
. We write
\theta
for the isomorphism
\GL_{n}(F_{\tilde{v}^{c}})\cong\GL_{n}(F_{\tilde{v}})
defined by

\theta(A)=(\Psi_{n}\ ^{t}A^{-1}\Psi_{n})^{c},

which preserves our chosen Borel subgroup
\mathrm{B}
.

For each
\tau\in\Hom(F^{+}_{\bar{v}},E)
,
K_{\bar{v}}=\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{F_{\tilde{v}}})\times\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{F_{\tilde{v}^{c}}})
acts ‘factor-by-factor’ on
{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}c}}
. Describing this representation in terms of block diagonal matrices, we get
\theta^{-1}{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}c}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}}}
, where
\theta^{-1}{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}c}}
denotes the representation of
\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{F_{\tilde{v}}})
given by pulling back the representation
{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}c}}
by
\theta^{-1}
. To finish the proof, we need to explain why
\theta^{-1}{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}c}}\cong{\mathcal{V}}_{-w_{0,n}\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}c}}
. To see this, consider the map

\displaystyle(\Ind_{\mathrm{B}_{n}}^{\GL_{n}}w_{0,n}\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}c})_{/{\mathcal{O}}}

\displaystyle\to(\Ind_{\mathrm{B}_{n}}^{\GL_{n}}-\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}c})_{/{\mathcal{O}}}

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle\mapsto\left(g\mapsto f(\Psi_{n}\ ^{t}g^{-1}\Psi_{n})\right)

and note that it gives the desired isomorphism.
∎

Proposition 2.2.15
.

Given a dominant weight

\tilde{\lambda}
for
\widetilde{G}
and a subset
\bar{S}\subseteq\bar{S}_{p}
,
let
\tilde{\lambda}^{\bar{S}}
be defined as follows:

•

if
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}
and
\tau\in\Hom(F^{+}_{\bar{v}},E)
, then
\tilde{\lambda}^{\bar{S}}_{\tau}:=(0,\dots,0)
.

•

if
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}_{p}\setminus\bar{S}
and

\tau\in\Hom(F^{+}_{\bar{v}},E)
, then
\tilde{\lambda}^{\bar{S}}_{\tau}:=\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}
.

Identify
\tilde{\lambda}
with a dominant weight
\lambda
for
G
as in
2.1.4
. For any integer
m\geq 1

there is a natural
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{T}
-equivariant isomorphism

\pi^{\ord}(\widetilde{K}^{\bar{S}},\tilde{\lambda},m)\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}\pi^{\ord}(\widetilde{K}^{\bar{S}},\tilde{\lambda}^{\bar{S}},m)\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{w^{P}_{0}\lambda_{\bar{S}}}/\varpi^{m}

in
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\Delta^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}}}_{\bar{S}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
.

Proof.

Note first that
\pi^{\ord}(\widetilde{K}^{\bar{S}},\tilde{\lambda},m)

only depends on
{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}}}/\varpi^{m}
as
an object in
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(U^{0}_{\bar{S}}\rtimes\Delta^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}},+}_{\bar{S}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
.
As in Lemma
2.1.12
, there is a
U^{0}_{\bar{S}}\rtimes\Delta^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}},+}_{\bar{S}}
-equivariant morphism
of finite free
{\mathcal{O}}
-modules

\mathrm{ev}:{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}}}\to{\mathcal{V}}_{w^{P}_{0}\lambda_{\bar{S}}}

given by evaluation of functions at the identity, where the action of

U^{0}_{\bar{S}}\rtimes\Delta^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}},+}_{\bar{S}}\subset\widetilde{\Delta}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}}}_{\bar{S}}
on the LHS is as in Lemma
2.1.17

and the action on the RHS factors through the
action of
\Delta^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}},+}_{\bar{S}}
. By Lemma
2.1.12
, this morphism
is surjective. Let
{\mathcal{K}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}}}:=\ker(\mathrm{ev})
,
a finite free
{\mathcal{O}}
-module with an action of
U^{0}_{\bar{S}}\rtimes\Delta^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}},+}_{\bar{S}}
.

For any
m\geq 1
, we have a short exact sequence of

{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}[U^{0}_{\bar{S}}\rtimes\Delta^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}},+}_{\bar{S}}]
-modules

0\to{\mathcal{K}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}}}/\varpi^{m}\to{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}}}/\varpi^{m}\to{\mathcal{V}}_{w^{P}_{0}\lambda_{\bar{S}}}/\varpi^{m}\to 0.

We first claim that
\mathrm{ev}\pmod{\varpi^{m}}
induces an isomorphism between

\pi^{\ord}(\widetilde{K}^{\bar{S}},\tilde{\lambda},m)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\ord R\Gamma\left(U^{0}_{\bar{S}},R\Gamma\left(\widetilde{K}^{\bar{S}},R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\widetilde{G}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m})\right)\right)

and

(2.2.2)

\ord R\Gamma\left(U^{0}_{\bar{S}},R\Gamma\left(\widetilde{K}^{\bar{S}},R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\widetilde{G}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}^{\bar{S}}}/\varpi^{m})\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{w^{P}_{0}\lambda_{\bar{S}}}/\varpi^{m}\right)\right).

By definition (cf. Lemma
2.1.8
), we have

R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\widetilde{G}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m})=R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\widetilde{G}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}^{\bar{S}}}/\varpi^{m})\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}}}/\varpi^{m}.

To prove the claim, it is therefore enough to show that

\ord R\Gamma\left(U^{0}_{\bar{S}},R\Gamma\left(\widetilde{K}^{\bar{S}},R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{\widetilde{G}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}^{\bar{S}}}/\varpi^{m})\otimes{\mathcal{K}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}}}/\varpi^{m}\right)\right)

is trivial. This follows from Lemma
2.2.16
below.

Finally, we observe that there is a natural isomorphism
in
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\Delta^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}}}_{\bar{S}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})

between (
2.2.2
)
and
\pi^{\ord}(\widetilde{K}^{\bar{S}},\tilde{\lambda}^{\bar{S}},m)\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{w^{P}_{0}\lambda_{\bar{S}}}/\varpi^{m}
.
Indeed,
\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},n}
,
\widetilde{K}^{\bar{S}}
and
U^{0}_{\bar{S}}
all act trivially on the finite free
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}
-module

{\mathcal{V}}_{w^{P}_{0}\lambda_{\bar{S}}}/\varpi^{m}
, so we can pull this factor outside all the functors being applied in (
2.2.2
).
∎

Lemma 2.2.16
.

Let
\tau\in\Hom(F^{+}_{\bar{v}},E)

and let

{\mathcal{K}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}}:=\ker\left({\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}}\to{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{-w_{0,n}\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}c}}}\right)

be the kernel of the evaluation at identity map. For any
m\geq 1
,
we have
(\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},n})^{m}({\mathcal{K}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}}/\varpi^{m})=0
.

Proof.

Since
{\mathcal{K}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}}
is the evaluation at
{\mathcal{O}}
of an algebraic representation of
\GL_{n}/{\mathcal{O}}
, it has a decomposition into weight spaces for the diagonal torus
\mathrm{T}_{n}/{\mathcal{O}}
. The parabolic subgroup
\mathrm{P}_{n,n}
corresponds to the set of simple roots
I=\Delta\backslash(e_{n}-e_{n+1})
. It follows from
[
Cab84
, Proposition 4.1]
that the weights which show up in
{\mathcal{K}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}}
are the weights
\mu
of
V_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}}
such that
\mu-w_{0,2n}\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}
contains a positive multiple of
(e_{n}-e_{n+1})
in its decomposition into simple roots. This condition corresponds to the rescaled action of
\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},n}
on the
\mu
-weight space acting as multiplication by a positive power of
\tilde{\tau}(\varpi_{\tilde{v}})
.
∎

The same result holds for the cohomology of
the Borel–Serre boundary, with the same proof.

Proposition 2.2.17
.

Given a weight

\tilde{\lambda}
and a subset
\bar{S}\subseteq\bar{S}_{p}
,
let
\tilde{\lambda}^{\bar{S}}
be defined as in Proposition
2.2.15
.
For any integer
m\geq 1

there is a natural
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{T}
-equivariant isomorphism

\pi^{\ord}_{\partial}(\widetilde{K}^{\bar{S}},\tilde{\lambda},m)\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}\pi^{\ord}_{\partial}(\widetilde{K}^{\bar{S}},\tilde{\lambda}^{\bar{S}},m)\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{w^{P}_{0}\lambda_{\bar{S}}}/\varpi^{m}

in
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\Delta^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}}}_{\bar{S}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
.

2.2.18.
A variant with dual cofficients

We will also make use of a variant of
P
-ordinary Hida theory which can be applied with dual coefficient systems. It is formulated in terms of the ordinary parts
\ord^{\vee}
,
\ord_{0}^{\vee}
defined using the Hecke action of
\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},n}^{-1}
on invariants under
\overline{U}^{1}_{\bar{v}}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}
respectively, where
\overline{U}^{1}_{\bar{v}}
is the block-strictly-
lower
-triangular part of the parahoric
{\mathcal{P}}_{\bar{v}}
. We will fix
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}
and start with a representation
\pi
of the inverse monoid
(\widetilde{\Delta}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}})^{-1}=\coprod_{\nu\in X_{Q_{\bar{v}}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\nu(\varpi_{\bar{v}})^{-1}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}
. Set
K_{\bar{v}}:={\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\cap G(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})
.

Lemma 2.2.19
.

Let
\pi\in D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}((\widetilde{\Delta}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}})^{-1},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
. There is a natural isomorphism

R\Gamma(K_{\bar{v}},\ord^{\vee}R\Gamma(\overline{U}^{1}_{\bar{v}},\pi))\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}\ord_{0}^{\vee}R\Gamma\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}},\pi\right)

in
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(K_{\bar{v}}[\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},n}^{\pm 1}]/K_{\bar{v}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
under which the action of
[K_{\bar{v}}\nu(\varpi_{\bar{v}})^{-1}K_{\bar{v}}]\in{\mathcal{H}}((\Delta^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}})^{-1},K_{\bar{v}})
on the left hand side matches with the action of
[{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\nu(\varpi_{\bar{v}})^{-1}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}]
on the right hand side.

Proof.

Conjugation by
\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},n}^{-1}w^{P}_{0}
sends
\overline{U}^{1}_{\bar{v}}
to
U^{0}_{\bar{v}}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}
to the parahoric
\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}^{w^{P}_{0}}_{\bar{v}}
corresponding to the standard parabolic with Levi subgroup
Q_{\bar{v}}^{w^{P}_{0}}\cap G(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})
. It moreover sends
(\widetilde{\Delta}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}})^{-1}
to
\widetilde{\Delta}^{\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}^{w^{P}_{0}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}}
.

Conjugation by
\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},n}^{-1}w^{P}_{0}
then identifies
R\Gamma(K_{\bar{v}},\ord^{\vee}R\Gamma(\overline{U}^{1}_{\bar{v}},\pi))
and
\ord_{0}^{\vee}R\Gamma\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}},\pi\right)
with
R\Gamma(K^{w^{P}_{0}}_{\bar{v}},\ord R\Gamma({U}^{0}_{\bar{v}},\pi^{\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},n}^{-1}w^{P}_{0}}))
and
\ord_{0}R\Gamma\left(\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}^{w^{P}_{0}}_{\bar{v}},\pi^{\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},n}^{-1}w^{P}_{0}}\right)
respectively, where
\pi^{\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},n}^{-1}w^{P}_{0}}\in D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\widetilde{\Delta}^{\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}^{w^{P}_{0}}_{\bar{v}}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
denotes
\pi
with the action of
x\in\widetilde{\Delta}^{\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}^{w^{P}_{0}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}}
given by the action of
(w^{P}_{0})^{-1}\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},n}x\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},n}^{-1}w^{P}_{0}\in(\widetilde{\Delta}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}})^{-1}
. Applying Lemma
2.2.12
now gives the desired result. ∎

An independence of weight statement for dual coefficients can be proved by following the proof of Proposition
2.2.15
, using the short exact sequence

0\to{\mathcal{V}}_{w^{P}_{0}\lambda_{\bar{S}}}^{\vee}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{ev}^{\vee}}{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}}}^{\vee}\to{\mathcal{K}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}}}^{\vee}\to 0

and the topological nilpotence of
\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},n}^{-1}
on
{\mathcal{K}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}}}^{\vee}
.

2.3.
New ingredients for degree shifting

2.3.1.
A computation of
P
-ordinary parts

In this section, we compute the
P
-ordinary part of a
parabolic induction from
G
to
\widetilde{G}
, in the same
spirit as the computation of ordinary parts
in
[
ACC+18
, §5.3]
. Our calculations here
are purely local. The global application is to the
boundary of the Borel–Serre compactification of the locally
symmetric spaces for
\widetilde{G}
and it is
carried out in §
4
.

Fix a prime
\bar{v}
of
F^{+}
dividing
p
and let
L:=F^{+}_{\bar{v}}
,
a
p
-adic field (with ring of integers
{\mathcal{O}}_{L}
and uniformiser

\varpi_{L}
). In this section, we let
\mathrm{G}/{\mathcal{O}}_{L}
be a
split connected reductive group with split maximal torus
\mathrm{T}\subset\mathrm{G}
.
Write
\mathrm{W}:=W(\mathrm{G},\mathrm{T})
for the Weyl group, and fix
a Borel subgroup
\mathrm{B}
containing
\mathrm{T}
and a parabolic subgroup

\mathrm{B}\subset\mathrm{P}\subset\mathrm{G}
with Levi decomposition

\mathrm{P}=\mathrm{M}\ltimes\mathrm{U}
. The Weyl group
\mathrm{W}_{\mathrm{P}}

of
\mathrm{M}
can be identified with a subgroup of
\mathrm{W}
. We denote by

\mathrm{W}^{\mathrm{P}}\subset\mathrm{W}
the subset of minimal length representatives
of
\mathrm{W}_{P}\backslash\mathrm{W}
. We denote the length of an element
w\in\mathrm{W}

by
\ell(w)\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
.

Recall from
[
BT65
, Cor. 5.20]
the (generalised)
Bruhat decomposition

\mathrm{G}(L)=\bigsqcup_{w\in\mathrm{W}^{\mathrm{P}}}\mathrm{P}(L)w\mathrm{B}(L).

Denote by
{{}^{\mathrm{P}}\mathrm{W}^{\mathrm{P}}}
the intersection
\mathrm{W}^{\mathrm{P}}\cap(\mathrm{W}^{\mathrm{P}})^{-1}
. This is a set of minimal length representatives
for the double cosets
\mathrm{W}_{\mathrm{P}}\backslash\mathrm{W}/\mathrm{W}_{\mathrm{P}}
,
cf.
[
DM91
, Lemma 3.2.2]
.

Lemma 2.3.2
.

We have a set-theoretic decomposition

\mathrm{G}(L)=\coprod_{w\in{{}^{\mathrm{P}}\mathrm{W}^{\mathrm{P}}}}\mathrm{P}(L)w\mathrm{P}(L).

The
closure relations (for the
p
-adic topology) are given by the Bruhat
ordering

\overline{\mathrm{P}(L)w\mathrm{P}(L)}=\coprod_{w^{\prime}\leq w\in{{}^{\mathrm{P}}\mathrm{W}^{\mathrm{P}}}}\mathrm{P}(L)w^{\prime}\mathrm{P}(L).

Moreover, if
\Omega\subset{{}^{\mathrm{P}}\mathrm{W}^{\mathrm{P}}}
is an upper subset
16
16

16

This means that,
if
w\in\Omega
and
w^{\prime}\in{{}^{\mathrm{P}}\mathrm{W}^{\mathrm{P}}}
satisfies
w^{\prime}\geq w
, then
w^{\prime}\in\Omega
.
,
then
\mathrm{P}(L)\Omega\mathrm{P}(L)
is open in
\mathrm{G}(L)
.

Proof.

See
[
Hau18
, Lemma 2.1.2]
.
∎

We are interested in the parabolic induction functor

\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathrm{P}(L)}^{\mathrm{G}(L)}:D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{P}(L),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\to D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{G}(L),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}).

This functor is exact and preserves injectives. We define several
functors related to it.

For
w\in{{}^{\mathrm{P}}\mathrm{W}^{\mathrm{P}}}
, define
S_{w}:=\mathrm{P}(L)w\mathrm{P}(L)
and

S_{w}^{\circ}:=\mathrm{P}(L)w\mathrm{M}(L)\mathrm{U}^{0}
, where

\mathrm{U}^{0}:=\mathrm{U}({\mathcal{O}}_{L})
. The subset
S_{w}^{\circ}\subset\mathrm{G}(L)
is invariant under left multiplication by
\mathrm{P}(L)
and right multiplication by inverses of elements in
\mathrm{M}(L)^{+}\ltimes\mathrm{U}^{0}
, where
\mathrm{M}(L)^{+}=\{m\in\mathrm{M}(L):m\mathrm{U}^{0}m^{-1}\subset\mathrm{U}^{0}\}
(this means that functions with support in
S_{w}^{\circ}
are stable under right translation by
\mathrm{M}(L)^{+}\ltimes\mathrm{U}^{0}
).

For any
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
, we define

\mathrm{G}_{\geq i}:=\bigsqcup_{\ell(w)\geq i}S_{w},

which is an open subset of
\mathrm{G}(L)
by Lemma
2.3.2
,
and which is invariant under left and right multiplication by

\mathrm{P}(L)
. For any
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
, we define
a functor

I_{\geq i}:\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{P}(L),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\to\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{P}(L),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})

by sending
\pi\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{P}(L),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
to

\begin{split}I_{\geq i}(\pi)=\{f:\mathrm{G}_{\geq i}\to\pi\mid f\text{ locally constant,}&\text{ of compact support modulo }\mathrm{P}(L),\\
&\forall p\in\mathrm{P}(L),g\in\mathrm{G}_{\geq i},f(pg)=pf(g)\},\end{split}

where
\mathrm{P}(L)
acts by right translation. For
w\in{{}^{\mathrm{P}}\mathrm{W}^{\mathrm{P}}}
, we define a functor

I_{w}:\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{P}(L),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\to\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{P}(L),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})

by sending
\pi\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{P}(L),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
to

\begin{split}I_{w}(\pi)=\{f:S_{w}\to\pi\mid f\text{ locally constant,}&\text{ of compact support modulo }\mathrm{P}(L),\\
&\forall p\in\mathrm{P}(L),g\in S_{w},f(pg)=pf(g)\},\end{split}

where again
\mathrm{P}(L)
acts by right translation.
Finally, for
w\in{{}^{\mathrm{P}}\mathrm{W}^{\mathrm{P}}}
, we also define a functor

I^{\circ}_{w}:\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{P}(L),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\to\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{M}(L)^{+}\ltimes U^{0},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})

by defining
I^{\circ}_{w}(\pi)\subset I_{w}(\pi)
to be the subset
of functions with support in
S^{\circ}_{w}
.

Proposition 2.3.3
.

(1)

We have
I_{\geq 0}=\mathrm{Res}^{\mathrm{G}(L)}_{\mathrm{P}(L)}\circ\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathrm{P}(L)}^{\mathrm{G}(L)}
.

(2)

Each functor
I_{\geq i}
,
I_{w}
and
I^{\circ}_{w}
is exact.

(3)

For each
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
and
\pi\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{P}(L),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
,
there is a functorial short exact sequence

0\to I_{\geq i+1}(\pi)\to I_{\geq i}(\pi)\to\bigoplus_{\ell(w)=i}I_{w}(\pi)\to 0.

Proof.

The first part follows from the definition of parabolic induction and
from the fact that
\mathrm{P}(L)\backslash\mathrm{G}(L)
is compact. The second part
follows from the fact that the natural map
\mathrm{G}(L)\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{P}(L)\backslash\mathrm{G}(L)
admits a continuous section,
which can be deduced from
[
Jan03
, Part II, §1.10]

and
[
Hau16
, Lemma 2.1.1]
.
The third part follows
from
[
Hau18
, Lemma 2.2.1]
, noting that the length function
\ell

is strictly monotonic for the Bruhat order.
∎

We deduce that, for any
\pi\in D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{P}(L),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
,
there is a functorial distinguished triangle

(2.3.1)

I_{\geq i+1}(\pi)\to I_{\geq i}(\pi)\to\bigoplus_{\ell(w)=i}I_{w}(\pi)\to I_{\geq i+1}(\pi)[1]

in
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{P}(L),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
.

Proposition 2.3.4
.

Let
\pi\in D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{P}(L),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})

and let
{\mathcal{V}}
be a finite free
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}
-module equipped with a smooth representation of an open submonoid
\Delta^{+}\subset\mathrm{M}(L)
containing an open subgroup
\mathrm{K}\subset\mathrm{M}({\mathcal{O}}_{L})
.
For any
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
and
j\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, the sequence

0\to R^{j}\Gamma\left(\mathrm{K}\ltimes\mathrm{U}^{0},{\mathcal{V}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}}I_{\geq i+1}(\pi)\right)\to R^{j}\Gamma\left(\mathrm{K}\ltimes\mathrm{U}^{0},{\mathcal{V}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}}I_{\geq i}(\pi)\right)

\to\bigoplus_{\ell(w)=i}R^{j}\Gamma\left(\mathrm{K}\ltimes\mathrm{U}^{0},{\mathcal{V}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}}I_{w}(\pi)\right)\to 0

associated to (
2.3.1
) is an exact sequence of
{\mathcal{H}}(\Delta^{+},\mathrm{K})
-modules.

Proof.

The distinguished triangle (
2.3.1
) gives a distinguished triangle

{\mathcal{V}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}}I_{\geq i+1}(\pi)\to{\mathcal{V}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}}I_{\geq i}(\pi)\to\bigoplus_{\ell(w)=i}{\mathcal{V}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}}I_{w}(\pi)\to{\mathcal{V}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}}I_{\geq i+1}(\pi)[1]

in
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\Delta^{+}\ltimes\mathrm{U}^{0},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
, and taking cohomology gives us the desired sequence of
{\mathcal{H}}(\Delta^{+},\mathrm{K})
-modules. It remains to check exactness, for which we can forget the Hecke action and just consider
{\mathcal{V}}
as a representation of
\mathrm{K}
. We consider
decompositions
\mathrm{G}_{\geq i}=U_{1}\sqcup U_{2}

into open and closed subsets that are
\mathrm{P}(L)
-invariant
on the left and
\mathrm{P}({\mathcal{O}}_{L})
-invariant on the right, and
such that
U_{1}\subset\mathrm{G}_{\geq i+1}
. Any such
decomposition induces a functorial decomposition

I_{\geq i}(\pi)=I_{U_{1}}(\pi)\oplus I_{U_{2}}(\pi)

in the category
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{K}\ltimes\mathrm{U}^{0},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
,
where
I_{U_{1}}
denotes functions with support in
U_{1}
,
and similarly for
U_{2}
(we can also tensor this decomposition with
{\mathcal{V}}
). In particular, for any

\pi\in D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{P}(L),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
, the associated
morphism

R^{j}\Gamma\left(\mathrm{K}\ltimes\mathrm{U}^{0},{\mathcal{V}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}}I_{U_{1}}(\pi)\right)\to R^{j}\Gamma\left(\mathrm{K}\ltimes\mathrm{U}^{0},{\mathcal{V}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}}I_{\geq i}(\pi)\right)

of
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}
-modules is injective. Lemma
2.3.5

below implies that
I_{\geq i+1}
can be written as a filtered direct limit of
of functors of the form
I_{U_{1}}
. Since the tensor product
{\mathcal{V}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}}\
and
the functor
R^{j}\Gamma(\mathrm{K}\ltimes\mathrm{U}^{0},\ )
commute with filtered
direct limits
17
17

17

Since
\mathrm{K}\ltimes\mathrm{U}^{0}
is compact,
a filtered direct limit of injective objects in
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{K}\ltimes\mathrm{U}^{0},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})

is again an injective object in
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{K}\ltimes\mathrm{U}^{0},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
,
cf.
[
Eme10b
, Prop. 2.1.3]
.
, it follows that the morphism

R^{j}\Gamma\left(\mathrm{K}\ltimes\mathrm{U}^{0},{\mathcal{V}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}}I_{\geq i+1}(\pi)\right)\to R^{j}\Gamma\left(\mathrm{K}\ltimes\mathrm{U}^{0},{\mathcal{V}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}}I_{\geq i}(\pi)\right)

is injective. Since the injectivity applies for any
j\in\mathbb{Z}
,
the long exact sequence of cohomology groups attached
to the distinguished triangle (
2.3.1
) (tensored with
{\mathcal{V}}
) gives
the statement of the proposition.
∎

Lemma 2.3.5
.

For any
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
, there exist decompositions

\mathrm{G}_{\geq i}=U^{m}_{1}\sqcup U^{m}_{2}
into
open and closed
subsets, indexed by
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
, that are
\mathrm{P}(L)
-invariant
on the left and
\mathrm{P}({\mathcal{O}}_{L})
-invariant on the right, such that

\mathrm{G}_{\geq i+1}=\bigcup_{m\geq 1}U^{m}_{1}

(in particular, each
U^{m}_{1}
is a subset of
\mathrm{G}_{\geq i+1}
).

Proof.

Let
\overline{S}_{w}:=\overline{\mathrm{P}(L)w\mathrm{P}(L)}
,
a closed subset of
\mathrm{G}(L)
. We claim that it
is enough to find, for each

w\in{{}^{\mathrm{P}}\mathrm{W}^{\mathrm{P}}}
with
\ell(w)=i
,
decompositions
\mathrm{G}(L)=U_{w,1}^{m}\sqcup U_{w,2}^{m}
into
open and closed subsets, indexed
by
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
, that are
\mathrm{P}(L)
-invariant
on the left and
\mathrm{P}({\mathcal{O}}_{L})
-invariant on the right,
such that

\overline{S}_{w}=\bigcap_{m\geq 1}U^{m}_{w,2}.

Indeed, once we have found such decompositions, we set

U_{2}^{m}:=\left(\bigcup_{\ell(w)=i}U^{m}_{w,2}\right)\cap\mathrm{G}_{\geq i},

which is open and closed in
\mathrm{G}_{\geq i}

because we have taken a finite union. The
U^{m}_{2}
and their
complements induce decompositions of
\mathrm{G}_{\geq i}

with the desired properties.

We now describe how to find
the decompositions for each
w
.
Set
\mathscr{F}\ell:=\mathrm{P}\backslash\mathrm{G}
;
this is a projective variety defined over
{\mathcal{O}}_{L}
.
The closed subset
\mathrm{P}(L)\backslash\overline{S}_{w}\subset\mathrm{P}(L)\backslash\mathrm{G}(L)

can be identified with the
{\mathcal{O}}_{L}
-points of
a closed Schubert subvariety
\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{w}

of
\mathscr{F}\ell
.
Let
\varpi_{L}
be a uniformiser of
{\mathcal{O}}_{L}
.
For each
m\geq 1
, we consider the subset
\overline{V}_{m}\subset\mathscr{F}\ell({\mathcal{O}}_{L}/\varpi^{m}_{L})
consisting of the
points that satisfy modulo
\varpi^{m}_{L}

the equations for
\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{w}
.
The preimage
V_{m}\subset\mathrm{P}(L)\backslash\mathrm{G}(L)
of
\overline{V}_{m}

is an open and closed subset that contains
\mathrm{P}(L)\backslash\overline{S}_{w}
,
and is invariant under multiplication on
the right by the finite index normal subgroup of
\mathrm{P}({\mathcal{O}}_{L})
of elements that
reduce to the identity modulo
\varpi_{L}^{m}
.
We intersect the translates of
V_{m}
by finitely many coset
representatives in
\mathrm{P}({\mathcal{O}}_{L})
to obtain an open and closed
subset
W_{m}\subset\mathrm{P}(L)\backslash\mathrm{G}(L)

that contains
\mathrm{P}(L)\backslash\overline{S}_{w}
,
and is invariant under multiplication on
the right by
\mathrm{P}({\mathcal{O}}_{L})
. Finally, we define

U^{m}_{w,2}
to be the preimage of
W_{m}
in
\mathrm{G}(L)
.
∎

Assume now that
\mathrm{G}=\GL_{2n}/L
and that

\mathrm{P}
is the standard upper-triangular parabolic with Levi
\GL_{n}\times\GL_{n}
.
Write
\tilde{u}_{L}:=\mathrm{diag}(\varpi_{L},\dots,\varpi_{L},1,\dots,1)
, where the uniformiser

\varpi_{L}
of
{\mathcal{O}}_{L}
occurs
n
times on the diagonal. Then

\mathrm{M}(L)^{+}
contains the monoid generated by
\mathrm{M}({\mathcal{O}}_{L})
and
\tilde{u}_{L}
. This means that if
\pi\in D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{M}(L)^{+}\ltimes\mathrm{U}^{0},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
, we can define
R\Gamma\left(\mathrm{U}^{0},\pi\right)\in D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{M}(L)^{+},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
and the localisation (inverting
\tilde{u}_{L}
)
\ord R\Gamma\left(\mathrm{U}^{0},\pi\right)\in D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{M}(L)^{+},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
.

We now compute the complexes
\ord R\Gamma\left(\mathrm{U}^{0},I_{w}(\pi)\right)

in two special cases:
for
w
equal to either the longest element of
{{}^{\mathrm{P}}\mathrm{W}^{\mathrm{P}}}
or to the identity element.

Lemma 2.3.6
.

Let
w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}
be the longest
element of
{{}^{\mathrm{P}}\mathrm{W}^{\mathrm{P}}}
. Then:

(1)

I^{\circ}_{w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}}
takes injectives to
\Gamma(\mathrm{U}^{0},\ )
-acyclics.

(2)

Let
\pi\in D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{P}(L),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
. Then there is a natural isomorphism

\ord R\Gamma(\mathrm{U}^{0},I^{\circ}_{w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}}(\pi))\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}\ord R\Gamma(\mathrm{U}^{0},I_{w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}}(\pi)).

Proof.

Since
w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}
is the longest element in
{{}^{\mathrm{P}}\mathrm{W}^{\mathrm{P}}}
, it normalises

\mathrm{M}(L)
and therefore we have
S^{\circ}_{w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}}=\mathrm{P}(L)w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}\mathrm{U}^{0}
.
The proof of
[
ACC+18
, Lemma 5.3.4]
applies verbatim;
for convenience, we reproduce it here.

For the first part, let
\pi\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{P}(L),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})

and fix an
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}
-linear embedding
\pi\hookrightarrow I
, where
I

is an injective
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}
-module. This gives rise to an embedding

\pi\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Ind}_{1}^{\mathrm{P}(L)}I
of smooth

{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}[\mathrm{P}(L)]
-modules. By
[
Eme10b
, Lemma 2.1.10]
,
it suffices to show that
I^{\circ}_{w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}}\left(\mathrm{Ind}_{1}^{\mathrm{P}(L)}I\right)
is
an injective smooth
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}[\mathrm{U}^{0}]
-module. There is a natural

\mathrm{U}^{0}
-equivariant isomorphism

I^{\circ}_{w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}}\left(\mathrm{Ind}_{1}^{\mathrm{P}(L)}I\right)\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}\left(\mathrm{P}(L)w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}\mathrm{U}^{0},I\right),

where
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}\left(\mathrm{P}(L)w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}\mathrm{U}^{0},I\right)
denotes the set of locally constant
I
-valued functions
on
\mathrm{P}(L)w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}\mathrm{U}^{0}
(with the action of
\mathrm{U}^{0}
by right translation).
The isomorphism sends a function
f\in I^{\circ}_{w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}}\left(\mathrm{Ind}_{1}^{\mathrm{P}(L)}I\right)

to
F\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}\left(\mathrm{P}(L)w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}\mathrm{U}^{0},I\right)
given by
F(x):=f(x)(1)
.
Since
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}\left(\mathrm{P}(L)w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}\mathrm{U}^{0},I\right)
is an injective
smooth
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}[\mathrm{U}^{0}]
-module, we conclude.

For the second part, we first define an exact functor

J_{w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}}(\pi):\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{P}(L),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\to\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{M}(L)^{+}\ltimes U^{0},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})

by the formula
J_{w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}}(\pi):=I_{w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}}(\pi)/I^{\circ}_{w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}}(\pi)
.
For each
\pi\in D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{P}(L),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
, we have
a distinguished triangle

\ord R\Gamma(\mathrm{U}^{0},I^{\circ}_{w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}}(\pi))\to\ord R\Gamma(\mathrm{U}^{0},I_{w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}}(\pi))\to\ord R\Gamma(\mathrm{U}^{0},J_{w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}}(\pi))\to\ord R\Gamma(\mathrm{U}^{0},I^{\circ}_{w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}}(\pi))[1].

It is enough to show that, for any
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have

\ord H^{j}(\mathrm{U}^{0},J_{w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}}(\pi))=0.

By direct computation, we see that
\tilde{u}_{L}
acts locally nilpotently
on
J_{w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}}(\pi)
: it shrinks the support of a function in
I_{w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}}(\pi)

towards
S^{\circ}_{w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}}
.
We conclude using the same argument as in
[
Hau16
, Lemma 3.3.1]
.
∎

Lemma 2.3.7
.

Let
w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}
be the longest
element of
{{}^{\mathrm{P}}\mathrm{W}^{\mathrm{P}}}
and let
\pi\in D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{P}(L),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
.
Then there is a natural isomorphism

R\Gamma(\mathrm{U}^{0},I^{\circ}_{w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}}(\pi))\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}\pi^{w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}}

in
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{M}(L)^{+},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
, where
m\in\mathrm{M}(L)^{+}
acts on

\pi^{w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}}
via the action of
w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}m(w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0})^{-1}
on
\pi
.

Proof.

By the first part of Lemma
2.3.6
, it is
enough to show that there is a natural isomorphism

(2.3.2)

\Gamma(\mathrm{U}^{0},I^{\circ}_{w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}}(\pi))\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}\pi^{w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}}

of underived functors. The map sends an
\mathrm{U}^{0}
-invariant function

f:\mathrm{P}(L)w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}\mathrm{U}^{0}\to\pi
to the value
f(w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0})\in\pi
. This
is an isomorphism of
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}
-modules; it remains
to check that (
2.3.2
) is
\mathrm{M}(L)^{+}
-equivariant,
for the Hecke action of
\mathrm{M}(L)^{+}
on the LHS and the action twisted
by
w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}
on the RHS. In other words, we have to check that,
for any
\mathrm{U}^{0}
-invariant function
f:\mathrm{P}(L)w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}\mathrm{U}^{0}\to\pi

and any
m\in\mathrm{M}(L)^{+}
, we have the equality

(2.3.3)

\sum_{\bar{u}\in\mathrm{U}^{0}/m\mathrm{U}^{0}m^{-1}}f(w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}um)=w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}m(w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0})^{-1}f(w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}).

This will hold if and only if the only
\bar{u}\in\mathrm{U}^{0}/m\mathrm{U}^{0}m^{-1}

that contributes to the LHS of (
2.3.3
) is the identity element.
We claim that
f(w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}um)=0
unless
u\in m\mathrm{U}^{0}m^{-1}
. Assume

f(w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}um)\not=0
; then
w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}um\in\mathrm{P}(L)w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}\mathrm{U}^{0}
. We write

w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}um=qw^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}u^{\prime}
with
q\in\mathrm{P}(L)
and
u^{\prime}\in\mathrm{U}^{0}
and we obtain

u=(w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0})^{-1}qw^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}u^{\prime}m^{-1}=((w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0})^{-1}qm^{\prime}w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0})(mu^{\prime}m^{-1}),

with
m^{\prime}:=w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}m^{-1}(w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0})^{-1}\in\mathrm{M}(L)
. But then

((w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0})^{-1}qm^{\prime}w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0})\in(w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0})^{-1}\mathrm{P}(L)w^{\mathrm{P}}_{0}\cap\mathrm{U}^{0}=\{\mathrm{id}\}

and we deduce
u\in m\mathrm{U}^{0}m^{-1}
.
∎

Let
\chi:\mathrm{M}(L)\to{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}
be the character
defined by the formula

\chi(m)=\frac{\mathrm{Nm}_{L/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\det_{L}\left(\mathrm{Ad}(m)|_{\mathrm{Lie}\ U(L)}\right)^{-1}}{|\mathrm{Nm}_{L/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\det_{L}\left(\mathrm{Ad}(m)|_{\mathrm{Lie}\ U(L)}\right)|_{p}}.

Lemma 2.3.8
.

Let
\pi\in D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{M}(L),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
.
Then there is a natural isomorphism

\ord R\Gamma\left(\mathrm{U}^{0},\mathrm{Inf}_{\mathrm{M}(L)^{+}}^{\mathrm{M}(L)^{+}\ltimes\mathrm{U}^{0}}\pi\right)\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}(\chi)\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}}\pi[-\mathrm{rk}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\mathrm{U}^{0}]

in
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{M}(L)^{+},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
.

Proof.

Since
\mathrm{U}^{0}
acts trivially on
\pi
, we have

\ord R\Gamma\left(\mathrm{U}^{0},\mathrm{Inf}_{\mathrm{M}(L)^{+}}^{\mathrm{M}(L)^{+}\ltimes\mathrm{U}^{0}}\pi\right)\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}\pi\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}}\ord R\Gamma\left(\mathrm{U}^{0},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}\right).

By the proof of
[
ACC+18
, Lemma 5.3.7]
, the continuous cohomology groups
of
\mathrm{U}^{0}
vanish below the top degree
\ell:=\mathrm{rk}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\mathrm{U}^{0}
after applying ordinary parts.
It remains to show that

H^{\ell}(\mathrm{U}^{0},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\simeq{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}(\chi)

as a representation of
\mathrm{M}(L)^{+}
. This follows, just like
[
Hau16
, Prop. 3.1.8]
,
from the natural isomorphism in
[
Eme10b
, Prop. 3.5.6]
and
from the explicit description of the corestriction map in
[
Eme10b
, Prop. 3.5.10]
.
∎

Remark 2.3.9
.

The functor
I_{\mathrm{id}}

is the identity on
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{P}(L),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})

and the functor
I^{\circ}_{\mathrm{id}}
is the natural restriction

D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{P}(L),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\to D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{M}(L)^{+}\ltimes\mathrm{U}^{0},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
.
Note also that in this case we have
S^{\circ}_{\mathrm{id}}=S_{\mathrm{id}}
,
so that we have a natural identification

R\Gamma(\mathrm{U}^{0},I^{\circ}_{\mathrm{id}}(\ ))\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}R\Gamma(\mathrm{U}^{0},I_{\mathrm{id}}(\ ))

in
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{M}(L)^{+},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
.
Furthermore, if we start with
\pi\in D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{M}(L),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
, we can identify

\mathrm{Inf}_{\mathrm{M}(L)^{+}}^{\mathrm{M}(L)^{+}\ltimes\mathrm{U}^{0}}\pi

with the functor
I^{\circ}_{\mathrm{id}}
applied to
\mathrm{Inf}_{\mathrm{M}(L)}^{\mathrm{P}(L)}\pi
.

Corollary 2.3.10
.

Let
\pi\in D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{M}(L),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
.
Then there is a natural isomorphism

\ord R\Gamma\left(\mathrm{U}^{0},I_{\mathrm{id}}\left(\mathrm{Inf}_{\mathrm{M}(L)}^{\mathrm{P}(L)}\pi\right)\right)\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}(\chi)\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}}\pi[-\mathrm{rk}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\mathrm{U}^{0}]

in
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{M}(L)^{+},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
.

We now apply our results in the setting of Section
2.2
, so we adopt the notation of that section. We fix a place
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}
and a standard parabolic
Q_{\bar{v}}\subset P_{\bar{v}}
. We apply the results of this section with
\mathrm{G}=\widetilde{G}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}_{\bar{v}}}}
,
\mathrm{P}=P_{\bar{v}}
,
\mathrm{M}=G_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}_{\bar{v}}}}
and
\mathrm{U}=U_{\bar{v}}
. Set
K_{\bar{v}}={\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\cap G(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})
. Note that
\Delta_{\bar{v}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}},+}
is a submonoid of
G(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})^{+}
. The following proposition summarises the key result
of this subsection.

Proposition 2.3.11
.

Let
\pi\in D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(G(F^{+}_{\bar{v}}),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})

and let
{\mathcal{V}}
be a finite free
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}
-module equipped with a smooth representation of
\Delta^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}},+}_{\bar{v}}
with
\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},n}
acting trivially, which we inflate to an action of
\widetilde{\Delta}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v},P}=\Delta^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}},+}_{\bar{v}}\ltimes U^{0}_{\bar{v}}
.
Then

\ord_{0}R^{j}\Gamma\left(K_{\bar{v}}\ltimes U^{0}_{\bar{v}},\mathrm{Ind}_{P(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})}^{\widetilde{G}(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})}\pi\otimes{\mathcal{V}}\right)

admits as
{\mathcal{H}}(\Delta^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}},K_{\bar{v}})
-module subquotients

R^{j}\Gamma(K_{\bar{v}},\pi^{w^{P}_{0}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}})\ \text{and}\ R^{j-\mathrm{rk}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}U^{0}_{\bar{v}}}\Gamma(K_{\bar{v}},\pi\otimes{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}(\chi)\otimes{\mathcal{V}}).

Proof.

By applying the exact functor
\mathrm{ord}_{0}
to the
short exact sequences in Proposition
2.3.4
,
we see that

\ord_{0}R^{j}\Gamma\left(K_{\bar{v}}\ltimes U^{0}_{\bar{v}},\mathrm{Ind}_{P(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})}^{\widetilde{G}(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})}\pi\otimes{\mathcal{V}}\right)

admits as subquotients

\mathrm{ord}_{0}R^{j}\Gamma(K_{\bar{v}}\ltimes U^{0}_{\bar{v}},I_{w}(\pi)\otimes{\mathcal{V}})

for
w=w^{P}_{0}
and for
w=\mathrm{id}
. We have an
isomorphism of functors

\mathrm{ord}_{0}\circ R\Gamma(K_{\bar{v}},\ )\simeq R\Gamma(K_{\bar{v}},\ )\circ\mathrm{ord},

as in §
2.2.11
. By Lemma
2.3.6
and
Lemma
2.3.7
, we have an isomorphism

\ord R\Gamma(U_{\bar{v}}^{0},I_{w^{P}_{0}}(\pi)\otimes{\mathcal{V}})\simeq\ord R\Gamma(U_{\bar{v}}^{0},I_{w^{P}_{0}}(\pi))\otimes{\mathcal{V}}\simeq\pi^{w^{P}_{0}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}.

By Corollary
2.3.10
, we have an isomorphim

\ord R\Gamma(U_{\bar{v}}^{0},I_{\mathrm{id}}(\pi)\otimes{\mathcal{V}})\simeq\ord R\Gamma(U_{\bar{v}}^{0},I_{\mathrm{id}}(\pi))\otimes{\mathcal{V}}\simeq\pi\otimes{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}(\chi)\otimes{\mathcal{V}}[-\mathrm{rk}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}U^{0}].

∎

We have a corollary which will be applied with ‘dual’ coefficients (cf. §
2.2.18
). This can be viewed as a computation of the ordinary part with respect to the opposite parabolic
\overline{P}
applied to a parabolic induction from
P
.

Corollary 2.3.12
.

Let
\pi\in D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(G(F^{+}_{\bar{v}}),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})

and let
{\mathcal{V}}
be a finite free
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}
-module equipped with a smooth representation of
\left(\Delta^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}},+}_{\bar{v}}\right)^{-1}
with
\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},n}^{-1}
acting trivially, which we inflate to an action of
\left(\widetilde{\Delta}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v},P}\right)^{-1}
.
Then

\ord_{0}^{\vee}R^{j}\Gamma\left(K_{\bar{v}}\ltimes\overline{U}^{1}_{\bar{v}},\mathrm{Ind}_{P(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})}^{\widetilde{G}(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})}\pi\otimes{\mathcal{V}}\right)

admits
R^{j}\Gamma(K_{\bar{v}},\pi\otimes{\mathcal{V}})
as a
{\mathcal{H}}(\left(\Delta^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}}\right)^{-1},K_{\bar{v}})
-module subquotient.

Proof.

We deduce the corollary from Proposition
2.3.11
by twisting. Indeed, multiplication by
\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},n}^{-1}w^{P}_{0}
induces an isomorphism

R^{j}\Gamma\left(\mathrm{K}\ltimes\overline{U}^{1}_{\bar{v}},\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathrm{P}(L)}^{\mathrm{G}(L)}\pi\otimes{\mathcal{V}}\right)\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}R^{j}\Gamma\left(\mathrm{K}^{w^{P}_{0}}\ltimes{U}^{0}_{\bar{v}},\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathrm{P}(L)}^{\mathrm{G}(L)}\pi\otimes{\mathcal{V}}^{w^{P}_{0}}\right)

where the action of
{\mathcal{H}}(\left(\Delta^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}},+}_{\bar{v}}\right)^{-1},K_{\bar{v}})
on the left is identified with the action of
{\mathcal{H}}(\Delta^{\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}^{w^{P}_{0}}_{\bar{v}},+}_{\bar{v}},K_{\bar{v}})
on the right by sending
[K_{\bar{v}}\nu(\varpi_{\bar{v}})^{-1}K_{\bar{v}}]
to
[K_{\bar{v}}^{w^{P}_{0}}(-w^{P}_{0}\nu)(\varpi_{\bar{v}})K_{\bar{v}}^{w^{P}_{0}}]
on the right. We recall from the proof of Lemma
2.2.19
that the
\overline{Q}^{w^{P}_{0}}_{\bar{v}}
is the standard parabolic with Levi subgroup
Q_{\bar{v}}^{w^{P}_{0}}\cap G(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})
. Now Proposition
2.3.11
tells us that we have a subquotient
R^{j}\Gamma(K_{\bar{v}}^{w^{P}_{0}},\pi^{w^{P}_{0}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}^{w^{P}_{0}})
, which can be identified with
R^{j}\Gamma(K_{\bar{v}},\pi\otimes{\mathcal{V}})
as a
{\mathcal{H}}(\left(\Delta^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}}\right)^{-1},K_{\bar{v}})
-module.∎

2.3.13.
Parabolic induction and cohomology

We return to the general situation of §
2.3.1
, for a split reductive group
\mathrm{G}
defined over (the ring of integers in) a local field
L
. In addition, we consider a compact Hausdorff
space
X
, equipped with a continuous action of the locally profinite group

\mathrm{P}(L)
. Set
\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}:=\mathrm{K}\cap\mathrm{P}(L)
.
We assume that
X
is a free
\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}
-space,
in the sense of
[
NT16
, Def. 2.23]
.

Denote by
X\times_{\mathrm{P}}\mathrm{G}
the quotient
of
X\times\mathrm{G}(L)
by the right action
(x,g)\cdot p=(xp,p^{-1}g)
of
\mathrm{P}(L)
. Right multiplication by
\mathrm{G}(L)
on itself gives
X\times_{\mathrm{P}}\mathrm{G}
a right action of
\mathrm{G}(L)
.

The Iwasawa decomposition
\mathrm{G}(L)=\mathrm{K}\mathrm{P}(L)

implies
that there exists a
\mathrm{K}
-equivariant homeomorphism

X\times_{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}}\mathrm{K}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}X\times_{\mathrm{P}}\mathrm{G}.

The LHS is visibly a compact Hausdorff space.
Therefore, it makes sense to consider

R\Gamma\left(\mathrm{X}\times_{\mathrm{P}}\mathrm{G},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}\right)
as
an element in
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{G}(L),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
.

Lemma 2.3.14
.

We have a natural isomorphism in

D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{G}(L),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})

(2.3.4)

R\Gamma\left(\mathrm{X}\times_{\mathrm{P}}\mathrm{G},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}\right)\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathrm{P}(L)}^{\mathrm{G}(L)}\ R\Gamma(X,{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}).

Proof.

First, we explain how to construct a natural map
from the LHS of (
2.3.4
)
to the RHS, then we observe that it is enough
to prove the map is an isomorphism after restriction to

D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{K},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
, then we prove the latter.

Consider the
\mathrm{P}(L)
-equivariant map
X\to\mathrm{X}\times_{\mathrm{P}}\mathrm{G}

given by
x\mapsto(x,1)
. We get an induced
morphism

R\Gamma(X\times_{\mathrm{P}}\mathrm{G},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\to R\Gamma(X,{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})

in
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{P}(L),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
,
which induces a morphism

R\Gamma(X\times_{\mathrm{P}}\mathrm{G},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\to\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathrm{P}(L)}^{\mathrm{G}(L)}\ R\Gamma(X,{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})

in
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{G}(L),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
,
by Frobenius reciprocity for smooth representations (also
using that
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathrm{P}(L)}^{\mathrm{G}(L)}
is an exact functor).

The same morphism can be constructed if we work with

\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}\subset\mathrm{K}
replacing
\mathrm{P}\subset\mathrm{G}
.
We now observe that we have a commutative
diagram in
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{K},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})

(2.3.5)

\textstyle{\mathrm{Res}^{\mathrm{G}(L)}_{\mathrm{K}}\circ R\Gamma\left(X\times_{\mathrm{P}}\mathrm{G},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Res}^{\mathrm{G}(L)}_{\mathrm{K}}\circ\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathrm{P}(L)}^{\mathrm{G}(L)}\ R\Gamma(X,{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{R\Gamma(X\times_{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}}\mathrm{K},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}}^{\mathrm{K}}\ R\Gamma(X,{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})}

whose vertical arrows are isomorphisms by the Iwasawa
decomposition.

We have a diagram with Cartesian outer square

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 22.23268pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ }$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 47.36462pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 47.67885pt\raise-54.99997pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 85.71869pt\raise-53.27774pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\phi_{1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 76.18666pt\raise-54.99997pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 51.03508pt\raise-37.44441pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\phi}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 22.23268pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-5.5pt\raise-62.99997pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ }$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 46.23268pt\raise-62.99997pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X/{\mathrm{K}}_{\mathrm{P}}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 107.37848pt\raise-62.99997pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ }$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

The map
\phi
is given by
x\mapsto(x,1)
; the top triangle is not commutative,
but the bottom one is.
Since
X\to X/\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}
is a
\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}
-torsor, the same holds true
for
X\times\mathrm{K}\to X\times_{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}}\mathrm{K}
. This also implies that

X\times_{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}}\mathrm{K}\to X/\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}
is a
\mathrm{K}
-torsor.
By
[
NT16
, Lemma 2.24]
,
we have inverse equivalences of categories
(\varphi_{2}^{*},\varphi_{2,*}^{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}})

between
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(X\times_{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}}\mathrm{K})
and
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}\times\mathrm{K}}(X\times\mathrm{K})
.
The analogous statements are true for
\varphi_{1}
,
\phi_{1}
and

\phi_{2}
.

The bottom horizontal arrow in (
2.3.5
)
is induced by
\phi^{*}
. Let
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}{\mathcal{I}}^{\bullet}
be an injective
resolution in
\mathrm{Sh}(X/\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}})
. We claim that
\phi^{*}

induces by Frobenius reciprocity a term-wise
\mathrm{K}
-equivariant
isomorphism of complexes

(2.3.6)

\Gamma(X\times_{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}}\mathrm{K},\phi_{2}^{*}{\mathcal{I}}^{\bullet})\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}}^{\mathrm{K}}\ \Gamma(X,\phi_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{I}}^{\bullet}).

To see the claim, note that
\phi_{2}^{*}=\varphi^{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}}_{2,*}\varphi_{2}^{*}\phi_{2}^{*}=\varphi_{2,*}^{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}}\varphi_{1}^{*}\phi_{1}^{*}

and we can identify
\phi^{*}
with the restriction to the identity of a function in

\Gamma\left(X\times_{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}}\mathrm{K},\varphi_{2,*}^{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}}\varphi_{1}^{*}(\phi_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{I}}^{\bullet})\right)\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}}^{\mathrm{K}}\ \Gamma(X,\phi_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{I}}^{\bullet}).

The map in (
2.3.6
) is precisely the horizontal map in the
bottom row of (
2.3.5
), which also implies
that the horizontal map in the top row is an isomorphism.
∎

We will also make use of the following lemma about the interaction
between smooth induction and restriction.

Lemma 2.3.15
.

Let
\pi_{1}\in\mathrm{Rep}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
and
\pi_{2}\in\mathrm{Rep}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{K},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})

with the property that
\pi_{2}
takes values in a finite free
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}
-module. There is a natural isomorphism

\left(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}}^{\mathrm{K}}\pi_{1}\right)\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}}\pi_{2}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}}^{\mathrm{K}}\left(\pi_{1}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}}\mathrm{Res}_{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}}^{\mathrm{K}}\pi_{2}\right)

in
\mathrm{Rep}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{K},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
.

Proof.

Since
\pi_{2}
is a finite
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}
-module, there exists a compact open normal subgroup
U\subset\mathrm{K}
that acts trivially on
\pi_{2}
.
One can define a natural map

\Phi:\left(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}}^{\mathrm{K}}\pi_{1}\right)\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}}\pi_{2}\to\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}}^{\mathrm{K}}\left(\pi_{1}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}}\mathrm{Res}_{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}}^{\mathrm{K}}\pi_{2}\right).

by extending
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}
-linearly from the formula

f\otimes v\mapsto(g\mapsto f(g)\otimes\pi_{2}(g)v).

One can check that
\Phi
lands in
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}}^{\mathrm{K}}\left(\pi_{1}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}}\mathrm{Res}_{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}}^{\mathrm{K}}\pi_{2}\right)
and is a
\mathrm{K}
-equivariant homomorphism. To see that
\Phi
does indeed take values in smooth functions, note that there is a compact open subgroup
U_{f}
that stabilises
f
. Then

f(gu)\otimes\pi_{2}(gu)v=f(g)\otimes\pi_{2}(g)v\ \forall u\in U_{f}\cap U\ \mathrm{and}\ g\in\mathrm{K},

which shows that each
\Phi(f\otimes v)
is a smooth function on
\mathrm{K}
.

It remains to show that
\Phi
is an isomorphism. For
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
, we can find a decreasing sequence of compact open normal subgroups
U_{m}
of
\mathrm{K}
with
U_{1}=U
and that form a basis of neighbourhoods of identity in
\mathrm{K}
. It is enough to prove that
\Phi
induces an isomorphism on the level of
U_{m}
-invariants for each
m
. Note that, for each
m
, the set of double cosets
\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}\backslash\mathrm{K}/U_{m}
is finite, and for any smooth representation
\pi
of
\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}
, we have an isomorphism

\left(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}}^{\mathrm{K}}\pi\right)^{U_{m}}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}\bigoplus_{\gamma\in\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}\backslash\mathrm{K}/U_{m}}\pi^{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}\cap\gamma U_{m}\gamma^{-1}},f\mapsto(f(\gamma))_{\gamma\in\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}\backslash\mathrm{K}/U_{m}}.

Note also that each
\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}\cap\gamma U_{m}\gamma^{-1}=\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}\cap U_{m}
acts trivially on
\pi_{2}
.
We obtain a commutative diagram

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 70.334pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-41.91466pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}}^{\mathrm{K}}\pi_{1}\right)^{U_{m}}\otimes\pi_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 78.54688pt\raise-6.15pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\cong}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 94.334pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-25.45667pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 94.334pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bigoplus_{\gamma\in\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}\backslash\mathrm{K}/U_{m}}(\pi_{1})^{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}\cap\gamma U_{m}\gamma^{-1}}\otimes\pi_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 168.34375pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-70.334pt\raise-34.36893pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern
