3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}}^{\mathrm{K}}\left(\pi_{1}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}}\mathrm{Res}_{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}}^{\mathrm{K}}\pi_{2}\right)\right)^{U_{m}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 78.54688pt\raise-40.51892pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\cong}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 94.334pt\raise-34.36893pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 94.334pt\raise-34.36893pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bigoplus_{\gamma\in\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}\backslash\mathrm{K}/U_{m}}(\pi_{1})^{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}\cap\gamma U_{m}\gamma^{-1}}\otimes\pi_{2}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

where the left vertical map is
\Phi
and the
right vertical map is
\mathrm{id}\otimes\gamma
in the component corresponding to
\gamma
. This is visibly an isomorphism.

∎

2.3.16.
A computation of group cohomology

Keep the notation from the previous subsection.
Inside
\mathrm{K}=\mathrm{M}({\mathcal{O}}_{L})
, consider the congruence
subgroups
\mathrm{K}_{m}:=\{k\in\mathrm{K}\mid k\equiv\mathrm{id}\pmod{\varpi_{L}^{m}}\}

indexed by
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
. The group
\mathrm{U}^{0}
is equipped
with the adjoint action of
\mathrm{K}
, so that we can view the
continuous cohomology
R\Gamma(\mathrm{U}^{0},\ )

as a functor
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{K}\ltimes\mathrm{U}^{0},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\to D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{K},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
.

Lemma 2.3.17
.

For any
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
, there
exists
M=M(m)\geq m
such that

R\Gamma(\mathrm{U}^{0},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\simeq\bigoplus^{\mathrm{rk}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\mathrm{U}^{0}}_{i=0}H^{i}(\mathrm{U}^{0},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})[-i].

as an object in
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{K}_{M},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
. Moreover,
each
H^{i}(\mathrm{U}^{0},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
is non-zero and equipped with
the trivial action of
\mathrm{K}_{M}
.

Proof.

By
[
Eme10b
, Prop. 2.1.11]
, an injective
smooth representation of
\mathrm{K}\ltimes\mathrm{U}^{0}
is also injective
as a representation of
\mathrm{U}^{0}
. Therefore, if we apply the forgetful
functor to
R\Gamma(\mathrm{U}^{0},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
in order to view it as an
object of
D^{+}({\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
,
we obtain the continuous group cohomology of
\mathrm{U}^{0}
with coefficients in
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}
.
If we forget the
\mathrm{K}
-action,
\mathrm{U}^{0}
is a free
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-module.
Therefore, we can compute its continuous group cohomology via
a Koszul complex, using
[
BMS18
, Lemma 7.3, part (ii)]
, for example. We see
that all the differentials in the Koszul complex vanish, so we obtain

R\Gamma(\mathrm{U}^{0},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\simeq\bigoplus_{i}H^{i}(\mathrm{U}^{0},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})[-i].

as an object in
D_{\mathrm{perf}}({\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
, the full subcategory of

D^{+}({\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
consisting of perfect complexes. Moreover, we can identify
each
H^{i}(\mathrm{U}^{0},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
with
\wedge^{i}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\mathrm{U}^{0}
, which
shows that the cohomology groups are non-zero precisely in the range

[0,\mathrm{rk}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\mathrm{U}^{0}]
.

Let
D_{\mathrm{perf}}(\mathrm{K}_{M},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
be the full subcategory
of
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathrm{K}_{M},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
whose essential image
under the forgetful functor is
D_{\mathrm{perf}}({\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
. It is enough
to show that, for every object
A
of
D_{\mathrm{perf}}(\mathrm{K},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
,
there exists
M\geq m
such that the restriction
A_{M}
of
A

to
D_{\mathrm{perf}}(\mathrm{K}_{M},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})

is isomorphic to the constant object
B_{M}
of
D_{\mathrm{perf}}(\mathrm{K}_{M},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})

corresponding to the image
B
of
A
in
D_{\mathrm{perf}}({\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
.
In turn, this would follow from showing that the natural map

(2.3.7)

\mathrm{colim}_{M}\left(\Hom_{D_{\mathrm{perf}}(\mathrm{K}_{M},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})}(B_{M},A_{M})\right)\to\Hom_{D_{\mathrm{perf}}({\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})}(B,A)

is a bijection. Indeed, on the RHS we have the identity morphism, which
must correspond to a morphism on the LHS for some
M\geq m
.
We can check that this morphism in
D_{\mathrm{perf}}(\mathrm{K}_{M},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})

is an isomorphism after applying the forgetful functor to
D_{\mathrm{perf}}({\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
.

The objects of
D_{\mathrm{perf}}({\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
are precisely the dualizable objects of
D^{+}({\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
and the dual is given by applying

\Hom_{{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}}(\ ,{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
. We have the adjunction (cf.
[
Sta13
,
Lemma 07VI
]
)

\Hom_{D_{\mathrm{perf}}({\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})}(B,A)=\Hom_{D_{\mathrm{perf}}({\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})}({\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m},B^{\vee}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}A)=H^{0}(B^{\vee}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}A).

Now, we have an adjunction

\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{\mathrm{perf}}(\mathrm{K}_{M},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})}\left(B_{M},A_{M}\right)=\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{\mathrm{perf}}({\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})}\left(B,R\Gamma(\mathrm{K}_{M},A_{M})\right)

and we have identified the right hand side with
H^{0}(B^{\vee}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}R\Gamma(\mathrm{K}_{M},A_{M}))
. Fixing a strictly perfect complex representing
B
, and equipping it with the trivial
\mathrm{K}_{M}
action to get a complex representing
B_{M}
, we identify
B^{\vee}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}R\Gamma(\mathrm{K}_{M},A_{M})=R\Gamma(\mathrm{K}_{M},B_{M}^{\vee}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}A_{M})
.

Therefore, the bijectivity in (
2.3.7
)
reduces to the statement that
\mathrm{colim}_{M}H^{0}(\mathrm{K}_{M},B_{M}^{\vee}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}A_{M})
(degree
0
hypercohomology)
is equal to the degree
0
cohomology of the underlying complex of
B^{\vee}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}A
.
This follows from the fact that
B^{\vee}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}A

is a complex of smooth representations of
\mathrm{K}
.
∎

We will apply the preceding lemma when proving the crucial Proposition
4.2.6
. In that proof, we will also need the following consequence of the Artin–Rees lemma:

Lemma 2.3.18
.

Let

N
be a finite
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-module and

M
be a subquotient of
N
. For any positive integer

m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
,
there exists an integer
m^{\prime}\geq m

such that
M/p^{m}M
is a subquotient of

N/p^{m^{\prime}}N
.

Proof.

If
M
is a quotient of
N
, then
we may take
m^{\prime}=m
. Therefore, it suffices to
consider the case when
M\hookrightarrow N

is a subobject. By the Artin–Rees lemma, cf.
[
AM16
, Cor. 10.10]
,
there exists
k\gg 0
such that

p^{m+k}N\cap M=p^{m}(p^{k}N\cap M)\subseteq p^{m}M.

Set
m^{\prime}:=m+k
. We then have an inclusion

M/(p^{m^{\prime}}N\cap M)\hookrightarrow N/p^{m^{\prime}}N

and a surjection
M/(p^{m^{\prime}}N\cap M)\twoheadrightarrow M/p^{m}M
.
∎

3.
Determinants,
P
-ordinary representations and deformation rings.

3.1.
The
P
-ordinary condition on the Galois side

We place ourselves in the setting of §
2.1.11
, so we have a CM field
F
, unitary group
\widetilde{G}
, etc. We fix a standard parabolic
Q_{\bar{v}}\subset P_{F^{+}_{\bar{v}}}
. After fixing a place
v|\bar{v}
, this corresponds under
\iota_{v}
to a standard parabolic
P_{n_{1},\ldots,n_{t}}\subset\GL_{2n}
, with
(n_{1},\ldots,n_{t})
a partition of
2n
refining
(n,n)
. Recall that
Q_{\bar{v}}=M_{\bar{v}}\ltimes N_{\bar{v}}
is a Levi decomposition for
Q_{\bar{v}}
. We use the notation of §
2.1.13
, so we have a parahoric subgroup
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}
associated with
Q_{\bar{v}}
.

For
1\leq k\leq t
, we define a cocharacter
\nu_{k}\in X_{Q_{\bar{v}}}
by

\nu_{k}(\varpi_{\bar{v}}):=\iota_{v}^{-1}\mathrm{diag}(\varpi_{v},\ldots,\varpi_{v},1,\ldots,1)\in\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{v}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}},

where there are
n_{1}+\cdots+n_{k}
entries equal to
\varpi
, and denote the Hecke operator
[{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\nu_{k}(\varpi_{\bar{v}}){\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}]\in{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{v}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}},{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}})
by
\widetilde{U}_{v}^{k}
. It follows from Lemma
2.1.15
that

{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{v}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}},{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}})\cong{\mathbb{Z}}[\widetilde{U}_{v}^{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}_{v}^{t-1},(\widetilde{U}_{v}^{t})^{\pm 1}].

If
\tilde{\lambda}\in({\mathbb{Z}}_{+}^{2n})^{\Hom(F^{+},\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p})}
is a dominant weight for
\widetilde{G}
and
\sigma
is a smooth
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
-representation of
\widetilde{G}(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})
, we define the
\tilde{\lambda}
-
rescaled
action of
{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{v}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}},{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}})
on
\sigma^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}
to be given by multiplying the usual double coset operator action of
[{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}g{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}]
by
\tilde{\alpha}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}}(g)^{-1}
(cf. Lemma
2.1.17
).

Definition 3.1.1
.

Let
\pi
be a cuspidal automorphic representation of
\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+}})
and fix an isomorphism
\iota:\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}{\mathbb{C}}
. Let
\tilde{\lambda}\in({\mathbb{Z}}_{+}^{2n})^{\Hom(F^{+},\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p})}
be a dominant weight for
\widetilde{G}
. We say that
\pi
is
\iota
-
Q_{\bar{v}}
-ordinary of weight
\tilde{\lambda}
if
\pi
is
\iota V_{\tilde{\lambda}}^{\vee}
-cohomological and the
\tilde{\lambda}
-rescaled Hecke operators
\{\widetilde{U}_{v}^{k}:1\leq k\leq t\}
have a simultaneous eigenvector with
p
-adic unit eigenvalues in
\iota^{-1}\pi^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}
.

If
\pi
is
\iota
-
Q_{\bar{v}}
-ordinary of weight
\tilde{\lambda}
, we define the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}
-ordinary subspace
of
\iota^{-1}\pi_{\bar{v}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}
to be the largest
{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{\Delta}_{\bar{v}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}},{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}})
-submodule on which the rescaled operators
\widetilde{U}_{v}^{k}
have only
p
-adic unit eigenvalues for
1\leq k\leq t
.

The goal of this section is to establish the following result, generalising
[
Ger19
, Corollary 2.33]
,
[
Tho15
, Theorem 2.4]
.

Theorem 3.1.2
.

Suppose that
\pi
is a cuspidal automorphic representation of
\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+}})
,
\iota
is an isomorphism
\iota:\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}{\mathbb{C}}
and
\bar{v}
is a
p
-adic place of
F^{+}

such that
\pi
is
\iota
-
Q_{\bar{v}}
-ordinary of weight
\tilde{\lambda}
.

Then we have the following conclusions:

(1)

The associated
p
-adic Galois representation

r_{\iota}(\pi):G_{F}\to\GL_{2n}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p})
satisfies

(3.1.1)

r_{\iota}(\pi)|_{G_{F_{v}}}\simeq\begin{pmatrix}r_{1}(\pi)&*&\cdots&*\\
0&r_{2}(\pi)&\cdots&*\\
0&0&\ddots&*\\
0&0&\cdots&r_{t}(\pi)\end{pmatrix},

where
r_{j}(\pi):G_{F_{v}}\to\GL_{n_{j}}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p})
is a crystalline representation for each
j=1,2,\ldots,t
.

(2)

The
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}
-ordinary subspace of
\iota^{-1}\pi_{\bar{v}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}
is one-dimensional.

(3)

For each embedding
\tau:F_{v}\hookrightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
, the

\tau
-Hodge–Tate weights of the
r_{j}(\pi)
are given by decomposing
\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau,2n}<\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau,2n-1}+1<\dots<\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau,1}+2n-1
according to the partition
(n_{1},\ldots,n_{t})
.

(4)

The determinants
\det r_{j}(\pi)
are given by the formulas:

•

\prod_{j=1}^{k}\det r_{j}(\pi)({\operatorname{Art}}_{F_{v}}(u))=\prod_{i=1}^{n_{1}+\cdots+n_{k}}\prod_{\tau:F_{v}\hookrightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}}\tau(u)^{-\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau,2n-i+1}-i+1}
for
u\in{\mathcal{O}}_{F_{v}}^{\times}
.

•

\prod_{j=1}^{k}\det r_{j}(\pi)({\operatorname{Art}}_{F_{v}}(\varpi_{v}))
is equal to
\epsilon_{p}^{\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}+\cdots+n_{k}}(1-i)}({\operatorname{Art}}_{F_{v}}(\varpi_{v}))
times the eigenvalue of
\widetilde{U}_{v}^{k}
on the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}
-ordinary subspace.

Before giving the proof, we first establish a preliminary result.

Lemma 3.1.3
.

Assume that

r:G_{F_{v}}\to\GL_{m}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p})
is a semi-stable Galois representation.
Let
v_{1}\leq v_{2}\leq\dots\leq v_{m}
denote the valuations of the eigenvalues
of the geometric Frobenius acting on
\mathrm{WD}(r)
, and,
for each
\tau:F\hookrightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
, let
h_{\tau,1}<h_{\tau,2}<\dots<h_{\tau,m}
denote the
\tau
-Hodge–Tate weights of
r
. Then

(3.1.2)

\sum_{i=1}^{j}v_{i}\geq\frac{1}{e_{v}}\sum_{i=1}^{j}\sum_{\tau}h_{\tau,i}

for any
0\leq j\leq m
, where
e_{v}
is the ramification degree of
F/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.

Furthermore, if we have an equality

\sum_{i=1}^{j}v_{\sigma(i)}=\frac{1}{e_{v}}\sum_{i=1}^{j}\sum_{\tau}h_{\tau,i}

for some
1\leq j\leq m-1
and permutation
\sigma\in S_{n}
, then

r\simeq\begin{pmatrix}r_{1}&*\\
0&r_{2}\end{pmatrix}

with
r_{1}:G_{F_{v}}\to\GL_{j}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p})

and
r_{2}:G_{F_{v}}\to\GL_{m-j}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p})
.
The representation
r_{1}
has

\tau
-Hodge–Tate weights equal to
h_{\tau,1}<\dots<h_{\tau,j}
for each
\tau
,
the eigenvalues of the geometric
Frobenius acting on
\mathrm{WD}(r_{1})
have valuations
v_{1}\leq v_{2}\leq\dots\leq v_{j}

and we have
v_{j}<v_{j+1}
.

Proof.

The first part follows from
[
HKV20
, Lemma 6.4.1]
.
For the second part, if equality holds for some permutation
\sigma
, then
by the first part and by the inequalities on the
v_{i}
, equality must also
hold for the identity permutation. Furthermore,
we have
\sum_{i=1}^{j}v_{i}=\sum_{i=1}^{j}v_{\sigma(i)}
.
If
\{\sigma(1),\dots,\sigma(j)\}\not=\{1,\dots,j\}
, then

\sigma(i)\geq j+1
for some
i\leq j
,
and so we must have
v_{j}=v_{j+1}
.
We show that
v_{j}=v_{j+1}
is impossible. As in
loc. cit.
, using
the inequality in (
3.1.2
)
for
j+1
and
j-1
,
we deduce that the inequality for
j-1
is actually an equality.
Moreover, this implies that
h_{\tau,j}=h_{\tau,j+1}
for
all
\tau
, which is a contradiction. We deduce that

\{\sigma(1),\dots,\sigma(j)\}=\{1,\dots,j\}

and that
v_{j}<v_{j+1}
. The last paragraph in
the proof of
[
HKV20
, Lemma 6.4.1]
gives a
j
-dimensional
sub-
G_{F_{v}}
-representation
r_{1}
of
r
with

\tau
-Hodge–Tate weights equal to
h_{\tau,1}<\dots<h_{\tau,j}

for each
\tau
and such that the eigenvalues of the geometric
Frobenius acting on
\mathrm{WD}(r_{1})
are
v_{1}\leq v_{2}\leq\dots\leq v_{j}
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3.1.2
.

We use
\iota_{{v}}
to identify
\widetilde{G}(F_{\bar{v}}^{+})
with
\GL_{2n}(F_{v})
. Since
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}
contains the Iwahori subgroup of
\widetilde{G}(F_{\bar{v}}^{+})
,
\iota^{-1}\pi_{\bar{v}}
is a subquotient of a normalised induction
\sigma=\mathrm{n-Ind}_{\mathrm{B}_{2n}(F_{v})}^{\GL_{2n}(F_{v})}(\chi_{1}\otimes\chi_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes\chi_{2n})
with each
\chi_{i}:F_{v}^{\times}\to\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}^{\times}
an unramified character.

We claim that, up to reordering the
\chi_{i}
, for each
1\leq k\leq t
the
p
-adic numbers
\prod_{i=1}^{n_{1}+\cdots n_{k}}\chi_{i}(\varpi_{v})
and
\delta_{\mathrm{B}_{2n}}(\nu_{k}(\varpi_{\bar{v}}))^{1/2}\alpha_{\tilde{\lambda}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}(\nu_{k}(\varpi_{\bar{v}}))
differ by a
p
-adic unit.

By the proof of
[
Tho12
, Prop. 5.4]
, we have an isomorphism
\sigma^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}\cong(J_{Q_{\bar{v}}}(\sigma))^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\cap M_{\bar{v}}(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})}
(normalized Jacquet module) with the
\tilde{\lambda}
-rescaled action of the Hecke operator
\widetilde{U}_{v}^{k}
on the left given by the action of
\alpha_{\tilde{\lambda}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}(\nu_{k}(\varpi_{\bar{v}}))^{-1}\delta_{Q_{\bar{v}}}^{-1/2}(\nu_{k}(\varpi_{\bar{v}}))\nu_{k}(\varpi_{\bar{v}})
on the right. We can further compose with the injection
(J_{Q_{\bar{v}}}(\sigma))^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\cap M_{\bar{v}}(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})}\hookrightarrow(J_{\mathrm{B}_{2n}}(\sigma))^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\cap\mathrm{T}_{2n}(F_{{v}})}
, with
\widetilde{U}_{v}^{k}
acting by
\alpha_{\tilde{\lambda}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}(\nu_{k}(\varpi_{\bar{v}}))^{-1}\delta_{\mathrm{B}_{2n}}^{-1/2}(\nu_{k}(\varpi_{\bar{v}}))\nu_{k}(\varpi_{\bar{v}})
on the target. Note that since
\nu_{k}(\varpi_{\bar{v}})
is central in
M_{\bar{v}}
, we have
\delta_{\mathrm{B}_{2n}}(\nu_{k}(\varpi_{\bar{v}}))=\delta_{Q_{\bar{v}}}(\nu_{k}(\varpi_{\bar{v}}))
. We have
J_{\mathrm{B}_{2n}}(\sigma)^{ss}=\bigoplus_{w\in W}\chi_{w(1)}\otimes\cdots\otimes\chi_{w(2n)}
. Choosing
w
so that the unit-eigenvalue eigenvector for the
\widetilde{U}_{v}^{k}
contributes to the summand indexed by
w
gives the desired reordering of the
\chi_{i}
.

By Theorem
2.1.19
, since
\tilde{\lambda}
is dominant,
the
\tau
-Hodge–Tate weights of
r_{\iota}(\pi)|_{G_{F_{v}}}

are equal to

\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau,2n}<\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau,2n-1}+1<\dots<\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau,1}+2n-1
. In addition, by making part (3)
of Theorem
2.1.19
explicit at
v
,
the eigenvalues of the geometric Frobenius acting on
\mathrm{WD}(r_{\iota}(\pi)|_{G_{F_{v}}})

are
q_{v}^{\frac{2n-1}{2}}\chi_{i}(\varpi_{v})
. Our claim about the
p
-adic valuations of the
\chi_{i}(\varpi_{v})
implies that the
p
-adic numbers

\prod_{i=1}^{n_{1}+\cdots+n_{k}}\chi_{i}(\varpi_{v})\ \mathrm{and}

q_{v}^{-\langle\nu_{k},\rho_{\mathrm{B}_{2n}}\rangle}\prod_{\tau:F_{v}\hookrightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}}\tau(\varpi_{v})^{\langle\nu_{k},w_{0}^{\widetilde{G}}\lambda_{\tau}\rangle}

differ by a
p
-adic unit. The latter term has the same
p
-adic valuation as

\prod_{i=1}^{n_{1}+\cdots+n_{k}}q_{v}^{\frac{1-2n}{2}}\prod_{\tau:F_{v}\hookrightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}}\tau(\varpi_{v})^{\lambda_{\tau,2n-i+1}+i-1}.

In turn, this implies that the inequality in (
3.1.2
)
is an equality for
j=n_{1}+\cdots+n_{k}
for each
k
. We conclude that
r_{\iota}(\pi)|_{G_{F_{v}}}

has the desired shape by Lemma
3.1.3
.

To show that the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}
-ordinary subspace of
\iota^{-1}\pi_{\bar{v}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}
is one-dimensional, it suffices to prove the analogous statement for
\sigma^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}\cong(J_{Q_{\bar{v}}}(\sigma))^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\cap M_{\bar{v}}(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})}
. By the geometric lemma
[
BZ77
, Lemma 2.12]
, we have

(3.1.3)

J_{Q_{\bar{v}}}(\sigma)^{ss}=\bigoplus_{w\in[W/W_{Q_{\bar{v}}}]}\left(\mathrm{n-Ind}^{M_{\bar{v}}}_{\mathrm{B}_{2n}\cap M_{\bar{v}}}\chi_{w(1)}\otimes\cdots\otimes\chi_{w(2n)}\right).

The notation
w\in[W/W_{Q_{\bar{v}}}]
means that we take the minimal length representative
w\in W
for each coset. Explicitly, this is given by permutations
w\in S_{2n}
which are order-preserving on each of the subsets
\{1,\ldots,n_{1}\},\{n_{1}+1,\ldots,n_{1}+n_{2}\},\ldots,\{n_{1}+\cdots+n_{t-1}+1,\ldots,2n\}
. Each summand in (
3.1.3
) has a one-dimensional space of invariants under the maximal compact subgroup
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\cap M_{\bar{v}}(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})
. On the invariants of the summand indexed by
w
, for each
1\leq k\leq t
the Hecke operator
\widetilde{U}_{v}^{k}
acts by the scalar

\alpha_{k,w}:=\alpha_{\tilde{\lambda}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}(\nu_{k}(\varpi_{\bar{v}}))^{-1}\delta_{\mathrm{B}_{2n}}^{-1/2}(\nu_{k}(\varpi_{\bar{v}}))\prod_{i=1}^{n_{1}+\cdots+n_{k}}\chi_{w(i)}(\varpi_{v}).

We know that
\alpha_{k,1}
is a
p
-adic unit for all
k
. The inequality in the conclusion of Lemma
3.1.3
shows that the
v_{p}(\alpha_{1,w})>0
if
w
does not preserve
\{1,\ldots,n_{1}\}
. For a fixed
w
, inducting on
k
and repeating this argument shows that
v_{p}(\alpha_{k,w})=0
for all
k
if and only if
w=1
. This shows that the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}
-ordinary subspace of
\iota^{-1}\pi_{\bar{v}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}
is one-dimensional.

The statements about the Hodge–Tate weights and determinant of the
r_{i}(\pi)
follow from the Lemma and the identifications above.

It remains to see that each
r_{i}(\pi)
is crystalline. We write the argument for
r_{1}(\pi)
, it is similar for the other factors. Let
w_{i}=v_{p}(\chi_{i}(\varpi_{v}))
for
i=1,\dots,2n
.
The eigenvalues of a geometric Frobenius on

\mathrm{WD}(r_{1}(\pi))
are
q_{v}^{\frac{2n-1}{2}}\chi_{i}(\varpi_{v})
for
i=1,\dots,n_{1}
. Lemma
3.1.3
implies that, up to reordering the
\chi_{i}

for
i=1,\dots,n_{1}
, we may assume that
w_{1}\leq w_{2}\dots\leq w_{n_{1}}<w_{n_{1}+1}
.
If
r_{1}(\pi)
is not crystalline, then by the Bernstein–Zelevinsky classification there exists some
i\in 2,\dots n_{1}

such that
\chi_{i-1}=\chi_{i}\cdot|\ |
and

\pi_{\bar{v}}
is a subquotient of the normalised parabolic induction

\sigma^{\prime}=\mathrm{n-Ind}_{Q^{\prime}(F_{v})}^{\GL_{2n}(F_{v})}(\chi_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes\chi_{i-2}\otimes\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(\chi_{i})\otimes\chi_{i+1}\otimes\dots\otimes\chi_{2n}),

for an appropriate standard parabolic subgroup
Q^{\prime}=M^{\prime}N^{\prime}\subset Q_{\bar{v}}
, where
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(\chi_{i})
denotes a twist of the Steinberg representation. We let
\sigma^{\prime}_{0}=\chi_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes\chi_{i-2}\otimes\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(\chi_{i})\otimes\chi_{i+1}\otimes\dots\otimes\chi_{2n}
.

Applying the geometric lemma again, we have

J_{Q_{\bar{v}}}(\sigma^{\prime})^{ss}=\bigoplus_{w\in[W_{Q^{\prime}}\backslash W/W_{Q_{\bar{v}}}]}\left(\mathrm{n-Ind}^{M_{\bar{v}}}_{w^{-1}Q^{\prime}w\cap M_{\bar{v}}}\left(J_{M^{\prime}\cap wQ_{\bar{v}}w^{-1}}\sigma^{\prime}_{0}\right)^{w}\right)^{ss}

As in the proof that the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}
-ordinary subspace is one-dimensional, it follows from considering valuations that the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}
-ordinary subspace of
\iota^{-1}\pi_{\bar{v}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}\cong(J_{Q_{\bar{v}}}(\sigma^{\prime}))^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\cap M_{\bar{v}}(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})}
can only contribute to the
w=1
term in this decomposition.
We deduce that

\left(\mathrm{n-Ind}^{M_{\bar{v}}}_{Q^{\prime}\cap M_{\bar{v}}}\left(\sigma^{\prime}_{0}\right)\right)^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\cap M_{\bar{v}}(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})}\neq 0,

but this is impossible because
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\cap M_{\bar{v}}(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})
is maximal compact and
\sigma_{0}^{\prime}
has a Steinberg factor.
∎

3.2.
Determinants

In this section we prove a key proposition which will be combined with Theorem
3.1.2
to pass information about local–global compatibility for Galois representations with coefficients in
p
-torsion free Hecke algebras for
\widetilde{G}
to Galois representations with coefficients in torsion Hecke algebras for
G
. The initial set-up is as follows: recall our coefficient field
E\supset{\mathcal{O}}\twoheadrightarrow k
and assume we have an absolutely irreducible continuous
representation

\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}:G_{F}\to\GL_{n}(k)

together with a continuous lift

\rho_{\mathfrak{m}}:G_{F}\to\GL_{n}(A)

with
coefficients in
A\in\operatorname{CNL}_{{\mathcal{O}}}
.

We moreover have a finite flat

{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra
\widetilde{A}\in\operatorname{CNL}_{{\mathcal{O}}}
with
\widetilde{A}[1/p]=\prod_{i=1}^{r}K_{i}
a
product of fields, equipped with a surjective map

\widetilde{A}\twoheadrightarrow A
. Extending
E
if necessary, we can assume
that every
K_{i}
has residue field
k
(indeed, we can also assume that each
K_{i}
is equal to
E
, but we won’t need to do this).
We suppose we have a continuous representation

\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}=\prod\widetilde{\rho}_{i,\mathfrak{m}}:G_{F}\to\prod\GL_{2n}(K_{i})=\GL_{2n}(\widetilde{A}[1/p])

such that the associated determinant of
G_{F}
(in the sense of

[
Che14
]
) arises from a continuous
\widetilde{A}
-valued determinant

D_{\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}}:\widetilde{A}[G_{F}]\to\widetilde{A}
. This is equivalent to the characteristic polynomials of
\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}(g)
having coefficients in
\widetilde{A}
for all
g\in G_{F}
.

We recall from
[
Che14
]
the important notion of a
Cayley–Hamilton
determinant:
a determinant
D:R\to A
is Cayley–Hamilton if the characteristic polynomial
\chi(r,t)=D(t-r)\in A[t]
vanishes when evaluated at
t=r
for all
r\in R
.

The determinant
D_{\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}}
factors through a
Cayley–Hamilton determinant (which we also denote by

D_{\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}}
) of

\widetilde{B}:=\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}(\widetilde{A}[G_{F}])\subset M_{2n}(\widetilde{A}[1/p])
. Since each element of
\widetilde{B}
has characteristic polynomial with coefficients in
\widetilde{A}
,
\widetilde{B}
is
integral over
\widetilde{A}
.
We also assume that we have a factorisation of
A
-valued determinants of
A[G_{F}]

D_{\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}}\otimes_{\widetilde{A}}A=D_{\rho_{\mathfrak{m}}}D_{\rho_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\vee,c}(1-2n)}.

Fix a place
v|p
of
F
. Extending
E
if necessary, we may assume that
every irreducible constituent of the local representation

\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}|_{G_{F_{v}}}
is absolutely irreducible.
We assume that, for each
i
, we have an
n
-dimensional

G_{F_{v}}
-sub-representation
(\widetilde{\rho}_{i,\mathfrak{m}}^{0},V_{i}^{0})\subset(\widetilde{\rho}_{i,\mathfrak{m}},K_{i}^{2n})
with quotient
(\widetilde{\rho}_{i,\mathfrak{m}}^{1},V_{i}^{1})
,
Finally, we assume that:

(1)

the isomorphism classes of the irreducible constituents of

\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}|_{G_{F_{v}}}
are disjoint from those of

\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\vee,c}(1-2n)|_{G_{F_{v}}}
;

(2)

for all
i
the isomorphism classes of the irreducible
constituents of the residual representation

\overline{(\widetilde{\rho}_{i,\mathfrak{m}}^{0})}
coincide with those of

\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}|_{G_{F_{v}}}
.

As in the proof of
[
Che14
, Thm. 2.22]
, we are going to use a basic
fact about idempotents, cf.
[
Bou98
, Chapter III, §4, Exercise 5 (a)]
.

Lemma 3.2.1
.

Let
S
be a Henselian local ring,
R
a (not necessarily commutative)

S
-algebra which is integral over
S
. Let
I
be a two-sided ideal of

R
. Every idempotent in
R/I
lifts to an idempotent in

R
.

Proof.

The statement can be reduced to the case
of a finite
S
-algebra
R
generated by
one element, in particular with
R
commutative (take the subalgebra of
R
generated by an arbitrary lift of the idempotent in
R/I
).
In this case the statement is clear, since

R
is then a product of finitely many Henselian local rings.
∎

The following lemma constructs an element
\widetilde{e}\in M_{2n}(\widetilde{A}[1/p])
which is an idempotent projection onto
\prod_{i}V_{i}^{0}
, lies in
\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}(\widetilde{A}[G_{F_{v}}])
and moroever cuts out the first factor in the product decomposition
D_{\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}}\otimes_{\widetilde{A}}k=D_{\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}}D_{\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\vee,c}(1-2n)}
of residual determinants. Roughly speaking, this is possible because our assumptions on the irreducible constituents of
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}|_{G_{F_{v}}}
mean the global factors
D_{\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}}
,
D_{\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\vee,c}(1-2n)}
can be distinguished locally at
v
.

Lemma 3.2.2
.

(1)

The natural map
k[G_{F}]\xrightarrow{\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}\times\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\vee,c}(1-2n)}M_{n}(k)\times M_{n}(k)
induces an isomorphism
\iota:k[G_{F}]/\ker(D_{\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}}\otimes_{\widetilde{A}}k)\cong M_{n}(k)\times M_{n}(k)
.

(2)

The idempotent
e=\iota^{-1}(1_{M_{n}(k)},0)
is contained
in the image of
k[G_{F_{v}}]
.

(3)

There is a lift
\widetilde{e}\in\widetilde{B}
of
e
which is in the image
\widetilde{B}_{v}\subset\widetilde{B}
of

\widetilde{A}[G_{F_{v}}]
. (Note that
D_{\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}}\otimes_{\widetilde{A}}k
factors through
\widetilde{B}\otimes_{\widetilde{A}}k
, so
k[G_{F}]/\ker(D_{\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}}\otimes_{\widetilde{A}}k)
is a quotient of
\widetilde{B}
.)

(4)

Choose
\widetilde{e}
as in the previous part. For each
i\in\{1,\ldots,r\}
let
\pi_{i}
denote the projection

\widetilde{A}[1/p]^{2n}\to V_{i}^{1}
. Thinking of
\widetilde{e}
as an element of

\End(\widetilde{A}[1/p]^{2n})
, we have
\pi_{i}\circ\widetilde{e}=0
and the image of
\widetilde{e}
in
\End(V_{i}^{0})
is the identity.
In other words,
\widetilde{e}
is an idempotent projection onto

\prod_{i}V_{i}^{0}
.

Proof.

The first claim follows from our assumption that
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}
and

\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\vee,c}(1-2n)
are distinct and absolutely irreducible, by

[
Che14
, Thm. 2.16]
.

For the second claim we consider the subalgebra
B=\iota(k[G_{F_{v}}])
of

M_{n}(k)\times M_{n}(k)
. The semisimple quotient
B/\mathrm{Rad}(B)

contains the idempotent
\overline{e}_{v}
which acts as the identity on each
irreducible
constituent of
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}|_{G_{F_{v}}}
and as zero on each irreducible
constituent of
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\vee,c}(1-2n)|_{G_{F_{v}}}
; here we are using
our
assumption that these two collections of irreducible constituents are
disjoint. Since
\mathrm{Rad}(B)
is nilpotent,
we can lift
\overline{e}_{v}
to an idempotent
e_{v}\in B
. By
considering
the composition series of
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}|_{G_{F_{v}}}
, we see that
e_{v}
maps
to an idempotent unit (i.e. the identity) under the first projection
to
M_{n}(k)
. Considering the composition series of

\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\vee,c}(1-2n)
,
e_{v}
maps to a nilpotent idempotent
(i.e. zero) under the
second projection, so we have
e_{v}=e
.

For the third claim, we know that
\widetilde{B}_{v}
is
integral over
\widetilde{A}
(since it is a subalgebra of
\widetilde{B}
). We apply Prop.
3.2.1

with
S=\widetilde{A}
, which is Henselian as it is a local ring and
finite over
{\mathcal{O}}
, and
R=\widetilde{B}_{v}
. We deduce that the idempotent
e
lifts to an idempotent
\widetilde{e}
in
\widetilde{B}_{v}
.

Now we come to the fourth claim. Fix an index
i
. We choose a
G_{F}
-stable

{\mathcal{O}}_{K_{i}}
-lattice
T\subset K_{i}^{2n}
. We have a short exact sequence

0\to V_{i}^{0}\cap T\to T\to\pi_{i}(T)\to 0

of

{\mathcal{O}}_{K_{i}}[G_{F_{v}}]
-modules (in particular the submodule
V_{i}^{0}\cap T
is
stable under
\widetilde{e}
). The image of
\widetilde{e}
in

\End_{k}(\pi_{i}(T)\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{K_{i}}}k)
is equal to zero, since

\widetilde{e}
lifts the idempotent which acts as zero on each irreducible
constituent of the
G_{F_{v}}
representation on

\pi_{i}(T)\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{K_{i}}}k
(by assumption
these coincide with the irreducible constituents of

\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\vee,c}(1-2n)|_{G_{F_{v}}}
). We deduce that the image of

\widetilde{e}
in
\End_{{\mathcal{O}}_{K_{i}}}(\pi_{i}(T))
is equal to zero, since
it is an idempotent with image in
\varpi_{K_{i}}\pi_{i}(T)
. This shows that

\pi_{i}\circ\widetilde{e}=0
, as claimed. Similarly, the image of

\widetilde{e}
in
\End_{k}((V_{i}^{0}\cap T)\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{K_{i}}}k)
is an
idempotent isomorphism, hence the identity, and so the image of

\widetilde{e}
in
\End_{{\mathcal{O}}_{K_{i}}}(V_{i}^{0}\cap T)
itself is an
idempotent isomorphism, hence equal to the identity.
∎

In the preceding lemma, we constructed an idempotent
\widetilde{e}
, compatible with both the local ‘
P
-ordinary’ decomposition and the residual global decomposition
D_{\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}}\otimes_{\widetilde{A}}k=D_{\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}}D_{\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\vee,c}(1-2n)}
. Following
[
BC09
, Theorem 1.4.4]
(and its generalization
[
Che14
, Theorem 2.22]
), we can now use the idempotents
e_{1}:=\widetilde{e},e_{2}:=1-\widetilde{e}
to equip
\widetilde{B}
with a
generalized matrix algebra
structure compatible with the determinant
D_{\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}}
. We now explain in detail what this means (see also
[
ANT20
, §2]
). By
[
Che14
, Lem. 2.4]
, we have determinants of dimension
n

\displaystyle D_{\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}},i}:e_{i}\widetilde{B}e_{i}

\displaystyle\to\widetilde{A}

\displaystyle x

\displaystyle\mapsto D_{\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}}(x+1-e_{i})

for
i=1,2
, with
(D_{\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}},1}\otimes_{\widetilde{A}}k)(e_{1}xe_{1})=D_{\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}}(x)
and
(D_{\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}},2}\otimes_{\widetilde{A}}k)(e_{2}xe_{2})=D_{\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\vee,c}(1-2n)}(x)
. The
e_{i}\widetilde{B}e_{i}
are equipped with
\widetilde{A}
-algebra isomorphisms

(3.2.1)

\psi_{i}:e_{i}\widetilde{B}e_{i}\cong M_{n}(\widetilde{A})

with
\det\circ\psi_{i}=D_{\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}},i}
. Moreover, the map
(\psi_{1},\psi_{2}):e_{1}\widetilde{B}e_{1}\oplus e_{2}\widetilde{B}e_{2}\to M_{n}(\widetilde{A})\times M_{n}(\widetilde{A})
lifts
\iota:k[G_{F}]/\ker(D_{\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}}\otimes_{\widetilde{A}}k)\cong M_{n}(k)\times M_{n}(k)
.

Define idempotents
E_{i}\in e_{i}\widetilde{B}e_{i}
by asking for
\psi_{i}(E_{i})
to be the matrix with a one in the top left entry and zeroes elsewhere. We can now define
{\mathcal{A}}
-submodules
{\mathcal{A}}_{i,j}:=E_{i}\widetilde{B}E_{j}\subset\widetilde{B}
. For each
1\leq i,j,k\leq 2
we write
{\mathcal{A}}_{i,j}{\mathcal{A}}_{j,k}
for the
\widetilde{A}
-module generated by products
xy
for
x\in{\mathcal{A}}_{i,j},y\in{\mathcal{A}}_{j,k}
. It is a submodule of
{\mathcal{A}}_{i,k}
. The
\psi_{i}
induce isomorphisms
{\mathcal{A}}_{i,i}\cong\widetilde{A}
, so we have a multiplication
{\mathcal{A}}_{i,j}\otimes_{\widetilde{A}}{\mathcal{A}}_{j,i}\to\widetilde{A}
and we identify
{\mathcal{A}}_{i,j}{\mathcal{A}}_{j,i}
with an ideal of
\widetilde{A}
. If
i\neq j
, then
e_{i}\widetilde{B}e_{j}\widetilde{B}e_{i}
maps to
\ker(D_{\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}}\otimes_{\widetilde{A}}k)
, and therefore
{\mathcal{A}}_{i,j}{\mathcal{A}}_{j,i}\subset\mathfrak{m}_{\widetilde{A}}
.

Putting everything together gives us isomorphisms

\displaystyle\widetilde{B}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}\begin{pmatrix}e_{1}\widetilde{B}e_{1}&e_{1}\widetilde{B}e_{2}\\
e_{2}\widetilde{B}e_{1}&e_{2}\widetilde{B}e_{2}\end{pmatrix}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}\begin{pmatrix}M_{n}(\widetilde{A})&M_{n}({\mathcal{A}}_{1,2})\\
M_{n}({\mathcal{A}}_{2,1})&M_{n}(\widetilde{A})\end{pmatrix}

with multiplication of the last matrix algebra given by using matrix multiplication and the maps
{\mathcal{A}}_{i,j}\otimes_{\widetilde{A}}{\mathcal{A}}_{j,i}\to\widetilde{A}
. The reducibility of the determinant
D_{\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}}
is reflected in the GMA structure. More precisely, we apply
[
ANT20
, Proposition 2.5]
in our setting (a mild generalization of
[
BC09
, Proposition 1.5.1]
) to deduce:

Proposition 3.2.3
.

An ideal
J\subset\widetilde{A}
contains
{\mathcal{A}}_{1,2}{\mathcal{A}}_{2,1}
if and only if there are determinants
D_{1},D_{2}:\widetilde{B}\otimes_{\widetilde{A}}\widetilde{A}/J\to\widetilde{A}/J
such that

\displaystyle D_{\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}}\otimes_{\widetilde{A}}\widetilde{A}/J

\displaystyle=D_{1}D_{2},

\displaystyle D_{1}\otimes_{\widetilde{A}/J}k

\displaystyle=D_{\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}},

\displaystyle\text{and }D_{2}\otimes_{\widetilde{A}/J}k

\displaystyle=D_{\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\vee,c}(1-2n)}.

If this property holds,
D_{1}
and
D_{2}
are uniquely determined and satisfy
\ker(D_{\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}}\otimes_{\widetilde{A}}\widetilde{A}/J)\subset\ker(D_{i})
for
i=1,2
.

Proof.

This follows immediately from
[
ANT20
, Proposition 2.5]
. Note that the property ‘(COM)’ in
[
BC09
, Lemma 1.3.5]
(which follows from the fact that the trace of a determinant satisfies the identity
\mathrm{Tr}(xy)=\mathrm{Tr}(yx)
) means that the ideals
{\mathcal{A}}_{1,2}{\mathcal{A}}_{2,1}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{2,1}{\mathcal{A}}_{1,2}
are equal.
∎

We can now state and prove the key proposition of this section, where we combine the GMA structure defined above with the fact that our idempotent
\widetilde{e}
was chosen to have good local properties at
v
.

Proposition 3.2.4
.

Choose an idempotent
\widetilde{e}\in\widetilde{B_{v}}
as in the third
part of Lemma
3.2.2
.

(1)

The map

\displaystyle A[G_{F}]

\displaystyle\to\widetilde{e}\widetilde{B}\widetilde{e}\otimes_{\widetilde{A}}A

\displaystyle x

\displaystyle\mapsto\widetilde{e}x\widetilde{e}\otimes 1

is a homomorphism and
it
induces the determinant
D_{\rho_{\mathfrak{m}}}
when we compose with

\widetilde{e}\widetilde{B}\widetilde{e}\otimes_{\widetilde{A}}A\xrightarrow{\psi_{1}\otimes\mathrm{id}}M_{n}(A)
and the usual determinant (see (
3.2.1
) for
\psi_{1}
).

(2)

The map

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}[G_{F_{v}}]

\displaystyle\to\widetilde{e}\widetilde{B}\widetilde{e}

\displaystyle x

\displaystyle\mapsto\widetilde{e}x\widetilde{e}

is also a homomorphism and
it
induces the representation
\prod_{i=1}^{r}\tilde{\rho}_{i,\mathfrak{m}}^{0}
when we compose with the natural inclusion
\widetilde{e}\widetilde{B}\widetilde{e}\subset\End_{\widetilde{A}[1/p]}(\prod_{i}V_{i}^{0})
(see the final part of Lemma
3.2.2
for why we have this inclusion).

(3)

There is an
\widetilde{A}
-valued lift of the
representation

\rho_{\mathfrak{m}}|_{G_{F_{v}}}
which becomes isomorphic to
\prod_{i=1}^{r}\widetilde{\rho}^{0}_{i,\mathfrak{m}}
when we invert
p
.

Proof.

For the first part, we use Proposition
3.2.3
. Since the determinant

D_{\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}}\otimes_{\widetilde{A}}A=D_{\rho_{\mathfrak{m}}}D_{\rho_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\vee,c}(1-2n)}
is reducible, this tells
us that the kernel

J
of
\widetilde{A}\to A
contains the reducibility ideal

{\mathcal{A}}_{1,2}{\mathcal{A}}_{2,1}
. It follows that the map

\displaystyle A[G_{F}]

\displaystyle\to\widetilde{e}\widetilde{B}\widetilde{e}\otimes_{\widetilde{A}}A

\displaystyle x

\displaystyle\mapsto\widetilde{e}x\widetilde{e}\otimes 1

is a homomorphism, and the determinant induced by
D_{\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}},1}\otimes_{\widetilde{A}}A
is equal to
D_{\rho_{\mathfrak{m}}}
by the uniqueness part of Proposition
3.2.3
.

For the second part, we can check that we have a homomorphism in
\End_{\widetilde{A}[1/p]}(\prod_{i}V_{i}^{0})
, where it follows from the fact that
\prod_{i}V_{i}^{0}
is
G_{F_{v}}
-stable. The identification of the representation with
\prod_{i=1}^{r}\tilde{\rho}_{i,\mathfrak{m}}^{0}
is now clear.

For the third part, since
\rho_{\mathfrak{m}}
is absolutely irreducible as a
G_{F}
-representation, it follows from Skolem–Noether (see e.g.
[
Mil80
, Proposition IV.1.4]
) that, after conjugating
\psi_{1}
by an element of
\GL_{n}(\widetilde{A})
, we can assume that the representation
A[G_{F}]\to M_{n}(A)
given by the first part is equal to
\rho_{\mathfrak{m}}
. By the second part,
\psi_{1}
also induces a
local
representation
\widetilde{A}[G_{F_{v}}]\to M_{n}(\widetilde{A})
which clearly lifts
\rho_{\mathfrak{m}}|_{G_{F_{v}}}
. It also follows from the second part that after inverting
p
this representation becomes isomorphic to
\prod_{i=1}^{r}\tilde{\rho}_{i,\mathfrak{m}}^{0}
.
∎

3.3.
Local deformation rings

In this section we fix a place
v\in S_{p}
in
F
and a residual local Galois representation

\overline{\rho}_{v}:G_{F_{v}}\to\GL_{n}(k).

Definition 3.3.1
.

Let
B
be a finite
E
-algebra, and let
\lambda_{v}=(\lambda_{\tau,1}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{\tau,n})_{\tau\in\Hom(F_{v},E)}
be a dominant weight for
(\mathrm{Res}_{F_{v}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\GL_{n})_{E}
.

(1)

A continuous representation
\rho:G_{F_{v}}\to\GL_{n}(B)
is semistable-ordinary of weight
\lambda_{v}
if it is conjugate to an upper triangular representation

\begin{pmatrix}\chi_{1}&*&\cdots&*\\
0&\chi_{2}&\cdots&*\\
0&0&\ddots&*\\
0&0&\cdots&\chi_{n}\end{pmatrix}

where for each
1\leq j\leq n
and
\sigma\in I_{F_{v}}
we have

\chi_{j}(\sigma)=\prod_{\tau\in\Hom(F_{v},E)}\tau({\operatorname{Art}}_{F_{v}}^{-1}(\sigma))^{-\lambda_{\tau,n+1-j}-(j-1)}

(cf.
[
Ger19
, Definition 3.8]
.)

(2)

We define a
p
-adic Hodge type (in the sense of
[
Kis08
, §2.6]
)
\mathbf{v}_{\lambda_{v}}
associated to
\lambda_{v}
as in
[
Ger19
, §3.3]
. This is an
n
-dimensional
E
-vector space
D_{E}
with a decreasing filtration on
D_{E}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}F_{v}
by
E\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}F_{v}
-submodules. More precisely
D_{E}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}F_{v}
is isomorphic as a filtered
E\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}F_{v}
-module to
D_{\mathrm{dR}}(\rho)
where
\rho:G_{F_{v}}\to\GL_{n}(E)
is a de Rham representation with labelled Hodge–Tate weights
(\lambda_{\tau,1}+n-1>\lambda_{\tau,2}+n-2>\cdots>\lambda_{\tau,n})_{\tau\in\Hom(F_{v},E)}
.

(3)

A semistable continuous representation
\rho:G_{F_{v}}\to\GL_{n}(B)
has
p
-adic Hodge type
\mathbf{v}_{\lambda_{v}}
if for each
i
there is an isomorphism of
B\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}F_{v}
-modules

\mathrm{gr}^{i}(\rho\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}B_{\mathrm{dR}})^{G_{F_{v}}}\cong B\otimes_{E}(\mathrm{gr}^{i}D_{E}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}F_{v}).

We recall (
[
Ger19
, Lemma 3.9]
) that a semistable-ordinary representation of weight
\lambda_{v}
is semistable with
p
-adic Hodge type
\mathbf{v}_{\lambda_{v}}
.

Lemma 3.3.2
.

Let
B
be a finite local
E
-algebra and suppose
\rho_{B}:G_{F_{v}}\to\GL_{n}(B)
is semistable of Hodge type
\mathbf{v}_{\lambda_{v}}
with
\rho:=\rho_{B}\otimes_{B}(B/\mathfrak{m}_{B})
semistable-ordinary of weight
\lambda_{v}
. Then
\rho_{B}
is semistable-ordinary of weight
\lambda_{v}
.

Proof.

Extending
E
if necessary, we assume
B
has residue field
E
. Let
v_{1}\leq v_{2}\leq\cdots\leq v_{n}
denote the valuations of the eigenvalues of geometric Frobenius acting on
\WD(\rho)
(with algebraic multiplicities). It follows from the proof of
[
HKV20
, Lemma 6.4.1]
that in fact this sequence of slopes is strictly increasing and we have an equality

(3.3.1)

v_{i}=\frac{1}{e_{v}}\sum_{\tau}(\lambda_{\tau,n+1-i}+i-1)

for each
1\leq i\leq n
. The ordinary filtration on
\rho
corresponds to a filtration
{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}
of
D_{\mathrm{st}}(\rho)
by admissible filtered
(\phi,N)
-modules which are free over
F_{v,0}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}E
. For
i=1,\ldots,n
,
{\mathcal{F}}^{i}\subset D_{\mathrm{st}}(\rho)
is the
E
-vector subspace spanned by the generalized eigenspaces of
\phi^{f_{v}}
with eigenvalues of valuation
\leq v_{i}
.
The (admissible) filtered
(\phi,N)
-module
D_{\mathrm{st}}(\rho_{B})
is a successive extension of copies of
D_{\mathrm{st}}(\rho)
. We define
{\mathcal{F}}^{i}_{B}
to be the
B
-submodule spanned by the generalized eigenspaces of
\phi^{f_{v}}
with eigenvalues of valuation
\leq v_{i}
. This defines a filtration of
D_{\mathrm{st}}(\rho_{B})
by free
F_{v,0}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}B
-submodules, stable under the actions of
\phi
and
N
. Weak admissibility of
D_{\mathrm{st}}(\rho_{B})
and the equalities (
3.3.1
) imply that each
{\mathcal{F}}_{B}^{i}
is weakly admissible and
{\mathcal{F}}_{B}^{i}/{\mathcal{F}}_{B}^{i+1}=D_{\mathrm{st}}(\chi_{i,B})
for a crystalline character
\chi_{i,B}:G_{F_{v}}\to E^{\times}
with labelled Hodge–Tate weights
(\lambda_{\tau,n+1-i}+i-1)_{\tau\in\Hom(F_{v},E)}
.
∎

We consider the lifting ring
R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\square}
with the universal lift
\rho_{v}^{\mathrm{univ}}:G_{F_{v}}\to\GL_{n}(R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\square})
, and recall some results of Bellovin, Geraghty, Hartl–Hellmann and Kisin:

Theorem 3.3.3
.

Let
\lambda_{v}=(\lambda_{\tau,1}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{\tau,n})_{\tau\in\Hom(F_{v},E)}
be a dominant weight for
(\mathrm{Res}_{F_{v}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\GL_{n})_{E}
.

(1)

There is a unique
{\mathcal{O}}
-flat quotient
R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\mathrm{st},\lambda_{v}}
of
R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\square}
with the following property:

•

If
B
is a finite
E
-algebra, an
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra map
\zeta:R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\square}\to B
factors through
R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\mathrm{st},\lambda_{v}}
if and only
\rho_{v}^{\mathrm{univ}}\otimes_{R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\mathrm{st},\lambda_{v}},\zeta}B
is semistable with
p
-adic Hodge type
\mathbf{v}_{\lambda_{v}}
.

(2)

R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\mathrm{st},\lambda_{v}}
is reduced.

(3)

There is a unique
{\mathcal{O}}
-flat quotient
R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\mathrm{cris},\lambda_{v}}
of
R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\square}
with the following property:

•

If
B
is a finite
E
-algebra, an
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra map
\zeta:R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\square}\to B
factors through
R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\mathrm{cris},\lambda_{v}}
if and only
\rho_{v}^{\mathrm{univ}}\otimes_{R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\mathrm{st},\lambda_{v}},\zeta}B
is crystalline with
p
-adic Hodge type
\mathbf{v}_{\lambda_{v}}
.

(4)

R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\mathrm{cris},\lambda_{v}}[\frac{1}{p}]
is regular (in particular,
R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\mathrm{cris},\lambda_{v}}
is reduced).

(5)

There is a unique
{\mathcal{O}}
-flat quotient
R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\triangle,\lambda_{v}}
of
R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\square}
with the following property:

•

If
B
is a finite
E
-algebra, an
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra map
\zeta:R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\square}\to B
factors through
R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\triangle,\lambda_{v}}
if and only
\rho_{v}^{\mathrm{univ}}\otimes_{R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\mathrm{st},\lambda_{v}},\zeta}B
is semistable-ordinary of weight
\lambda_{v}
.

(6)

\Spec(R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\triangle,\lambda_{v}}[\frac{1}{p}])
is an open and closed subspace of
\Spec(R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\mathrm{st},\lambda_{v}})[\frac{1}{p}]
. In particular,
R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\triangle,\lambda_{v}}
is reduced.

Proof.

The first and third parts are
[
Kis08
, Theorem 2.7.6, Corollary 2.7.7]
. The fourth part is a consequence of
[
Kis08
, Theorem 3.3.8]
. The second part is a consequence of (a very special case of)
[
BG19
, Theorem 3.3.3]
(see also
[
Bel16
,
HH20
]
). The fifth part is
[
Ger19
, Lemma 3.10]
. The sixth part follows from Lemma
3.3.2
.
∎

Remark 3.3.4
.

We have emphasised the reducedness of our local deformation rings because in some parts of the literature the maximal reduced quotients of Kisin’s deformation rings are introduced, characterised by their morphisms to finite field extensions of
E
(e.g. in
[
BLGGT14
]
). It won’t make any difference to us in practice, because in the end we will be considering maps from local deformation rings to reduced finite flat
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebras
\widetilde{A}
as in §
3.2
.

3.3.5.
Fixed determinant deformation rings

It is often useful to consider deformation rings with fixed determinant. Let
\lambda_{v}
be a dominant weight for
(\mathrm{Res}_{F_{v}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\GL_{n})_{E}
and suppose
\psi:G_{F_{v}}\to{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}
is a crystalline character with
\tau
-labelled Hodge–Tate weights
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{\tau,i}+(n-i)
for each
\tau:F\hookrightarrow E
. Suppose moreover that
\det\overline{\rho}_{v}
coincides with
\overline{\psi}:G_{F_{v}}\to k^{\times}
, the reduction of
\psi
. Then we have a quotient
R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\square,\psi}
of
R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\square}
, classifying liftings with determinant
\psi
(composed with the structure map from
{\mathcal{O}}
to the test
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra).

We define quotients
R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\mathrm{cris},\lambda_{v},\psi}=R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\mathrm{cris},\lambda_{v}}\otimes_{R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\square}}R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\square,\psi}
and
R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\triangle,\lambda_{v},\psi}=R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\mathrm{cris},\lambda_{v}}\otimes_{R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\square}}R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\square,\psi}
of our local deformation rings.

Lemma 3.3.6
.

Suppose
p\nmid n
. Let
\lambda_{v}
and
\psi
be as above. Let
R=R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\mathrm{cris},\lambda_{v}}
or
R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\triangle,\lambda_{v}}
, and
R^{\psi}=R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\mathrm{cris},\lambda_{v},\psi}
or
R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\triangle,\lambda_{v},\psi}
respectively. Then there is a section to the quotient map
R\to R^{\psi}
extending to an isomorphism
R^{\psi}\llbracket X\rrbracket\cong R
. In particular,
R^{\psi}
is
{\mathcal{O}}
-flat and reduced and can be characterised using the properties of Theorem
3.3.3
parts (
3
) or (
•
‣
5
) respectively for maps from
R^{\square,\psi}_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}
to finite
E
-algebras.

Proof.

This is essentially
[
EG14
, Lemma 4.3.1]
. If we consider the universal lifting
\rho^{\mathrm{univ}}:G_{F_{v}}\to\GL_{n}(R)
, then the composition of the character
\psi^{-1}\det\rho^{\mathrm{univ}}
with any map
R\to B
to a finite
E
-algebra is crystalline with all labelled Hodge–Tate weights equal to zero. In other words these compositions are unramified. Since
R
is
{\mathcal{O}}
-flat and Noetherian, it follows that
\psi^{-1}\det\rho^{\mathrm{univ}}
is itself unramified. This character is also residually trivial. Since
p\nmid n
, Hensel’s lemma implies that there is an unramified character
\alpha:G_{F_{v}}\to 1+\mathfrak{m}_{R}
with
\alpha^{n}=\psi^{-1}\det\rho^{\mathrm{univ}}
. The representation
\alpha^{-1}\otimes\rho^{\mathrm{univ}}
has determinant
\psi
and defines a section of the quotient map
R\to R^{\psi}
. This extends to a map
R^{\psi}\llbracket X\rrbracket\to R
sending
X
to
\alpha(\mathrm{Frob}_{v})-1
. We can identify
R^{\psi}\llbracket X\rrbracket
with a quotient of
R^{\square}_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}
(in fact, of
R
) using the lifting
\rho^{\mathrm{univ},\psi}\otimes\mathrm{ur}(1+X)
, the twist of the universal lifting to
R^{\psi}
with the unramified character taking
\mathrm{Frob}_{v}
to
1+X
. Composing this lifting with our map
R^{\psi}\llbracket X\rrbracket\to R
gives
\rho^{\mathrm{univ}}
, and it follows that our map is an isomorphism
R^{\psi}\llbracket X\rrbracket\cong R
.
∎

4.
Local-global compatibility in the crystalline case

4.1.
A computation of boundary cohomology

The goal of this section (Corollary
4.1.9
) is to describe, in terms of the cohomology of the
G
-locally symmetric spaces, a particular direct summand of the completed cohomology for the boundary of the
\widetilde{G}
-locally symmetric spaces. It will be one of the key ingredients allowing us to describe Hecke algebras acting on cohomology for
G
in an arbitrary fixed cohomological degree in terms of the middle degree cohomology for
\widetilde{G}
.

4.1.1.
Notation

Let
\bar{T}\supseteq\bar{S}_{p}

be a finite set of finite places of
F^{+}
with preimage the finite set of
finite places
T
of
F
.
Let
\bar{S}\subseteq\bar{S}_{p}
be a set of
primes of
F^{+}
dividing
p
with preimage

S\subseteq S_{p}
. For
\mathrm{G}=\widetilde{G},P,U
, or
G
, we set
\mathrm{G}^{0}_{\bar{S}}=\prod_{\bar{v}\in\bar{S}}\mathrm{G}({\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}_{\bar{v}}})
.

Given

\tilde{\lambda}\in(\mathbb{Z}^{2n}_{+})^{\Hom(F^{+},E)}

a dominant weight for
\widetilde{G}
, we define

{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}}}:=\bigotimes_{\bar{v}\in\bar{S}}\bigotimes_{\tau\in\Hom(F^{+}_{\bar{v}},E),{\mathcal{O}}}{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}}.

We also define the object

{\mathcal{V}}_{U}(\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}},m)
in

D^{b}(\mathrm{Sh}_{G^{T}\times K_{T}}(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{G},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}))

corresponding to the object

R\Gamma(U^{0}_{\bar{S}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}}}/\varpi^{m})
of

D^{b}_{\mathrm{sm}}(K_{S},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})

(after inflation to
D^{b}_{\mathrm{sm}}(G^{T}\times K_{T},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
). The boundedness is a consequence of the finite cohomological dimension of the torsion-free compact
p
-adic analytic group
U^{0}_{\bar{S}}
cf.
[
Ser65
]
.

We let
{\mathcal{V}}^{j}_{U}(\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}},m)
denote its
cohomology sheaves; note that these are non-zero precisely when
j
ranges from
0

to
n^{2}\sum_{\bar{v}\in\bar{S}}[F^{+}_{\bar{v}}:{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}]
, by the Künneth formula
for group cohomology and by
Lemma
2.3.17
.

If
K\subset G({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})
is a good subgroup, each
{\mathcal{V}}_{U}(\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}},m)
descends to an object in
D^{b}(\mathrm{Sh}(X_{K},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}))
with locally constant cohomology sheaves. Taking a homotopy limit gives
{\mathcal{V}}_{U}(\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}})\in D^{b}(\mathrm{Sh}(X_{K},{\mathcal{O}}))
again with locally constant cohomology sheaves (since
X_{K}
is locally contractible we can find opens over which the
{\mathcal{V}}^{j}_{U}(\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}},m)
are constant for all
m\geq 1
). By passing to a limit over
m
, the cohomology
R\Gamma(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{U}(\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}})\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}_{p}\backslash\bar{S}}})
comes with whatever actions (e.g. of
{\mathbb{T}}^{T}
or a monoid acting at
\bar{S}_{p}\backslash\bar{S}
) we have on
R\Gamma(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{U}(\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}},m)\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}_{p}\backslash\bar{S}}}/\varpi^{m})
.

We will often have an action of a commutative
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra
{\mathbb{T}}
(one of our Hecke algebras) on an
{\mathcal{O}}
-module or, more generally, an object in
C
in
D({\mathcal{O}})
. We will then write
{\mathbb{T}}(C)
for the image of
{\mathbb{T}}
in
\End_{D({\mathcal{O}})}(C)
.

Given
\lambda\in(\mathbb{Z}^{n}_{+})^{\Hom(F,E)}

a dominant weight for
G
, we
define

{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{S}}:=\bigotimes_{v\in S}\bigotimes_{\tau\in\Hom(F_{v},E),{\mathcal{O}}}{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\tau}}.

4.1.2.
Boundary cohomology

Theorem 4.1.3
.

Assume that
\widetilde{K}\subset\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})
is
a good subgroup that is decomposed with respect to
P
,
and with the property that, for each
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}_{p}
,

\widetilde{K}_{U,\bar{v}}=U^{0}_{\bar{v}}
.
Let
\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathbb{T}^{T}
be a non-Eisenstein
maximal ideal and let
\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}:={\mathcal{S}}^{*}(\mathfrak{m})\subset\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{T}
.

Choose a partition

\bar{S}_{p}=\bar{S}_{1}\sqcup\bar{S}_{2}

of the set
\bar{S}_{p}
of primes of
F^{+}
lying above
p
.
Let
\tilde{\lambda}
be a dominant weight
for
\widetilde{G}
satisfying
\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{v}}=0
for all
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}_{2}
.
Then

{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\circ\mathrm{Ind}_{P_{\bar{S}_{2}}}^{\widetilde{G}_{\bar{S}_{2}}}R\Gamma\left(K^{\bar{S}_{2}},R\Gamma\left(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{G},{\mathcal{V}}_{U}(\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}_{1}},m)\right)\right)_{\mathfrak{m}}

is a
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{T}
-equivariant direct summand of

R\Gamma\left(\widetilde{K}^{\bar{S}_{2}},R\Gamma\left(\partial\mathfrak{X}_{\widetilde{G}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m}\right)\right)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}

in
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\widetilde{G}_{\bar{S}_{2}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
.

Proof.

By combining Proposition
4.1.4
and Lemma
4.1.5
,
we obtain a
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{T}
-equivariant
isomorphism in
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\widetilde{G}_{\bar{S}_{2}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})

(4.1.1)

R\Gamma\left(\widetilde{K}^{\bar{S}_{2}},R\Gamma\left(\partial\mathfrak{X}_{\widetilde{G}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m}\right)\right)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}R\Gamma\left(\widetilde{K}^{\bar{S}_{2}},\text{Ind}_{P^{\bar{S}_{1}}\times P^{0}_{\bar{S}_{1}}}^{\widetilde{G}^{\bar{S}_{1}}\times\widetilde{G}^{0}_{\bar{S}_{1}}}R\Gamma\left(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{P},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m}\right)\right)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}.

Using the analogue for smooth representations of
[
NT16
, Corollary 2.6]
,
we can rewrite the RHS of (
4.1.1
)
as
r_{P}^{*}
applied to

(4.1.2)

\bigoplus_{g}\text{Ind}_{P_{\bar{S}_{2}}}^{\widetilde{G}_{\bar{S}_{2}}}R\Gamma\left(g\widetilde{K}^{\bar{S}_{2}}g^{-1}\cap P({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f}),R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{P},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m})\right)_{r_{G}^{*}\mathfrak{m}},

where
g
runs over the double cosets

(P_{T\setminus\bar{S}_{p}}\times P^{0}_{\bar{S}_{1}})\backslash(\widetilde{G}_{T\setminus\bar{S}_{p}}\times\widetilde{G}^{0}_{\bar{S}_{1}})/(\widetilde{K}_{T\setminus\bar{S}_{p}}\times\widetilde{K}_{\bar{S}_{1}}),

and we view each
R\Gamma\left(g\widetilde{K}^{\bar{S}_{2}}g^{-1}\cap P({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f}),R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{P},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m})\right)

as a
\mathbb{T}_{P}^{T}
-module in
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(P_{\bar{S}_{2}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
. We restrict to the direct summand corresponding to
g=1
in (
4.1.2
).
We now conclude by Proposition
4.1.8
.
∎

Proposition 4.1.4
.

(1)

We have
\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})
-equivariant closed immersion

(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{P}\times\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f}))/P({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})\hookrightarrow\partial\mathfrak{X}_{\widetilde{G}},

whose complement is a disjoint union of locally closed subspaces of the form

(\mathfrak{X}_{Q}\times\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f}))/Q({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})
with

Q\subset\widetilde{G}
a standard
F^{+}
-rational
parabolic such that
Q\not\subseteq P
.

(2)

With all assumptions as in Theorem
4.1.3
,
the natural pullback map induces a
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{T}
-equivariant
isomorphism in
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\widetilde{G}_{\bar{S}_{2}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})

R\Gamma\left(\widetilde{K}^{\bar{S}_{2}},R\Gamma\left(\partial\mathfrak{X}_{\widetilde{G}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m}\right)\right)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}R\Gamma\left(\widetilde{K}^{\bar{S}_{2}},R\Gamma\left((\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{P}\times\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f}))/P({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f}),{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m}\right)\right)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}.

Proof.

The first part follows from the description of the boundary of
the Borel–Serre compactification in
[
NT16
, §3.1.2]
, see especially Lemma 3.10 of
loc. cit.
This reference uses the ‘discrete’ versions
\mathfrak{X}_{Q}^{\mathrm{dis}}
of the spaces
\mathfrak{X}_{Q}
, but we can compare the two situations after taking quotients by compact open subgroups, and the space
(\mathfrak{X}_{Q}\times\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f}))/Q({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})
is equal to the inverse limit of its quotients by compact open subgroups
\widetilde{K}
of
\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})
(we can use the fact that
\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})\rightarrow Q({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})\backslash\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})
is a locally trivial
Q({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})
-torsor, as in the proof of Proposition
2.3.3
). These quotients can in turn be computed as quotients of
(\mathfrak{X}_{Q}^{\mathrm{dis}}\times\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})^{\mathrm{dis}})/Q({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})^{\mathrm{dis}}
, because
Q({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})\backslash\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})/\widetilde{K}
is a finite set for each compact open
\widetilde{K}
.

For the second part, we
first note that we can check whether a map is an isomorphism on
the level of cohomology groups. For a standard
F^{+}
-rational proper parabolic
subgroup
Q\subset\widetilde{G}
and a good subgroup
\widetilde{K}\subset\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})
, set

\widetilde{X}^{Q}_{\widetilde{K}}:=(\mathfrak{X}_{Q}\times\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f}))/Q({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})\widetilde{K},

which is a disjoint union of finitely many locally symmetric spaces for
Q
.
Using excision and Lemma
2.1.7
,
we see that it is enough to show that, for
Q\subset\widetilde{G}
a standard
F^{+}
-rational
parabolic with
Q\not\subseteq P
, we have

H^{i}_{c}(\widetilde{X}^{Q}_{\widetilde{K}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}=0,

for any
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
. This is standard by now, see for example
the proof of
[
ACC+18
, Theorem 3.4.2]
.
∎

Lemma 4.1.5
.

Keep the assumption on
\tilde{\lambda}
from the statement
of Theorem
4.1.3
. There is a natural
isomorphism in
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\widetilde{G}^{\bar{S}_{1}}\times\widetilde{G}^{0}_{\bar{S}_{1}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})

R\Gamma\left((\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{P}\times\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f}))/P({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f}),{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m}\right)\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}\mathrm{Ind}^{\widetilde{G}^{\bar{S}_{1}}\times\widetilde{G}^{0}_{\bar{S}_{1}}}_{P^{\bar{S}_{1}}\times P^{0}_{\bar{S}_{1}}}R\Gamma\left(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{P},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m}\right).

Proof.

The case of constant coefficients
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}

follows from Lemma
2.3.14
combined with
the Iwasawa decomposition at primes in
\bar{S}_{1}
. For coefficients in a
local system
{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m}
, where by assumption the action is non-trivial only at the
primes in
\bar{S}_{1}
,
we use the projection formula in Lemma
2.1.8

and the tensor identity in Lemma
2.3.15
(at primes in
\bar{S}_{1}
)
to reduce to the case of constant coefficients.
∎

Lemma 4.1.6
.

There is a natural isomorphism

\mathrm{Inf}_{G({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})}^{P({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})}R\Gamma\left(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{G},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}\right)\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}R\Gamma\left(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{P},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}\right)

in
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(P({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f}),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
.

Proof.

The map is given by
pullback along the
P({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})
-equivariant projection

\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{P}\twoheadrightarrow\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{G}
.
To show that the map is an isomorphism, it is enough to check
that it induces an isomorphism after applying the forgetful functor to
D^{+}({\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
,
and then it is enough to consider it on the level of cohomology groups.
By Lemma
2.1.7
, this
reduces to establishing the isomorphism

\varinjlim_{K}H^{i}(X_{K},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}\varinjlim_{K_{P}}H^{i}(X^{P}_{K_{P}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}),

where
K_{P}\subset P({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})
runs over compact open subgroups
of the form
K\ltimes K_{U}
with
K\subset G({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})
and

K_{U}\subset U({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})
compact open subgroups.
We use the Leray–Serre spectral sequence for the fibration

X^{U}_{K_{U}}\to X^{P}_{K_{P}}\to X_{K}
.
It is enough to check that

\varinjlim_{K_{U}}H^{j}(X^{U}_{K_{U}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})=\varinjlim_{\Gamma_{U}:=U(F^{+})\cap K_{U}}H^{j}(\Gamma_{U}\backslash U(F^{+}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathbb{R}),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})=\begin{cases}{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}&\text{for }j=0\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

The first equality follows by strong approximation for
U
,
the second by direct computation.
We note that this computation should be thought of in the
category of
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}[\![K]\!]
-modules, with a trivial action of
K
on the RHS.
∎

Lemma 4.1.7
.

With the notation as in the proof of Theorem
4.1.3
,
there exists a natural isomorphism

\mathrm{Inf}_{G^{T\setminus\bar{S}_{2}}}^{P^{T\setminus\bar{S}_{2}}}R\Gamma\left(K_{T\setminus\bar{S}_{2}},R\Gamma\left(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{G},{\mathcal{V}}_{U}(\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}_{1}},m)\right)\right)\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}R\Gamma\left(K_{P,T\setminus\bar{S}_{2}},R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{P},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m})\right)

in
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(P^{T\setminus\bar{S}_{2}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
.

Proof.

By Lemma
2.1.9
,
we have isomorphisms

R\Gamma\left(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{G},{\mathcal{V}}_{U}(\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}_{1}},m)\right)\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{G},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}}R\Gamma(K_{U,\bar{S}_{1}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m})

\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{G},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}}R\Gamma(K_{U,T\setminus\bar{S}_{2}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m})

in
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(G^{T\setminus\bar{S}_{2}}\times K_{T\setminus\bar{S}_{2}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
.
We have a natural isomorphism

\mathrm{Inf}_{G^{T\setminus\bar{S}_{2}}}^{P^{T\setminus\bar{S}_{2}}}\left(R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{G},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}}R\Gamma(K_{U,T\setminus\bar{S}_{2}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m})\right)\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}

R\Gamma\left(K_{U,T\setminus\bar{S}_{2}},\mathrm{Inf}_{G({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})}^{P({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})}R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{G},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}}{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m}\right)

in
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(P^{T\setminus\bar{S}_{2}}\times K_{T\setminus\bar{S}_{2}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})

because an arbitrary colimit of injective representations of
K_{U,T\setminus\bar{S}_{2}}

is injective. We conclude by Lemma
4.1.6
, by
Hochschild–Serre applied to
K_{P,T\setminus\bar{S}_{2}}=K_{T\setminus\bar{S}_{2}}\ltimes K_{U,T\setminus\bar{S}_{2}}
,
and by Lemma
2.1.8
applied to
\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{P}
.
∎

Proposition 4.1.8
.

With the notation as in the proof of Theorem
4.1.3
,
we have a
\mathbb{T}^{T}_{P}
-equivariant isomorphism

R\Gamma\left(K_{P}^{\bar{S}_{2}},R\Gamma(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{P},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m})\right)\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}r_{G}^{*}\circ\mathrm{Inf}_{G_{\bar{S}_{2}}}^{P_{\bar{S}_{2}}}R\Gamma\left(K^{\bar{S}_{2}},R\Gamma\left(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{G},{\mathcal{V}}_{U}(\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}_{1}},m)\right)\right)

in
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(P_{\bar{S}_{2}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
.

Proof.

We separate the set of finite places of
F^{+}
away from
\bar{S}_{2}

into the union of the set of finite places away from
T
and the set of finite places
T\setminus\bar{S}_{2}
,
which contains
\bar{S}_{1}
.
By the analogue for smooth representations
of
[
NT16
, Corollary 2.8]
,
there is a
\mathbb{T}^{T}_{P}
-equivariant
natural transformation in
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(P_{\bar{S}_{2}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
between

r_{G}^{*}R\Gamma\left(K^{T},\mathrm{Inf}_{G_{\bar{S}_{2}}}^{P_{\bar{S}_{2}}}R\Gamma(K_{T\setminus\bar{S}_{2}},R\Gamma\left(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{G},{\mathcal{V}}_{U}(\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}_{1}},m)\right)\right)

and

R\Gamma\left(K_{P}^{T},\mathrm{Inf}_{G^{T\setminus\bar{S}_{2}}}^{P^{T\setminus\bar{S}_{2}}}R\Gamma(K_{T\setminus\bar{S}_{2}},R\Gamma\left(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{G},{\mathcal{V}}_{U}(\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}_{1}},m)\right)\right).

If we can show that this natural transformation is an isomorphism, we can
conclude by Lemma
4.1.7
. To prove that the natural transformation
is an isomorphism, we can forget the
\mathbb{T}^{T}_{P}
-action and work
in
D^{+}({\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
. There, it reduces to the statement that
taking
K^{T}_{U}
-invariants is an exact functor,
which holds true because
K^{T}_{U}
is a profinite abelian group
with trivial
p
-part.
∎

The following is a result in the style of
[
ACC+18
, Theorems 4.2.1 and 5.4.1]
.
This is the only part of this subsection that we will use in what follows.

Corollary 4.1.9
.

Assume that
\widetilde{K}\subset\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})
is
a good subgroup that is decomposed with respect to
P
,
and with the property that, for each
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}_{p}
,

\widetilde{K}_{U,\bar{v}}=U^{0}_{\bar{v}}
.
Let
\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathbb{T}^{T}
be a non-Eisenstein
maximal ideal and let
\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}:={\mathcal{S}}^{*}(\mathfrak{m})\subset\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{T}
.

Choose a partition

\bar{S}_{p}=\bar{S}_{1}\sqcup\bar{S}_{2}\sqcup\bar{S}_{3}

of the set
\bar{S}_{p}
of primes of
F^{+}
lying above
p
.
Let
\tilde{\lambda}
and

\lambda

be dominant weights for
\widetilde{G}

and
G
, respectively. Assume that the following conditions are satisfied:

(1)

For each
\tau:F^{+}\hookrightarrow E

inducing a place
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}_{1}
,
\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}=(-\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}c},\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}})

(identification as in (
2.1.4
));

(2)

For each
\tau:F^{+}\hookrightarrow E

inducing a place
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}_{2}\sqcup\bar{S}_{3}
,
\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}=0
.

Then

{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\circ\mathrm{Ind}_{P_{\bar{S}_{3}}}^{\widetilde{G}_{\bar{S}_{3}}}R\Gamma\left(K^{\bar{S}_{3}},R\Gamma\left(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{G},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}_{1}}}/\varpi^{m}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{U}(\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}_{2}},m)\right)\right)_{\mathfrak{m}}

is a
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{T}
-equivariant direct summand of

R\Gamma\left(\widetilde{K}^{\bar{S}_{3}},R\Gamma\left(\partial\mathfrak{X}_{\widetilde{G}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m}\right)\right)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}

in
D^{+}_{\mathrm{sm}}(\widetilde{G}_{\bar{S}_{3}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})
.

Proof.

This follows from Theorem
4.1.3

applied with
\bar{S}^{\prime}_{1}:=\bar{S}_{1}\sqcup\bar{S}_{2}
and

\bar{S}^{\prime}_{2}:=\bar{S}_{3}
,
as long as we can show that
{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}_{1}}}/\varpi^{m}

is a
G^{T}\times K_{T}
-equivariant direct summand
of
{\mathcal{V}}_{U}(\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}_{1}},m)
. This follows
from
[
NT16
, Corollary 2.11]
, as in the proof of
[
ACC+18
, Theorem 4.2.1]
(and the proof of
[
ACC+18
, Theorem 2.4.4]
).
∎

4.2.
The integral case

In this section we prove our main results on local–global compatibility: Proposition
4.2.13
and Theorem
4.2.15
.

4.2.1.
Degree shifting

Let
\bar{S}\subset\bar{S}_{p}
. Recall that, for each
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}_{p}
,
we have chosen a place
\tilde{v}\mid\bar{v}
of
F
, with complex
conjugate
\tilde{v}^{c}
. The isomorphism
\iota_{\tilde{v}}:\widetilde{G}(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})\cong\GL_{2n}(F_{\tilde{v}})
identifies the Levi subgroup
G(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})=\GL_{n}(F_{\tilde{v}})\times\GL_{n}(F_{\tilde{v}^{c}})
with block diagonal matrices in
\GL_{2n}(F_{\tilde{v}})
via
(A_{\tilde{v}},A_{\tilde{v}^{c}})\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}(\Psi_{n}\ ^{t}A_{\tilde{v}^{c}}^{-1}\Psi_{n})^{c}&0\\
0&A_{\tilde{v}}\end{pmatrix}
.

We have standard parabolics
Q_{\bar{v}}\subset P_{\bar{v}}
for each
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}
and we set
K_{\bar{v}}:={\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\cap G_{\bar{v}}(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})
, a parahoric subgroup of
G_{\bar{v}}(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})
. The conjugate parahoric
(K_{\bar{v}})^{w^{P}_{0}}
can also be viewed as the intersection
\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}_{\bar{v}}^{w^{P}_{0}}\cap G_{\bar{v}}(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})
, where
\overline{Q}_{\bar{v}}^{w^{P}_{0}}
is the standard parabolic with Levi subgroup
Q_{\bar{v}}^{w^{P}_{0}}\cap G_{\bar{v}}(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})
.

We introduce the following notation on the level of abstract
Hecke algebras:

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}},\bar{S}-\ord}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{T}^{T}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\left(\bigotimes_{\bar{v}\in\bar{S}}{\mathcal{H}}(\Delta^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}},K_{\bar{v}})\right),\mathbb{T}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}},\bar{S}-\ord}_{w^{P}_{0}}:=\mathbb{T}^{T}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\left(\bigotimes_{\bar{v}\in\bar{S}}{\mathcal{H}}((\Delta^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}})^{w^{P}_{0}},K_{\bar{v}}^{w^{P}_{0}})\right)

\displaystyle\mathrm{and}\ \widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}},\bar{S}-\ord}

\displaystyle:=\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{T}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\left(\bigotimes_{\bar{v}\in\bar{S}}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{\Delta}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}},{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}})[\widetilde{U}_{\tilde{v},n}^{-1}]\right).

The unnormalised Satake transform
{\mathcal{S}}:\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}\to\mathbb{T}
extends to a morphism
denoted by

{\mathcal{S}}^{w^{P}_{0}}:\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}},\bar{S}-\ord}\to\mathbb{T}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}},\bar{S}-\ord}_{w^{P}_{0}},

given by
[{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\nu(\varpi_{\bar{v}}){\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}]\mapsto[K_{\bar{v}}^{w^{P}_{0}}\nu(\varpi_{\bar{v}})^{w^{P}_{0}}K_{\bar{v}}^{w^{P}_{0}}]
. In particular,
\widetilde{U}_{\tilde{v},n}
maps to
U_{\tilde{v}}=[K_{\bar{v}}^{w^{P}_{0}}\mathrm{diag}(\varpi_{\tilde{v}},\cdots,\varpi_{\tilde{v}})K_{\bar{v}}^{w^{P}_{0}}]
and
\widetilde{U}_{\tilde{v},2n}
maps to

U_{\tilde{v}}U_{\tilde{v}^{c}}^{-1}=[K_{\bar{v}}^{w^{P}_{0}}\left(\mathrm{diag}(\varpi_{\tilde{v}},\cdots,\varpi_{\tilde{v}}),(\mathrm{diag}(\varpi_{\tilde{v}},\cdots,\varpi_{\tilde{v}})^{-1})^{c}\right)K_{\bar{v}}^{w^{P}_{0}}].

To make use of Poincaré duality, we will need to introduce Hecke algebras twisted by the duality involutions
\iota,\tilde{\iota}
which are defined by

\iota[K^{w^{P}_{0}}gK^{w^{P}_{0}}]=[K^{w^{P}_{0}}g^{-1}K^{w^{P}_{0}}]\text{ and }\tilde{\iota}[\widetilde{K}g\widetilde{K}]=[\widetilde{K}g^{-1}\widetilde{K}]

(see
[
ACC+18
, §2.2.19]
). They give isomorphisms

\iota:\mathbb{T}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}},\bar{S}-\ord}_{w^{P}_{0}}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}\mathbb{T}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}},\bar{S}-\ord,\iota}_{w^{P}_{0}}:=\mathbb{T}^{T}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\left(\bigotimes_{\bar{v}\in\bar{S}}{\mathcal{H}}(\left((\Delta^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}})^{w^{P}_{0}}\right)^{-1},K_{\bar{v}}^{w^{P}_{0}})\right)

and

\tilde{\iota}:\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}},\bar{S}-\ord}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}},\bar{S}-\ord,\tilde{\iota}}:=\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{T}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\left(\bigotimes_{\bar{v}\in\bar{S}}{\mathcal{H}}(\left(\widetilde{\Delta}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}}\right)^{-1},{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}})[[{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\tilde{u}_{\tilde{v},n}^{-1}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}]^{-1}]\right).

We will also make use of an untwisted Hecke algebra for the Levi, defined by
\mathbb{T}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}},\bar{S}-\ord,\iota}:=\mathbb{T}^{T}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\left(\bigotimes_{\bar{v}\in\bar{S}}{\mathcal{H}}(\left(\Delta^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}}\right)^{-1},K_{\bar{v}})\right)
.

We define
{\mathcal{S}}^{\iota}:\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}},\bar{S}-\ord,\tilde{\iota}}\to\mathbb{T}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}},\bar{S}-\ord,\iota}
extending
{\mathcal{S}}
according to the formula

[{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\nu(\varpi_{\bar{v}})^{-1}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}]\mapsto[K_{\bar{v}}\nu(\varpi_{\bar{v}})^{-1}K_{\bar{v}}].

Proposition 4.2.2
.

Assume that
\widetilde{K}\subset\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})
is
a good subgroup that is decomposed with respect to
P

and with the property that, for each
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}_{p}
,

\widetilde{K}_{U,\bar{v}}=U^{0}_{\bar{v}}
.
Let
\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathbb{T}^{T}
be a non-Eisenstein
maximal ideal, let
\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}:={\mathcal{S}}^{*}(\mathfrak{m})\subset\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{T}
,
and assume that
\bar{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
is decomposed generic
in the sense of Definition
2.1.27
.

Choose a partition

\bar{S}_{p}=\bar{S}_{1}\sqcup\bar{S}_{2}\sqcup\bar{S}_{3}

of the set
\bar{S}_{p}
of primes of
F^{+}
lying above
p
, together with standard parabolic subgroups
Q_{\bar{v}}\subset P_{\bar{v}}
for each
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}_{3}
.
Let
\tilde{\lambda}
and

\lambda

be dominant weights for
\widetilde{G}

and
G
, respectively. Assume that the following conditions are satisfied:

(1)

For each
\tau:F^{+}\hookrightarrow E

inducing a place
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}_{1}
,
\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}=(-w_{0,n}\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}c},\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}})

(identification as in (
2.1.4
));

(2)

For each
\tau:F^{+}\hookrightarrow E

inducing a place
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}_{2}
,
\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}=0
.

(3)

For each
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}_{3}
,
\widetilde{K}_{\bar{v}}={\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}
. For each

\tau:F^{+}\hookrightarrow E
inducing such a place
\bar{v}
, we also have the standard identification

\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}=(-w_{0,n}\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}c},\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}})

Then the unnormalised Satake transform

{\mathcal{S}}^{w^{P}_{0}}:\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}_{3}},\bar{S}_{3}-\ord}\to\mathbb{T}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}_{3}},\bar{S}_{3}-\ord}_{w^{P}_{0}}
descends to a
homomorphism

\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}_{3}},\bar{S}_{3}-\ord}\left(H^{d}\left(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}\right)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\ord}\right)\to\mathbb{T}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}_{3}},\bar{S}_{3}-\ord}_{w^{P}_{0}}\left(\mathbb{H}^{d}\left(X_{K^{\bar{S}_{3}}K_{\bar{S}_{3}}^{w^{P}_{0}}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}_{1}}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{U}(\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}_{2}})\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}_{3}}}\right)_{\mathfrak{m}}\right),

where
\mathbb{H}^{d}
denotes the degree
d
hypercohomology. The
Hecke action on the source is defined in (
2.1.9
) and
the one on the target is defined in (
2.1.10
). The
Hecke operators
\widetilde{U}_{\tilde{v},n}
for
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}
are
invertible on the source because we are considering the ordinary part of
cohomology at the primes in
\bar{S}
.

Moreover, we have an injection

\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}_{3}},\bar{S}_{3}-\ord}\left(H^{d}\left(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}\right)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\ord}\right)\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}_{3}},\bar{S}_{3}-\ord}\left(H^{d}\left(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}[1/p]\right)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\ord}\right).

Proof.

We will apply Theorem
2.1.28
, noting that taking the ordinary part preserves injectivity and surjectivity of the maps in that statement. This reduces us to proving that
{\mathcal{S}}^{w^{P}_{0}}
descends to a homomorphism

\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}_{3}},\bar{S}_{3}-\ord}\left(H^{d}\left(\partial\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}\right)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\ord}\right)\to\mathbb{T}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}_{3}},\bar{S}_{3}-\ord}_{w^{P}_{0}}\left(\mathbb{H}^{d}\left(X_{K^{\bar{S}_{3}}K_{\bar{S}_{3}}^{w^{P}_{0}}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}_{1}}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{U}(\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}_{2}})\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}_{3}}}\right)_{\mathfrak{m}}\right).

Combining Proposition
2.2.17
and Lemma
2.2.12
shows that
H^{d}(\partial\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m})^{\ord}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
is isomorphic as a
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}_{3}},\bar{S}_{3}-\ord}
-module with

R^{d}\Gamma\left(K_{\bar{S}_{3}},\pi^{\ord}_{\partial}(\widetilde{K}^{\bar{S}_{3}},\tilde{\lambda}^{\bar{S}_{3}},m)\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}_{3}}}^{w^{P}_{0}}/\varpi^{m}\right).

For each
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}_{3}
, the action of
{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{\Delta}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}},{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}})[\widetilde{U}_{\tilde{v},n}^{-1}]
is via its isomorphism to
{\mathcal{H}}({\Delta}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\bar{v}},K_{\bar{v}})
(cf. Lemma
2.1.15
).

Using Corollary
4.1.9
and interchanging the
P
-ordinary part with the tensor product by
{\mathcal{V}}^{w^{P}_{0}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}_{3}}}
, we find a
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}_{3}},\bar{S}_{3}-\ord}
-equivariant direct summand

(4.2.1)

\ord_{0}\mathbb{H}^{d}\left(K_{\bar{S}_{3}}\ltimes U^{0}_{\bar{S}_{3}},\mathrm{Ind}_{P_{\bar{S}_{3}}}^{\widetilde{G}_{\bar{S}_{3}}}R\Gamma\left(K^{\bar{S}_{3}},R\Gamma\left(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{G},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}_{1}}}/\varpi^{m}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{U}(\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}_{2}},m)\right)\right)\otimes{\mathcal{V}}^{w^{P}_{0}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}_{3}}}/\varpi^{m}\right)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}.

We find a subquotient of the term (
4.2.1
) using the
w^{P}_{0}
-case
of Proposition
2.3.11
. We claim that it is

\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}_{3}},\bar{S}_{3}-\ord}
-equivariantly
isomorphic to

(4.2.2)

\mathbb{H}^{d}\left(X_{K^{\bar{S}_{3}}K_{\bar{S}_{3}}^{w^{P}_{0}}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}_{1}}}/\varpi^{m}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{U}(\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}_{2}},m)\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}_{3}}}/\varpi^{m}\right)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}},

where the action of
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}_{3}},\bar{S}_{3}-\ord}
on (
4.2.2
) is
via the extension
{\mathcal{S}}^{w^{P}_{0}}
of the unnormalised Satake transform defined above. This follows from the fact that the action of
G_{\bar{S}_{3}}
on
