R\Gamma\left(K^{\bar{S}_{3}},R\Gamma\left(\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{G},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}_{1}}}/\varpi^{m}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{U}(\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}_{2}},m)\right)\right)
needs to be
pre-conjugated by
w^{P}_{0}
when applying Proposition
2.3.11
. Note that this is compatible with the rescaled Hecke actions, since the fact that
w^{P}_{0}
commutes with
w_{0}^{G}
implies that we have the equality

\alpha^{\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}_{\bar{v}}^{w^{P}_{0}}}_{\lambda}(\nu(\varpi_{\bar{v}})^{w^{P}_{0}})=\alpha^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}_{\lambda^{w^{P}_{0}}}(\nu(\varpi_{\bar{v}})).

Taking stock,
we obtain a homomorphism

\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}_{3}},\bar{S}_{3}-\ord}\left(H^{d}\left(\partial\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m}\right)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\ord}\right)\\
\to\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}_{3}},\bar{S}_{3}-\ord}\left(\mathbb{H}^{d}\left(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}_{1}}}/\varpi^{m}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{U}(\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}_{2}},m)\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}_{3}}}/\varpi^{m}\right)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}\right),

where the action of
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}_{3}},\bar{S}_{3}-\ord}

on the RHS is via
{\mathcal{S}}^{w^{P}_{0}}
.

Finally, these morphisms
are compatible as
m
varies and all
the above cohomology groups are finitely generated
{\mathcal{O}}
-modules,
so we can take inverse limits with respect to
m
to obtain
a homomorphism

\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}_{3}},\bar{S}_{3}-\ord}\left(H^{d}\left(\partial\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}\right)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\ord}\right)\to\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}_{3}},\bar{S}_{3}-\ord}\left(\mathbb{H}^{d}\left(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}_{1}}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{U}(\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}_{2}})\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}_{3}}}\right)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}\right).

We conclude because
{\mathcal{S}}^{*}({\mathfrak{m}})=\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}
, so
\mathbb{H}^{d}\left(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}_{1}}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{U}(\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}_{2}})\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}_{3}}}\right)_{\mathfrak{m}}

is a Hecke-equivariant direct summand of

\mathbb{H}^{d}\left(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}_{1}}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{U}(\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}_{2}})\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}_{3}}}\right)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
.
∎

We have a similar statement to the above which will be useful after applying Poincaré duality. Most things go through very similarly. At places in
\bar{S}_{2}
, the weight
\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}_{2}}
is trivial so nothing changes when we take a dual. At places in
\bar{S}_{3}
we use the results of §
2.2.18
and Corollary
2.3.12
. However, we need to modify things at places in
\overline{S}_{1}
, because
R\Gamma(U^{0}_{\bar{S}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}}}^{\vee}/\varpi^{m})
may not admit
{\mathcal{V}}_{{\lambda}_{\bar{S}}}^{\vee}/\varpi^{m}
as a
K_{\bar{S}}
-equivariant direct summand. This causes a problem for the analogue of Corollary
4.1.9
on the dual side. The following lemma will act as a replacement.

Lemma 4.2.3
.

Let
\bar{S}\subset\bar{S}_{p}
, let
\tilde{\lambda}
and
\lambda
be dominant weights for
\widetilde{G}
and
G
respectively. Assume that the following condition is satisfied:

(1)

For each
\tau:F^{+}\hookrightarrow E
inducing a place
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}
,
\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}=(\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}},-w_{0,n}\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}c})
(note that this is the
w^{P}_{0}
-conjugate of the standard identification).

Let
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
be an integer. Then
R\Gamma\left(U^{0}_{\bar{S}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}}}^{\vee}/\varpi^{m}\right)
admits
{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}}}^{\vee}/\varpi^{m}
as a
K_{\bar{S}}
-equivariant direct summand.

Proof.

Taking duals and applying the proof of
[
ACC+18
, Theorem 2.4.4]
, it suffices to show that there is a
{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}}}
surjective
P({\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+},\bar{S}})
-equivariant map

{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}}}\to{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}}}

with a
K_{\bar{S}}
-equivariant splitting. It follows from
[
NT16
, Proposition 2.10]
that there is a
K_{\bar{S}}
-equivariant decomposition
{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}}}={\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}}}\oplus W
, with
{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}}}
invariant under the action of the unipotent subgroup
\overline{U}({\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+},\bar{S}})
in the parabolic opposite to
P
. Moreover, it follows from the main theorem of
[
Cab84
]
that, after extending scalars to
\overline{E}
,
W\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}}\overline{E}
is identified with the
P(F^{+}_{\bar{S}})
-stable subspace
(1-U(F^{+}_{\bar{S}})){\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S},\overline{E}}}\subset{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S},\overline{E}}}
(thanks to Lambert A’Campo for pointing this out). This implies that
W
is
P({\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+},\bar{S}})
-stable, so quotienting out by
W
gives the desired map
{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}}}\to{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}}}
.
∎

We can now state our version of Proposition
4.2.2
with dual coefficients.

Proposition 4.2.4
.

Assume that
\widetilde{K}\subset\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})
is
a good subgroup that is decomposed with respect to
P
,
and with the property that, for each
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}_{p}
,

\widetilde{K}_{U,\bar{v}}=U^{0}_{\bar{v}}
.
Let
\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathbb{T}^{T}
be a non-Eisenstein
maximal ideal and assume that
\bar{\rho}_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}(\mathfrak{m}^{\vee})}
is decomposed generic
in the sense of Definition
2.1.27
.

Choose a partition

\bar{S}_{p}=\bar{S}_{1}\sqcup\bar{S}_{2}\sqcup\bar{S}_{3}

of the set
\bar{S}_{p}
of primes of
F^{+}
lying above
p
, together with standard parabolic subgroups
Q_{\bar{v}}\subset P_{\bar{v}}
for each
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}_{3}
.
Let
\tilde{\lambda}
and

\lambda

be dominant weights for
\widetilde{G}

and
G
, respectively. Assume that the following conditions are satisfied:

(1)

For each
\tau:F^{+}\hookrightarrow E

inducing a place
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}_{1}
,
\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}=(\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}},-w_{0,n}\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}c})

(note the change compared to Proposition
4.2.2
);

(2)

For each
\tau:F^{+}\hookrightarrow E

inducing a place
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}_{2}
,
\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}=0
.

(3)

For each
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}_{3}
,
\widetilde{K}_{\bar{v}}={\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}
. For each

\tau:F^{+}\hookrightarrow E
inducing such a place
\bar{v}
, we also have

\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}=(\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}},-w_{0,n}\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}c})

Then the unnormalised Satake transform

{\mathcal{S}}^{\iota}:\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}_{3}},\bar{S}_{3}-\ord,\tilde{\iota}}\to\mathbb{T}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}_{3}},\bar{S}_{3}-\ord,\iota}
descends to a
homomorphism

\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}_{3}},\bar{S}_{3}-\ord,\tilde{\iota}}\left(H^{d}\left(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}^{\vee}\right)_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}(\mathfrak{m}^{\vee})}^{\ord^{\vee}}\right)\to\mathbb{T}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}_{3}},\bar{S}_{3}-\ord,\iota}\left(\mathbb{H}^{d}\left(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}_{1}}}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{U}(\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}_{2}})\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}_{3}}}^{\vee}\right)_{\mathfrak{m}^{\vee}}\right),

where
\mathbb{H}^{d}
denotes the degree
d
hypercohomology.
Moreover, we have an injection

\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}_{3}},\bar{S}_{3}-\ord,\tilde{\iota}}\left(H^{d}\left(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}^{\vee}\right)_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}(\mathfrak{m}^{\vee})}^{\ord^{\vee}}\right)\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}_{3}},\bar{S}_{3}-\ord,\tilde{\iota}}\left(H^{d}\left(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}^{\vee}[1/p]\right)_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}(\mathfrak{m}^{\vee})}^{\ord^{\vee}}\right)

and the target is isomorphic, via Poincaré duality, to
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}_{3}},\bar{S}_{3}-\ord}\left(H^{d}\left(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}[1/p]\right)_{\tilde{\iota}^{*}{\mathcal{S}}^{*}(\mathfrak{m}^{\vee})}^{\ord}\right)
.

Proof.

With the ingredients mentioned in the preamble, this is a straightforward modification of the proof of Proposition
4.2.2
.
∎

Note that we have
\overline{\rho}_{\tilde{\iota}^{*}{\mathcal{S}}^{*}(\mathfrak{m}^{\vee})}=\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}(-n)\oplus\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\vee,c}(1-n)
.

Assume we are given a non-Eisenstein maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathbb{T}

with
\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}:={\mathcal{S}}^{*}(\mathfrak{m})
, and a subset
\bar{S}\subseteq\bar{S}_{p}
.
We will use the following notation:

A(K,\lambda,q):=\mathbb{T}^{\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}_{\bar{S}}^{w^{P}_{0}},\bar{S}-\ord}_{w^{P}_{0}}\left(H^{q}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda})_{\mathfrak{m}}\right),

A(K,\lambda,q,m):=\mathbb{T}^{\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}_{\bar{S}}^{w^{P}_{0}},\bar{S}-\ord}_{w^{P}_{0}}\left(H^{q}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}/\varpi^{m})_{\mathfrak{m}}\right),\ \mathrm{and}

\widetilde{A}(\widetilde{K},\tilde{\lambda},\bar{S}):=\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}_{\bar{S}}^{w^{P}_{0}},\bar{S}-\ord}\left(H^{d}(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}})^{\ord}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}\right).

Given a neat compact open subgroup
K\subset\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,f})
and an integer

m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
, define the subgroup
K(m,\bar{S})\subset K
by setting

K(m,\bar{S})_{v}:=K_{v}\cap\left\{(1_{n})\text{ mod }\varpi_{v}^{m}\right\}\subset\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{F_{v}})

if
v
is a
p
-adic place of
F
which lies above a place in
\bar{S}
, and

K(m,\bar{S})_{v}:=K_{v}
otherwise.
Also, given a good subgroup
\widetilde{K}\subset\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})
and an integer
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
,
define the good subgroup
\widetilde{K}(m,\bar{S})\subset\widetilde{K}
by setting

\widetilde{K}(m,\bar{S})_{\bar{v}}:=\widetilde{K}_{\bar{v}}\cap\left\{\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1_{n}&*\\
0&1_{n}\end{array}\right)\text{ mod }\varpi_{\tilde{v}}^{m}\right\}\subset\GL_{2n}({\mathcal{O}}_{F_{\tilde{v}}})=\widetilde{G}({\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}_{\bar{v}}})

if
\bar{v}
is a
p
-adic place of
F^{+}
contained in
\bar{S}
, and
\widetilde{K}(m,\bar{S})_{\bar{v}}:=\widetilde{K}_{\bar{v}}

otherwise.

Lemma 4.2.5
.

Assume that the subset
\bar{S}\subset\bar{S}_{p}
has the following property:

\sum_{\bar{v}\not\in\bar{S}}[F^{+}_{\bar{v}}:{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}]\geq\frac{1}{2}[F^{+}:{\mathbb{Q}}]
.
Let
q\in\left[\left\lfloor{\frac{d}{2}}\right\rfloor,d-1\right]
. Then

d-q\leq\sum_{\bar{v}\not\in\bar{S}}n^{2}[F^{+}_{\bar{v}}:{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}]
.

Proof.

If
d
is odd, then so is
[F^{+}:{\mathbb{Q}}]
, and we have

d-q\leq\frac{d+1}{2}\leq\frac{d+n^{2}}{2}\leq\sum_{\bar{v}\not\in\bar{S}}n^{2}[F^{+}_{\bar{v}}:{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}].

If
d
is even, then

d-q\leq\frac{d}{2}\leq\sum_{\bar{v}\not\in\bar{S}}n^{2}[F^{+}_{\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}}:{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}]
.
∎

Proposition 4.2.6
.

Let
\bar{v}
,
\bar{v}^{\prime}
be two distinct
places of
\bar{S}_{p}
. Let

\bar{S}_{1}:=\{\bar{v}^{\prime}\}
,
\bar{S}_{3}:=\{\bar{v}\}
and
\bar{S}_{2}
be their complement
in
\bar{S}_{p}
. Let
\lambda\in\left({\mathbb{Z}}^{n}_{+}\right)^{\Hom(F,E)}

be a highest weight for
G
. Let
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
be an integer and

\widetilde{K}\subset\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})
be a good subgroup. Assume
that the following conditions are satisfied.

(1)

We have

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}\in\bar{S}_{p}\\
\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}\not=\bar{v},\bar{v}^{\prime}\end{subarray}}[F^{+}_{\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}}:{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}]\geq\frac{1}{2}[F^{+}:{\mathbb{Q}}].

(2)

For each
p
-adic place
\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}
of
F^{+}
not equal to
\bar{v}
(including
\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}=\bar{v}^{\prime}
),
we have
U({\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}_{\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}}})\subset\widetilde{K}_{\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}}
and
\widetilde{K}_{\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}}=\widetilde{K}(m,\bar{S}_{1}\cup\bar{S}_{2})_{\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}}
; in other words
\widetilde{K}_{\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}}\subset\left\{\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1_{n}&*\\
0&1_{n}\end{array}\right)\text{ mod }\varpi_{\tilde{v}^{\prime\prime}}^{m}\right\}
for each of these places.
Finally, we
have
\widetilde{K}_{\bar{v}}=\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}_{\bar{v}}^{w^{P}_{0}}
corresponding to the standard parabolic
\overline{Q}_{\bar{v}}^{w^{P}_{0}}\subset P_{F^{+}_{\bar{v}}}
with Levi subgroup
Q_{\bar{v}}^{w^{P}_{0}}\cap G(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})
.

(3)

For each embedding
\tau:F\hookrightarrow E
inducing the place
\bar{v}
or
\bar{v}^{\prime}

of
F^{+}
, we have
-\lambda_{\tau c,1}-\lambda_{\tau,1}\geq 0
.

(4)

\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathbb{T}
is a non-Eisenstein maximal ideal such that
\bar{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}

is decomposed generic.

Define a weight
\tilde{\lambda}\in({\mathbb{Z}}^{2n}_{+})^{\Hom(F^{+},E)}
as follows: if

\tau:F^{+}\hookrightarrow E
does not induce either
\bar{v}
or
\bar{v}^{\prime}
, set
\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}=0
.
If
\tau
induces
\bar{v}
or
\bar{v}^{\prime}
, set
\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}=(-\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}c},\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}})

(identification as in (
2.1.4
))
19
19

19

The condition (3) in the statement
of the Proposition guarantees that
\tilde{\lambda}
will be dominant.
.
Set
K:=(\widetilde{K}^{\bar{v}}\cap G({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f}^{\bar{v}}))\cdot({\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\cap G(F^{+}_{\bar{v}}))
, identified in the usual way with a neat subgroup of
\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,f})
.

Let
q\in\left[\left\lfloor{\frac{d}{2}}\right\rfloor,d-1\right]
. Then there exists an integer
m^{\prime}\geq m
, an integer
N\geq 1
,
a nilpotent ideal
J\subset A(K,\lambda,q,m)
satisfying
J^{N}=0
, and a
commutative diagram

\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}^{w^{P}_{0}}_{\bar{v}},\{\bar{v}\}-\ord}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{S}}^{w^{P}_{0}}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{A}\left(\widetilde{K}(m^{\prime},\bar{S}_{2}),\tilde{\lambda},\bar{v}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{T}_{w^{P}_{0}}^{\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}^{w^{P}_{0}}_{\bar{v}},\{\bar{v}\}-\ord}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A(K,\lambda,q,m)/J.}

Moreover, the integer
N
can be chosen to only depend on
n
and on
[F^{+}:{\mathbb{Q}}]
.

Proof.

To simplify notation, we set
{\mathbb{T}}:=\mathbb{T}_{w^{P}_{0}}^{\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}^{w^{P}_{0}}_{\bar{v}},\{\bar{v}\}-\ord}
,
\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}:=\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}^{w^{P}_{0}}_{\bar{v}},\{\bar{v}\}-\ord}
. We would like to show that there exist non-negative integers
m^{\prime}\geq m

and
N
such that

{\mathcal{S}}^{w^{P}_{0}}\left(\mathrm{Ann}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}}\ H^{d}(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}(m^{\prime},\bar{S}_{2})},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}})^{\ord}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}\right)^{N}\subseteq\mathrm{Ann}_{\mathbb{T}}\ H^{q}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}/\varpi^{m})_{\mathfrak{m}}.

This is similar to
[
ACC+18
, Prop. 4.4.1]
;
we will apply Proposition
4.2.2
repeatedly, which plays
the same role as Proposition 4.3.1 in
loc. cit.
.
The argument is subtle, for two reasons.

•

We need to work with
{\mathcal{O}}
-coefficients
in order to access the Hecke algebras
\widetilde{A}\left(\widetilde{K}(m^{\prime},\bar{S}_{2}),\tilde{\lambda},\bar{v}\right)
,
whilst the Hecke algebra
A(K,\lambda,q,m)
acts on cohomology with torsion coefficients.

•

The spectral sequence computing the hypercohomology in Proposition
4.2.2

is not known to degenerate.

The second issue does not occur in
[
ACC+18
]
.
To deal with both these issues, we will argue by descending induction
on the degree
q
. The induction hypothesis is the following.

Hypothesis 4.2.7
.

Let
q\in\left[\left\lfloor{\frac{d}{2}}\right\rfloor,d-1\right]
.
Then the Proposition holds for every cohomological degree
i\in[q+1,d-1]

and for every
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
. Moreover, the integer
N
can be chosen
to depend only on
n
,
[F^{+}:{\mathbb{Q}}]
and
q
.

Note that the induction hypothesis is satisfied automatically for
q=d-1
.
Assume that the hypothesis is satisfied for some
q\in\left[\left\lfloor{\frac{d}{2}}\right\rfloor,d-1\right]
.
We will prove the Proposition for
q
, which will imply the induction hypothesis for
q-1
.

Fix
m
,
\widetilde{K}
and
\lambda
as in the statement. Let
M=M(m)\geq m
be the integer
guaranteed by Lemma
2.3.17
. We first increase the level,
going from
X_{K}
to
X_{K(M,\bar{S}_{2})}
. This uses the same argument as in the
proof of
[
ACC+18
, Prop. 4.4.1]
, that we briefly recall here. Firstly, Poincaré
duality gives an equality

\mathrm{Ann}_{\mathbb{T}}\ H^{q}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}/\varpi^{m})_{\mathfrak{m}}=\iota\left(\mathrm{Ann}_{\mathbb{T}^{\iota}}\ H^{d-1-q}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}^{\vee}/\varpi^{m})_{\mathfrak{m}^{\vee}}\right),

where
\mathfrak{m}^{\vee}=\iota(\mathfrak{m})\subset\mathbb{T}^{T}
and
{\mathbb{T}}^{\iota}:=\mathbb{T}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}_{3}},\bar{S}_{3}-\ord,\iota}_{w^{P}_{0}}
.

The Hochschild–Serre spectral
sequence gives an inclusion

\prod_{i=0}^{d-q-1}\mathrm{Ann}_{\mathbb{T}^{\iota}}\ H^{i}(X_{K(M,\bar{S}_{2})},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}^{\vee}/\varpi^{m})_{\mathfrak{m}^{\vee}}\subset\mathrm{Ann}_{\mathbb{T}^{\iota}}\ H^{d-1-q}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}^{\vee}/\varpi^{m})_{\mathfrak{m}^{\vee}},

and Poincaré duality gives

\prod_{i=q}^{d-1}\mathrm{Ann}_{\mathbb{T}}\ H^{i}(X_{K(M,\bar{S}_{2})},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}/\varpi^{m})_{\mathfrak{m}}\subset\mathrm{Ann}_{\mathbb{T}}\ H^{q}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}/\varpi^{m})_{\mathfrak{m}}.

We deal with the terms for
i\geq q+1
using induction.
(See the last paragraph of the proof for more details on
how one applies the induction hypothesis.) Therefore, we are left
with the term for
i=q
and we may assume that
K=K(M,\bar{S}_{2})
.

Now, note that the
\mathbb{T}
-algebra

A(K,\lambda,q,m)
does not depend on
\lambda_{\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}}
for
\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}\not=\bar{v}
,
because the level
K_{\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}}
is deep enough that the action on
{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}/\varpi^{m}

is trivial. Therefore, we replace
{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}/\varpi^{m}
by

{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{v}}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{v}^{\prime}}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}^{d-q}_{U}(\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}_{2}},m).

This is non-zero by Lemmas
2.3.17
and
4.2.5
.
More generally, for any non-negative integer

j\leq\sum_{\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}\in\bar{S}_{2}}n^{2}[F^{+}_{\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}}:{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}]
,
set

{\mathcal{V}}^{j}:={\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{v}}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{v}^{\prime}}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}^{j}_{U}(\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}_{2}})
.
We have a short exact sequence of
\mathbb{T}
-modules

0\to H^{q}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}^{d-q})_{\mathfrak{m}}/\varpi^{m}\to H^{q}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}^{d-q}/\varpi^{m})_{\mathfrak{m}}\to H^{q+1}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}^{d-q})_{\mathfrak{m}}[\varpi^{m}]\to 0,

where we are interested in understanding the Hecke algebra

A(K,\lambda,q,m)
acting on the term in the middle. We can understand the
Hecke algebra acting on the
\varpi^{m}
-torsion in
H^{q+1}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}^{d-q})_{\mathfrak{m}}

using the induction hypothesis: the argument is identical to the argument used
in the proof of
[
ACC+18
, Prop. 4.4.1]
. Therefore, we are
left with understanding the faithful quotient of
\mathbb{T}
acting on

H^{q}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}^{d-q})_{\mathfrak{m}}/\varpi^{m}
.

There is a
\mathbb{T}
-equivariant spectral sequence

(4.2.3)

E_{2}^{i,j}({\mathcal{O}}):=H^{i}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}^{j})_{\mathfrak{m}}\Rightarrow\mathbb{H}^{i+j}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{v}}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{v}^{\prime}}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{U}(\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}_{2}}))_{\mathfrak{m}}.

If we knew that this spectral sequence
degenerates on the
E_{2}
page, we would deduce that

H^{q}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}^{d-q})_{\mathfrak{m}}

is a
\mathbb{T}
-equivariant
subquotient of
\mathbb{H}^{d}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}_{1}}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{S}_{3}}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{U}(\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}_{2}}))_{\mathfrak{m}}
,
and we would win by Proposition
4.2.2
.
However, it is not clear, in this generality, whether the spectral
sequence (
4.2.3
) degenerates.
Instead, we compare it to the following
\mathbb{T}
-equivariant spectral sequence,
whose terms are
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}
-modules

(4.2.4)

E_{2}^{i,j}({\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}):=H^{i}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}^{j}/\varpi^{m})_{\mathfrak{m}}\Rightarrow\mathbb{H}^{i+j}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{v}}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\bar{v}^{\prime}}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{U}(\tilde{\lambda}_{\bar{S}_{2}},m))_{\mathfrak{m}}.

Since
{K}={K}(M,\bar{S}_{2})
, Lemma
2.3.17
implies
that all the differentials in (
4.2.4
) are zero.
Let

\phi_{r}^{i,j}:E_{r}^{i,j}({\mathcal{O}})\to E_{r}^{i,j}({\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m})

be the natural,
\mathbb{T}
-equivariant map between
the spectral sequences (
4.2.3
) and (
4.2.4
).
Let
F_{r}^{i,j}:=\mathrm{Im}(\phi_{r}^{i,j})
. When
r=2
, we have
F_{2}^{i,j}=E_{2}^{i,j}({\mathcal{O}})/\varpi^{m}
.
For
r\geq 3
, we at least have a surjection
E_{r}^{i,j}({\mathcal{O}})/\varpi^{m}\twoheadrightarrow F_{r}^{i,j}
,
because
F_{r}^{i,j}
is an
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}
-module.

With this new notation, we are interested in relating
\mathrm{Ann}_{\mathbb{T}}(F_{2}^{q,d-q})
to

\mathrm{Ann}_{\mathbb{T}}(E_{\infty}^{d}({\mathcal{O}}))
.
For any
r\geq 2
, let
d_{r}^{-}:E_{r}^{q-r,d-q+1-r}\to E_{r}^{q,d-q}
and
d_{r}^{+}:E_{r}^{q,d-q}\to E_{r}^{q+r,d-q+1-r}

denote the
r
th differentials. Because all the differentials in (
4.2.4
) are zero,
we have that
\mathrm{Im}(d_{r}^{-})\subseteq\mathrm{Ker}(\phi_{r}^{q,d-q})
. Since
\phi^{q,d-q}_{r}

induces
\phi^{q,d-q}_{r+1}
, we deduce that we have an injection

F^{q,d-q}_{r+1}=\mathrm{Ker}(d_{r}^{+})/\left(\mathrm{Im}(d_{r}^{-})+\mathrm{Ker}(d_{r}^{+})\cap\mathrm{Ker}(\phi_{r}^{q,d-q})\right)\hookrightarrow F^{q,d-q}_{r}=E^{q,d-q}_{r}/\mathrm{Ker}(\phi_{r}^{q,d-q}).

Moreover, the cokernel of this injection becomes identified, under the map induced by
d^{+}_{r}
,
with
\mathrm{Im}(d^{+}_{r})/d^{+}_{r}(\mathrm{Ker}(\phi_{r}^{q,d-q}))
. Since
(\varpi^{m})\subseteq\mathrm{Ker}(\phi_{r}^{q,d-q})
,
the latter is a quotient of
\mathrm{Im}(d^{+}_{r})/\varpi^{m}
. By Lemma
2.3.18
,
there exists some
m^{\prime}_{r}\geq m
such that the latter is a subquotient of
E_{2}^{q+r,d-q+1-r}({\mathcal{O}})/\varpi^{m^{\prime}_{r}}
,
or even of
E_{2}^{q+r,d-q+1-r}({\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m^{\prime}_{r}})
.
We therefore have an inclusion

\mathrm{Ann}_{\mathbb{T}}\ E_{\infty}^{d}({\mathcal{O}})\cdot\prod_{r=2}^{d-q-1}\mathrm{Ann}_{\mathbb{T}}\ E_{2}^{q+r,d+1-q-r}({\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m^{\prime}_{r}})\subseteq\mathrm{Ann}_{\mathbb{T}}\ F_{2}^{q,d-q}.

For the
E_{\infty}^{d}({\mathcal{O}})
term, Proposition
4.2.2
implies that
there is an inclusion

{\mathcal{S}}^{w^{P}_{0}}\left(\mathrm{Ann}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}}\ H^{d}(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}})^{\ord}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}\right)\subseteq\mathrm{Ann}_{\mathbb{T}}\ E_{\infty}^{d}({\mathcal{O}}).

For each
E_{2}^{q+r,d+1-q-r}({\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m^{\prime}_{r}})=H^{q+r}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}^{d+1-q-r}/\varpi^{m^{\prime}_{r}})_{\mathfrak{m}}
,
we use the argument above via Poincaré duality and the Hochschild–Serre
spectral sequence to increase the level to
{K}(m^{\prime}_{r},\bar{S}_{2})
.
We then apply the induction hypothesis.

Each time we apply the induction hypothesis, we find some integer

m^{\prime}_{i}\geq m
and some nilpotence degree
N_{i}
, which can be bounded in terms of
\dim(X_{K})
, for
i
running over some finite index set
I
whose size can also be bounded in terms of
\dim(X_{K})
. To find a common
m^{\prime}\geq m
, we let
m^{\prime}:=\mathrm{sup}_{i\in I}m^{\prime}_{i}
.
For each
i
, we have

\mathrm{Ann}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}}\ H^{d}(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}(m^{\prime},\bar{S}_{2})},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}})^{\ord}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}\subseteq\mathrm{Ann}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}}\ H^{d}(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}(m^{\prime}_{i},\bar{S}_{2})},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}})^{\ord}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}},

because this is true rationally and the cohomology groups are torsion-free.
We then let
J
denote the image of the ideal

{\mathcal{S}}^{w^{P}_{0}}\left(\mathrm{Ann}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}}\ H^{d}(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}(m^{\prime},\bar{S}_{2})},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}})^{\ord}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}\right)

in
A(K,\lambda,q,m)
.
To find an appropriate nilpotence degree
N
, we set
N=1+\sum_{i}N_{i}
.
∎

Again we have a similar statement with dual coefficients. We introduce some more notation, depending on a decomposition
\bar{S}_{p}=\bar{S}_{1}\cup\bar{S}_{2}\cup\bar{S}_{3}
:

A^{\vee}(K,\lambda,q):=\mathbb{T}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}_{3}},\bar{S}_{3}-\ord,\iota}\left(H^{q}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}^{\vee})_{\mathfrak{m}^{\vee}}\right),

A^{\vee}(K,\lambda,q,m):=\mathbb{T}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}_{3}},\bar{S}_{3}-\ord,\iota}\left(H^{q}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}^{\vee}/\varpi^{m})_{\mathfrak{m}^{\vee}}\right),\ \mathrm{and}

\widetilde{A}^{\vee}(\widetilde{K},\tilde{\lambda},\bar{S}_{3}):=\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{S}_{3}},\bar{S}_{3}-\ord,\tilde{\iota}}\left(H^{d}(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}}^{\vee})^{\ord^{\vee}}_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}{\mathfrak{m}^{\vee}}}\right).

Proposition 4.2.8
.

Let
\bar{v}
,
\bar{v}^{\prime}
be two distinct
places of
\bar{S}_{p}
. Let

\bar{S}_{1}:=\{\bar{v}^{\prime}\}
,
\bar{S}_{3}:=\{\bar{v}\}
and
\bar{S}_{2}
be their complement
in
\bar{S}_{p}
. Let
\lambda\in\left({\mathbb{Z}}^{n}_{+}\right)^{\Hom(F,E)}

be a highest weight for
G
. Let
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
be an integer and

\widetilde{K}\subset\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})
be a good subgroup. Assume
that the following conditions are satisfied.

(1)

We have

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}\in\bar{S}_{p}\\
\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}\not=\bar{v},\bar{v}^{\prime}\end{subarray}}[F^{+}_{\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}}:{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}]\geq\frac{1}{2}[F^{+}:{\mathbb{Q}}].

(2)

For each
p
-adic place
\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}
of

not equal to
\bar{v}
(including
\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}=\bar{v}^{\prime}
),
we have
U({\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}_{\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}}})\subset\widetilde{K}_{\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}}
and
\widetilde{K}_{\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}}=\widetilde{K}(m,\bar{S}_{1}\cup\bar{S}_{2})_{\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}}
; in other words
\widetilde{K}_{\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}}\subset\left\{\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1_{n}&*\\
0&1_{n}\end{array}\right)\text{ mod }\varpi_{\tilde{v}^{\prime\prime}}^{m}\right\}
for each of these places.
Finally, we
have
\widetilde{K}_{\bar{v}}={{\mathcal{Q}}}_{\bar{v}}
corresponding to the standard parabolic
{Q}_{\bar{v}}\subset P_{F^{+}_{\bar{v}}}
.

(3)

For each embedding
\tau:F\hookrightarrow E
inducing the place
\bar{v}
or
\bar{v}^{\prime}

of
F^{+}
, we have
\lambda_{\tau c,n}+\lambda_{\tau,n}\geq 0
.

(4)

\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathbb{T}
is a non-Eisenstein maximal ideal such that
\bar{\rho}_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}(\mathfrak{m}^{\vee})}

is decomposed generic.

Define a weight
\tilde{\lambda}\in({\mathbb{Z}}^{2n}_{+})^{\Hom(F^{+},E)}
as follows: if

\tau:F^{+}\hookrightarrow E
does not induce either
\bar{v}
or
\bar{v}^{\prime}
, set
\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}=0
.
If
\tau
induces
\bar{v}
or
\bar{v}^{\prime}
, set
\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}=(\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}},-\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}c})
.
Set
K:=\widetilde{K}\cap G({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})
, identified in the usual way with a neat subgroup of
\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,f})
.

Let
q\in\left[\left\lfloor{\frac{d}{2}}\right\rfloor,d-1\right]
. Then there exists an integer
m^{\prime}\geq m
, an integer
N\geq 1
,
a nilpotent ideal

satisfying
J^{N}=0
, and a
commutative diagram

\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{{{\mathcal{Q}}}_{\bar{v}},\{\bar{v}\}-\ord,\tilde{\iota}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{S}}^{\iota}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{A}^{\vee}\left(\widetilde{K}(m^{\prime},\bar{S}_{2}),\tilde{\lambda},\bar{v}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{T}^{{{\mathcal{Q}}}_{\bar{v}},\{\bar{v}\}-\ord,\iota}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\vee}(K,\lambda,q,m)/J.}

Moreover, the integer
N
can be chosen to only depend on
n
and on
[F^{+}:{\mathbb{Q}}]
.

We will now be able to reduce questions about Galois representations with coefficients in the torsion Hecke algebras
A(K,\lambda,q,m)
to understanding the properties of the Galois representations with coefficients in the
p
-torsion free Hecke algebras
\widetilde{A}(\cdots)
and
\widetilde{A}^{\vee}(\cdots)
. To do this, we need some results about automorphic Galois representations in characteristic 0.

For the statement of the next proposition, recall that we have introduced Hecke operators
\widetilde{U}_{v}^{k}
for a place
v\in S_{p}
in §
3.1
.

Proposition 4.2.9
.

Let
\bar{v}
be a
p
-adic place of
F^{+}
,
let
\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathbb{T}_{w^{P}_{0}}^{\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}_{\bar{v}}^{w^{P}_{0}},\bar{v}-\ord}
be a non-Eisenstein maximal ideal in the
support of some
H^{*}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda})
,
and set
\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}:=({\mathcal{S}}^{w^{P}_{0}})^{*}(\mathfrak{m})
, a maximal
ideal of
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}_{\bar{v}}^{w^{P}_{0}},\bar{v}-\ord}
. Fix
v|\bar{v}
in
F
and suppose that
\widetilde{U}_{v}^{k}\notin\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}
for
1\leq k\leq t
.

Assume that
\pi
is a cuspidal automorphic representation of
\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+}})
,
\iota
is an isomorphism
\iota:\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}{\mathbb{C}}
and
\pi
is
\iota
-
\overline{Q}_{\bar{v}}^{w^{P}_{0}}
-ordinary of weight
\tilde{\lambda}
. Suppose moreover that the Hecke eigenvalues on
(\iota^{-1}\pi^{\infty})^{\widetilde{K},\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}_{\bar{v}}^{w^{P}_{0}}-\ord}
come from a map

f:\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}_{\bar{v}}^{w^{P}_{0}}-\ord}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}\to\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p},

where the superscript ‘
\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}_{\bar{v}}^{w^{P}_{0}}-\ord
’ denotes that we replace the local factor
\pi_{\bar{v}}^{\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}_{\bar{v}}^{w^{P}_{0}}}
with its one-dimensional
\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}_{\bar{v}}^{w^{P}_{0}}
-ordinary subspace.

We have the associated
p
-adic Galois representation

r_{\iota}(\pi):G_{F}\to\GL_{2n}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p})
(the existence of
f
implies that we have an isomorphism of semi-simplified reductions
\overline{r}_{\iota}(\pi)\cong\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
). Consider the
(n,n)
-block decomposition

r_{\iota}(\pi)|_{G_{F_{\tilde{v}}}}\simeq\begin{pmatrix}r_{1}(\pi)&*\\
0&r_{2}(\pi)\end{pmatrix}

guaranteed by Theorem
3.1.2
(noting that
r_{1}(\pi),r_{2}(\pi)
may be futher decomposed according to the shape of
\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}_{\bar{v}}^{w^{P}_{0}}
).
For
i=1,2
, assume that
E
is large enough that
r_{i}(\pi)

can be defined over it, via the embedding
E\hookrightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
coming from
f
, and let
\overline{r_{i}(\pi)}
be the semi-simplification of the reduction modulo

\varpi
of
r_{i}(\pi)
. Then

\det\overline{r_{1}(\pi)}\left({\operatorname{Art}}_{F_{{v}}}(\varpi_{{v}})\right)=\det\bar{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}({\operatorname{Art}}_{F_{{v}}}(\varpi_{{v}}))\ \mathrm{and}

\det\overline{r_{2}(\pi)}\left({\operatorname{Art}}_{F_{{v}}}(\varpi_{{v}})\right)=\det\left(\bar{\rho}^{\vee,c}_{\mathfrak{m}}(1-2n)\right)({\operatorname{Art}}_{F_{{v}}}(\varpi_{{v}})).

Proof.

By Theorem
3.1.2
,

\det r_{1}(\pi)\left({\operatorname{Art}}_{F_{\tilde{v}}}(\varpi_{\tilde{v}})\right)\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}

is equal to
\epsilon_{p}^{\frac{n(1-n)}{2}}(\mathrm{Art}_{F_{v}}(\varpi_{v}))

times the eigenvalue of
\widetilde{U}_{{v},n}
acting on
the
\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}_{\bar{v}}^{w^{P}_{0}}
-ordinary subspace of
\iota^{-1}\pi^{\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}_{\bar{v}}^{w^{P}_{0}}}
.
By the description of the map
{\mathcal{S}}^{w^{P}_{0}}
, the reduction
of this eigenvalue modulo
\varpi
is equal to
the image of
U_{{v}}
in
\mathbb{T}_{w^{P}_{0}}^{\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}_{\bar{v}}^{w^{P}_{0}},\bar{v}-\ord}/\mathfrak{m}
.
By Lemma
2.1.21
,

\det\bar{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}({\operatorname{Art}}_{F_{{v}}}(\varpi_{{v}}))

is equal to
\bar{\epsilon}_{p}^{\frac{n(1-n)}{2}}(\mathrm{Art}_{F_{v}}(\varpi_{v}))

times the eigenvalue of
U_{{v}}
acting
on
H^{*}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}/\varpi)_{\mathfrak{m}}
,
so we obtain the first equation.
For the second equation, let
\overline{r_{\iota}(\pi)}
be the
semi-simplification of the reduction modulo

\varpi
of
r_{\iota}(\pi)
.
The same line of reasoning
implies that
\det\overline{r_{\iota}(\pi)}\left({\operatorname{Art}}_{F_{{v}}}(\varpi_{{v}})\right)
is equal to

\bar{\epsilon}_{p}^{n(1-2n)}(\mathrm{Art}_{F_{v}}(\varpi_{v}))
times
the image of
U_{{v}}\cdot U_{{v}^{c}}^{-1}
in
\mathbb{T}^{\bar{v}-\ord}/\mathfrak{m}
.
We conclude by the first equation and by
another application of Lemma
2.1.21
.
∎

To proceed, we recall the notion of a CTG weight from
[
ACC+18
, Def. 4.3.5]
.

Definition 4.2.10
.

A weight
\tilde{\lambda}\in({\mathbb{Z}}^{2n}_{+})^{\Hom(F^{+},E)}

is CTG (“cohomologically trivial for
G
”) if it satisfies the following condition

•

Given
w\in W^{P}
, define
\lambda_{w}=w(\tilde{\lambda}+\rho)-\rho
,
viewed as an element of
({\mathbb{Z}}^{n}_{+})^{\Hom(F,E)}
in the usual way. For each

w\in W^{P}
and
i_{0}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, there exists
\tau\in\Hom(F,E)
such that
\lambda_{w,\tau}-\lambda_{w,\tau c}^{\vee}\not=(i_{0},i_{0},\dots,i_{0})
.

An important application of the CTG assumption is the following variant of
[
ACC+18
, Theorem 2.4.11]
:

Proposition 4.2.11
.

Let
\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathbb{T}^{T}
be a non-Eisenstein
maximal ideal. Fix a place
\bar{v}\in\bar{S}_{p}
and a standard parabolic
Q_{\bar{v}}\subset P_{\bar{v}}
and suppose
\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}
is a maximal ideal of
\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}},\{\bar{v}\}-\ord}
which extends
{\mathcal{S}}^{*}(\mathfrak{m})
. Let
\widetilde{K}\subset\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})
be a good subgroup such that
\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}
is in the support of
H^{*}(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}})^{\ord}
for a CTG weight
\tilde{\lambda}
. Suppose that
\widetilde{U}_{v}^{k}\notin\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}
for
1\leq k\leq t
(in other words, these Hecke operators act with unit eigenvalues on
H^{*}(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}})^{\ord}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
). Let
d=\frac{1}{2}\dim_{{\mathbb{R}}}X^{\widetilde{G}}=n^{2}[F^{+}:{\mathbb{Q}}]
.

Then
H^{d}(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}})^{\ord}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}[1/p]
is a semisimple
\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}},\{\bar{v}\}-\ord}[1/p]
-module, and for every homomorphism

f:\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}},\{\bar{v}\}-\ord}(H^{d}(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\lambda}})^{\ord}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}})\to\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p},

and isomorphism
\iota:\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}\to{\mathbb{C}}
there exists a cuspidal automorphic representation
\pi
of
\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+}})
which is
\iota
-
Q_{\bar{v}}
-ordinary of weight
\tilde{\lambda}
such that
f
is associated to the Hecke eigenvalues on
(\iota^{-1}\pi^{\infty})^{\widetilde{K},{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}-\ord}
, where ‘
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}-\ord
’ indicates that we replace the local factor
\pi_{\bar{v}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}
with its one-dimensional
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}
-ordinary subspace.

Proof.

This follows from combining (the proof of)
[
ACC+18
, Theorem 2.4.11]
with the fact that the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}
-ordinary subspace of
\pi_{\bar{v}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}}
(which is all that contributes to cohomology localised at
\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}
) is one-dimensional, which is part of Theorem
3.1.2
.
∎

Remark 4.2.12
.

In Proposition
4.2.6
, we may assume
that the weight
\tilde{\lambda}
is CTG, without changing the Hecke algebra

A(K,\lambda,q,m)
. This is because
[
ACC+18
, Lemma 4.3.6]

shows that a weight can be ensured to be CTG by modifying it at only one embedding

\tau:F^{+}\hookrightarrow E
. Choose a
\tau
which induces the place
\bar{v}^{\prime}

of
F^{+}
. Because the level at
\bar{v}^{\prime}
is assumed to be deep enough in Proposition
4.2.6
,
we may modify
\tilde{\lambda}_{\tau}=(-\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}c},\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}})

without changing the Hecke algebra
A(K,\lambda,q,m)
.

We can now apply the results of §
3
to obtain the main result of this section.

Proposition 4.2.13
.

Assume that
p
splits in an imaginary quadratic subfield of
F
. Let
K\subset G({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})=\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,f})
be a good subgroup and fix distinct places
\bar{v},\bar{v^{\prime}}\in S_{p}
. Let
\lambda
be a dominant weight for
G
.

Let
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
be an integer. Fix a standard parabolic
Q_{\bar{v}}\subset P_{\bar{v}}
, suppose that
K_{\bar{v}}={\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\cap G(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})
and let
\mathfrak{m}\subset{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}},\{\bar{v}-\ord\}}
be a maximal ideal in the support of
H^{*}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}/\varpi^{m})
.

Fix
\tilde{v}|\bar{v}
in
F
. Using
\iota_{\tilde{v}}
, we identify
Q_{\bar{v}}
with a standard block-upper-triangular parabolic subgroup of
\GL_{2n}
corresponding to a decomposition
(n_{1},\ldots,n_{t})
of
2n
. Suppose that
n=n_{1}+\cdots+n_{r}
.

Assume that:

(1)

We have

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}\in\bar{S}_{p}\\
\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}\not=\bar{v},\bar{v}^{\prime}\end{subarray}}[F^{+}_{\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}}:{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}]\geq\frac{1}{2}[F^{+}:{\mathbb{Q}}].

(2)

\mathfrak{m}
is a non-Eisenstein maximal ideal such that
\bar{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}

is decomposed generic.

(3)

Let
v\notin T
be a finite place, with residue characteristic
l
. Then either

T
contains no
l
-adic places and
l
is unramified in
F
, or there is an
imaginary quadratic subfield of
F
in which
l
splits.

(4)

For all
\nu\in X_{Q_{\bar{v}}}
, the Hecke operator
[K_{\bar{v}}\nu(\varpi_{\bar{v}})K_{\bar{v}}]
is not contained in
\mathfrak{m}
.

Then for each
q\in[0,d-1]
there exists an integer
N\geq 1
, depending only on
n
and
[F^{+}:{\mathbb{Q}}]
, a nilpotent ideal
J
of
{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}},\{\bar{v}-\ord\}}(H^{q}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}/\varpi^{m})_{\mathfrak{m}})
with
J^{N}=0
and a continuous
n
-dimensional representation

\rho_{\mathfrak{m}}:G_{F,T}\to\GL_{n}({\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}},\{\bar{v}-\ord\}}(H^{q}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}/\varpi^{m})_{\mathfrak{m}})/J)

such that the following conditions are satisfied:

(1)

For each place
v\notin T
of
F
, the characteristic polynomial of
\rho_{\mathfrak{m}}(\mathrm{Frob}_{v})
is equal to the image of
P_{v}(X)
.

(2)

For
v|\bar{v}
, the representation
\rho_{\mathfrak{m}}|_{G_{F_{{v}}}}
has a lift to
\tilde{\rho}_{{v}}:G_{F_{{v}}}\to\GL_{n}(\widetilde{A})
, where
\widetilde{A}
is a finite flat local
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra equipped with a morphism

f:\widetilde{A}\to{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}},\{\bar{v}-\ord\}}(H^{q}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}/\varpi^{m})_{\mathfrak{m}})/J.

(3)

Inverting
p
, the lift
\tilde{\rho}_{{v}}[1/p]
is semistable with labelled Hodge–Tate weights
(\lambda_{\tau,n}<\cdots<\lambda_{\tau,1}+n-1)_{\tau:F_{{v}}\hookrightarrow E}
.

(4)

Furthermore, these semistable lifts satisfy

\tilde{\rho}_{\tilde{v}}[1/p]\simeq\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{\rho}_{\tilde{v},r+1}&*&\cdots&*\\
0&\tilde{\rho}_{\tilde{v},r+2}&\cdots&*\\
0&0&\ddots&*\\
0&0&\cdots&\tilde{\rho}_{\tilde{v},t}\end{pmatrix}\text{ and }\tilde{\rho}_{\tilde{v}^{c}}[1/p]\simeq\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{\rho}_{\tilde{v}^{c},r}&*&\cdots&*\\
0&\tilde{\rho}_{\tilde{v}^{c},r-1}&\cdots&*\\
0&0&\ddots&*\\
0&0&\cdots&\tilde{\rho}_{\tilde{v}^{c},1}\end{pmatrix}

where the representations
\tilde{\rho}_{v,j}:G_{F_{v}}\to\GL_{n_{j}}(\widetilde{A}[1/p])
are crystalline with labelled Hodge–Tate weights determined by the requirement that they are increasing from top left to bottom right.

(5)

For
j=r+1,\ldots,t
, the characters
\det\tilde{\rho}_{\tilde{v},j}
take values in
\tilde{A}
and their image under
f
is given by characters
\psi_{j}
determined by:

•

\prod_{j=r+1}^{k}\psi_{j}({\operatorname{Art}}_{F_{\tilde{v}}}(u))=\prod_{i=1}^{n_{r+1}+\cdots+n_{k}}\prod_{\tau:F_{\tilde{v}}\hookrightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}}\tau(u)^{-{\lambda}_{\tau,n-i+1}-i+1}
for
u\in{\mathcal{O}}_{F_{\tilde{v}}}^{\times}
.

•

\prod_{j=r+1}^{k}\psi_{j}({\operatorname{Art}}_{F_{\tilde{v}}}(\varpi_{\tilde{v}}))
is equal to
\epsilon_{p}^{\sum_{i=1}^{n_{r+1}+\cdots+n_{k}}(1-i)}({\operatorname{Art}}_{F_{\tilde{v}}}(\varpi_{\tilde{v}}))\widetilde{U}_{v}^{k-r}
.

Proof.

We already know the existence of
\rho_{\mathfrak{m}}
satisfying the first condition (local–global compatibility at unramified places), so we are free to enlarge
T
. As explained in the proof of
[
ACC+18
, Corollary 4.4.8]
, we may assume (applying a twisting argument) that
\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}=\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}\oplus\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\vee,c}(1-2n)
is decomposed generic, not just that
\bar{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}
is decomposed generic. We will use a similar twisting argument later in this proof. We can also use Hochschild–Serre to reduce to the case when
K_{\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}}\subset\left\{\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1_{n}&*\\
0&1_{n}\end{array}\right)\text{ mod }\varpi_{\tilde{v}^{\prime\prime}}^{m}\right\}
for each
\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}\in\overline{S}_{p}-\{\bar{v}\}
.
This means we can moreover assume that
\lambda_{\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}}=0
if
\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}\in\bar{S}_{p}-\{\bar{v}\}
.

Now we let
\widetilde{K}\subset\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})
be a good subgroup satisfying:

•

\widetilde{K}\cap G({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})=K
.

•

\widetilde{K}^{T}=\widetilde{G}(\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}^{T}_{F^{+}})
.

•

For each
\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}\in\overline{S}_{p}-\{\bar{v}\}
,
we have
U({\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}_{\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}}})\subset\widetilde{K}_{\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}}
and
\widetilde{K}_{\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}}\subset\left\{\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1_{n}&*\\
0&1_{n}\end{array}\right)\text{ mod }\varpi_{\tilde{v}^{\prime\prime}}^{m}\right\}
.

•

\widetilde{K}_{\bar{v}}=\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}_{\bar{v}}^{w^{P}_{0}}
(the corresponding standard parabolic has block sizes
(n_{r+1},\ldots,n_{t},n_{1},\ldots,n_{r})
).

Next, we use a twisting argument to reduce to the case when
-\lambda_{\tilde{\tau}c,1}-\lambda_{\tau,1}\geq 0
. Indeed, twisting by
\epsilon_{p}^{\mu}
moves us from the weight
\lambda
to the weight
\lambda^{\prime}:=(\lambda_{\tau,1}-\mu,\ldots,\lambda_{\tau,n}-\mu)_{\tau:F\hookrightarrow E}
(cf.
[
ACC+18
, Proposition 2.2.22]
), and we satisfy the desired condition if we take
\mu
to be sufficiently positive.

At this point we assume that
q\geq\lfloor\frac{d}{2}\rfloor
. We will handle small
q
at the end of the proof using Poincaré duality. We are now in a position to apply Proposition
4.2.6
. Following Remark
4.2.12
, we are free to modify
\lambda_{\bar{v}^{\prime}}
so that the weight
\tilde{\lambda}
is CTG. We have
A(K,\lambda,q,m)={\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}},\{\bar{v}-\ord\}}(H^{q}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}/\varpi^{m})_{\mathfrak{m}})
.

Suppose we have a continuous character
\overline{\psi}:G_{F}\to k^{\times}
(perhaps after extending
{\mathcal{O}}
), which is unramified at
S_{p}
, and let
\psi:G_{F}\to{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}
denote the Teichmüller lift of
\overline{\psi}
. Choose a finite set
{T}^{\prime}\supset{T}
(closed under complex conjugation) which also contains all the places where
\psi
is ramified, and a good normal subgroup
K^{\prime}\subset K
satisfying:

•

(K^{\prime})^{T^{\prime}-T}=K^{T^{\prime}-T}
.

•

K^{\prime}/K
is abelian of order prime to
p
.

•

For each place
v
of
F
, the restriction of
\psi|_{G_{F_{v}}}\circ{\operatorname{Art}}_{F_{v}}
to
\det(K^{\prime}_{v})
is trivial.

•

T^{\prime}
satisfies assumption (
3
) from the Proposition.

We will then consider the Hecke algebras for the twist

A(K^{\prime},\lambda,q,m,\psi):={\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}},\{\bar{v}-\ord\}}(H^{q}(X_{K^{\prime}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}/\varpi^{m})_{\mathfrak{m}(\psi)}),

(see
[
ACC+18
, §2.2.19]
for the definition of
\mathfrak{m}(\psi)
and note that
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}(\psi)}=\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}\otimes\overline{\psi}
). Establishing the proposition for any of these twists will imply it for the Hecke algebra
A(K,\lambda,q,m)
. We will always assume that
\overline{\psi}
is chosen so that
\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}(\psi)}}
remains decomposed generic.

We can, twisting by a suitable
\psi
if necessary, assume that the isomorphism classes of the irreducible constituents of
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}|_{G_{F_{\tilde{v}}}}
are disjoint from those of

\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\vee,c}(1-2n)|_{G_{F_{\tilde{v}}}}
.

Applying Proposition
4.2.6
, for each
\psi
as above we have a finite flat
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra
\widetilde{A}(\psi)
and a nilpotent ideal
J_{\psi}
with a map
f_{\psi}:\widetilde{A}(\psi)\to A(K^{\prime},\lambda,q,m,\psi)/J_{\psi}
. Using Propositions
4.2.9
and
4.2.11
, we deduce that there is a
\overline{Q}^{w^{P}_{0}}_{\bar{v}}
-ordinary Galois representation
\tilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}(\psi)}=\prod_{i=1}^{r}\tilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}(\psi)}^{i}:G_{F}\to\GL_{2n}(\widetilde{A}(\psi)[1/p])=\prod_{i=1}^{r}E
such that, for each
i
, the factor
\tilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}(\psi)}^{i}
comes with a
(n,n)
-block decomposition

\tilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}(\psi)}^{i}|_{G_{F_{\tilde{v}}}}\simeq\begin{pmatrix}r_{1,\psi}^{i}&*\\
0&r_{2,\psi}^{i}\end{pmatrix}

We can take a semisimplified reduction to the residue field and then, by Proposition
4.2.9
, we have

(4.2.5)

\det\overline{r^{i}_{1,\psi}}\left({\operatorname{Art}}_{F_{{\tilde{v}}}}(\varpi_{\tilde{v}})\right)=\det(\bar{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}\otimes\overline{\psi})({\operatorname{Art}}_{F_{\tilde{v}}}(\varpi_{\tilde{v}})).

Sub-lemma 1
.

Possibly after enlarging
{\mathcal{O}}
, there is a continuous character
\overline{\psi}:G_{F}\to k^{\times}
, unramified at
S_{p}
and with
\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}(\psi)}}
decomposed generic, such
that

(1)

the isomorphism classes of the irreducible constituents of

\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}(\psi)}|_{G_{F_{\tilde{v}}}}
are disjoint from those of

\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}(\psi)}^{\vee,c}(1-2n)|_{G_{F_{\tilde{v}}}}
;

(2)

for all
1\leq i\leq r
the isomorphism classes of the irreducible
constituents of the residual representation

\overline{r^{i}_{1,\psi}}
coincide with those of

\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}(\psi)}|_{G_{F_{\tilde{v}}}}
.

Proof.

We denote the irreducible constituents (with multiplicity) of

\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}|_{G_{F_{\tilde{v}}}}
by
S_{1},\ldots,S_{m}
, and let
d_{j}=\dim S_{j}

and
\delta_{j}=\det S_{j}
.
The irreducible constituents of
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\vee,c}(1-2n)|_{G_{F_{\tilde{v}}}}

are given by
T_{1},\ldots,T_{m}
where
T_{j}=S_{j}^{\vee,c}(1-2n)
. For each
\psi
, the
irreducible constituents of

\overline{r_{1,\psi}^{i}}
are given by a multiset
\{S_{j}\otimes\bar{\psi}:j\in I_{i}\}\coprod\{T_{j}\otimes\bar{\psi}^{\vee,c}:j\in J_{i}\}
for two subsets
I_{i},J_{i}\subset\{1,\ldots,m\}
with
\sum_{j\in I_{i}}d_{j}+\sum_{j\in J_{i}}d_{j}=n
.

Now comparing what this entails for
\det\overline{r^{i}_{1,\psi}}({\operatorname{Art}}_{F_{\tilde{v}}}(\varpi_{\tilde{v}}))
with the formula (
4.2.5
), we get:

\left(\bar{\psi}^{n}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\delta_{j}\right)({\operatorname{Art}}_{F_{\tilde{v}}}(\varpi_{\tilde{v}}))=\left(\prod_{j\in I_{i}}\bar{\psi}^{d_{j}}\delta_{j}\prod_{j\in J_{i}}(\overline{\psi}^{\vee,c})^{d_{j}}\delta_{j}^{\vee,c}\bar{\epsilon}_{p}^{d_{j}(1-2n)}\right)({\operatorname{Art}}_{F_{\tilde{v}}}(\varpi_{\tilde{v}}))

which rearranges to

\displaystyle(\bar{\psi}^{\vee,c})^{\sum_{j\in J_{i}}d_{j}}({\operatorname{Art}}_{F_{\tilde{v}}}(\varpi_{\tilde{v}}))

\displaystyle=\left(\bar{\psi}^{n-\sum_{j\in I_{i}}d_{j}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\delta_{j}\prod_{j\in I_{i}}\delta_{j}^{\vee}\prod_{j\in J_{i}}\delta_{j}^{c}\bar{\epsilon}_{p}^{d_{j}(2n-1)}\right)({\operatorname{Art}}_{F_{\tilde{v}}}(\varpi_{\tilde{v}}))

\displaystyle=\left(\bar{\psi}^{n-\sum_{j\in I_{i}}d_{j}}\cdot\delta(I_{i},J_{i})\right)({\operatorname{Art}}_{F_{\tilde{v}}}(\varpi_{\tilde{v}})),

where the character
\delta(I_{i},J_{i})
only depends on the (finitely many) possible choices of
I_{i}
and
J_{i}
. Now we
can choose
\overline{\psi}

so that any equation of this form forces
\sum_{j\in J_{i}}d_{j}=0
and

\sum_{j\in I_{i}}d_{j}=n
(whilst also preserving the first condition of the
sub-lemma). Indeed, since Grunwald–Wang allows us to find
\overline{\psi}
with specified behaviour at any finite set of places, we can choose
\overline{\psi}
locally trivial at a prime which is decomposed generic for
\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
and the pair
\overline{\psi}({\operatorname{Art}}_{F_{\tilde{v}}}(\varpi_{\tilde{v}})),\overline{\psi}({\operatorname{Art}}_{F_{\tilde{v}^{c}}}(\varpi_{\tilde{v}^{c}}))
arbitrary in
(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}^{\times})^{2}
. We choose this pair of elements with orders bigger than
n
, coprime to each other, and coprime to the orders of the elements
\delta(I_{i},J_{i})({\operatorname{Art}}_{F_{\tilde{v}}}(\varpi_{\tilde{v}}))
.

We conclude that
J_{i}=\emptyset
and
I_{i}=\{1,\ldots,m\}
for every
i
, so in other words the isomorphism classes of
the irreducible
constituents of the residual representation

\overline{r_{1,\psi}^{i}}
coincide with those of

\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}(\psi)}|_{G_{F_{\tilde{v}}}}
.
∎

We may now assume that the isomorphism classes of the irreducible constituents of

\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}|_{G_{F_{\tilde{v}}}}
are disjoint from those of

\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\vee,c}(1-2n)|_{G_{F_{\tilde{v}}}}
and that for all
1\leq i\leq r
the isomorphism classes of the irreducible
constituents of the residual representation

\overline{r^{i}_{1}}
coincide with those of

\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}|_{G_{F_{\tilde{v}}}}
. Applying Proposition
3.2.4
and Theorem
3.1.2
we deduce the statement of the Proposition for the Hecke algebra
A(K,\lambda,q,m)
.

It remains to handle the case
q<\lfloor\frac{d}{2}\rfloor
. Poincaré duality gives an isomorphism:

\iota:A(K,\lambda,q,m)\cong A^{\vee}(K,\lambda,d-1-q,m).

We can now run the same argument as above, using Proposition
4.2.8
. Since the Satake map in the dual degree shifting is untwisted, in this case we will have
2n
-dimensional
Q_{\bar{v}}
-ordinary representations
\tilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}^{\vee}}
lifting

\overline{\rho}_{\tilde{\iota}^{*}{\mathcal{S}}^{*}(\mathfrak{m}^{\vee})}=\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}(-n)\oplus\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\vee,c}(1-n)

with a decomposition

\tilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}^{\vee}}|_{G_{F_{\tilde{v}}}}\simeq\begin{pmatrix}r_{1}&*\\
0&r_{2}\end{pmatrix}

such that the
lower right
block
r_{2}
lifts
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}(-n)
. This is compatible with the following: in the dual case, we twist by a sufficiently
negative
power of cyclotomic to arrange that
\tilde{\lambda}=w^{P}_{0}\lambda
is dominant for
\widetilde{G}
(using our standard identification of weights for
G
and
\widetilde{G}
). Then
\tilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}^{\vee}}
is cohomological of weight
w^{P}_{0}\lambda
, so the
\tau
-labelled Hodge–Tate weights of
\tilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}^{\vee}}|_{G_{F_{\tilde{v}}}}
are given by

-\lambda_{\tau c,1}<\cdots<-\lambda_{\tau c,n}+n-1<\lambda_{\tau,n}+n<\cdots<\lambda_{\tau,1}+2n-1.

In particular, the Hodge–Tate weights of
r_{2}(n)
are as expected. This completes the proof for all values of
q
.
∎

4.2.14.
Local–global–compatibility using deformation rings

We formulate a consequence of Proposition
4.2.13
in terms of Galois deformation rings. The local deformation rings we need were defined in §
3.3
.

Theorem 4.2.15
.

Suppose that
F
is an imaginary CM field that contains an imaginary quadratic field. Let
p

be a prime which splits in an imaginary quadratic subfield of
F
. Let
T
be a finite set of finite places of
F
, which
contains
S_{p}
and which
is stable under complex conjugation, and such that the following condition is satisfied:

•

Let
v\notin T
be a finite place, with residue characteristic
\ell
. Then either

T
contains no
\ell
-adic places and
\ell
is unramified in
F
, or there exists an imaginary quadratic subfield of
F
in which
\ell
splits.

Let
K\subset G({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+},f})=\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}}_{F,f})
be a good subgroup with
K_{v}=\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{F_{v}})

\forall v\not\in T
.
Fix distinct places
\bar{v},\bar{v^{\prime}}\in S_{p}
. Let
\lambda
be a dominant weight for
G
.

We fix a standard parabolic
Q_{\bar{v}}\subset P_{\bar{v}}
and suppose we are in one of three cases:

(cr-ord)

\iota_{\tilde{v}}(Q_{\bar{v}})
is the standard parabolic given by the partition
(n,1,\ldots,1)
of
2n
.

(ord)

\iota_{\tilde{v}}(Q_{\bar{v}})=\mathrm{B}_{2n}

(cr)

Q_{\bar{v}}=P_{\bar{v}}
.

Suppose that
K_{\bar{v}}={\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}}\cap G(F^{+}_{\bar{v}})
and let
\mathfrak{m}\subset{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}},\{\bar{v}-\ord\}}
be a maximal ideal in the support of
H^{*}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda})
.

Assume that:

(1)

We have

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}\in\bar{S}_{p}\\
\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}\not=\bar{v},\bar{v}^{\prime}\end{subarray}}[F^{+}_{\bar{v}^{\prime\prime}}:{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}]\geq\frac{1}{2}[F^{+}:{\mathbb{Q}}].

(2)

\mathfrak{m}
is a non-Eisenstein maximal ideal such that
\bar{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}

is decomposed generic.

(3)

For all
\nu\in X_{Q_{\bar{v}}}
, the Hecke operator
[K_{\bar{v}}\nu(\varpi_{\bar{v}})K_{\bar{v}}]
is not contained in
\mathfrak{m}
.

Then there exists an integer
N\geq 1
, depending only on
n
and
[F^{+}:{\mathbb{Q}}]
, a nilpotent ideal
J
of
{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}},\{\bar{v}-\ord\}}(R\Gamma(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda})_{\mathfrak{m}})
with
J^{N}=0
and a continuous
n
-dimensional representation

\rho_{\mathfrak{m}}:G_{F,T}\to\GL_{n}({\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}},\{\bar{v}-\ord\}}(R\Gamma(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda})_{\mathfrak{m}})/J)

satisfying

•

For each place
v\notin T
of
F
,
\rho_{\mathfrak{m}}
is unramified and
the characteristic polynomial of
\rho_{\mathfrak{m}}(\mathrm{Frob}_{v})
is equal to the image of
P_{v}(X)
.

Moreover, the induced map
t_{\rho_{\mathfrak{m}}}:R^{\square}_{\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}}\to{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}},\{\bar{v}-\ord\}}(R\Gamma(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda})_{\mathfrak{m}})/J
has the following property in each of our three cases:

(cr-ord)

The restriction of
t_{\rho_{\mathfrak{m}}}
to
R_{\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}|_{G_{F_{\tilde{v}}}}}^{\square}
factors through
R_{\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}|_{G_{F_{\tilde{v}}}}}^{\triangle,\lambda_{\tilde{v}}}
and the restriction to
R_{\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}|_{G_{F_{\tilde{v}^{c}}}}}^{\square}
factors through
R_{\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}|_{G_{F_{\tilde{v}^{c}}}}}^{\mathrm{cris},\lambda_{\tilde{v}^{c}}}
.

(ord)

For
v|\bar{v}
, restriction of
t_{\rho_{\mathfrak{m}}}
to
R_{\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}|_{G_{F_{{v}}}}}^{\square}
factors through
R_{\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}|_{G_{F_{{v}}}}}^{\triangle,\lambda_{{v}}}
.

(cr)

For
v|\bar{v}
, restriction of
t_{\rho_{\mathfrak{m}}}
to
R_{\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}|_{G_{F_{{v}}}}}^{\square}
factors through
R_{\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}|_{G_{F_{{v}}}}}^{\mathrm{cris},\lambda_{{v}}}
.

Proof.

The first point is that is enough to prove our statement for Galois representations with coefficients in
{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}},\{\bar{v}-\ord\}}(R\Gamma(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}/\varpi^{m})_{\mathfrak{m}})
for integers
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
. This is because we have an isomorphism (by
[
NT16
, Lemma 3.11]
)

{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}},\{\bar{v}-\ord\}}(R\Gamma(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda})_{\mathfrak{m}})\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\to}\limits^{\sim}}\varprojlim_{m}{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}},\{\bar{v}-\ord\}}(R\Gamma(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}/\varpi^{m})_{\mathfrak{m}}).

In fact, we can prove the statement one cohomological degree at a time (cf. the proof of
[
ACC+18
, Theorem 4.5.1]
). Indeed, the kernel of the map

{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}},\{\bar{v}-\ord\}}(R\Gamma(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}/\varpi^{m})_{\mathfrak{m}})\to\prod_{q}{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}},\{\bar{v}-\ord\}}(H^{q}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}/\varpi^{m})_{\mathfrak{m}})

is a nilpotent ideal with vanishing
d
th power, and a Galois representation with coefficients in a quotient of
\prod_{q}{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}},\{\bar{v}-\ord\}}(H^{q}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}/\varpi^{m})_{\mathfrak{m}})
which satisfies condition (
•
‣
4.2.15
) can be conjugated to take values in the image of
{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}},\{\bar{v}-\ord\}}(R\Gamma(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}/\varpi^{m})_{\mathfrak{m}})
(by Carayol’s lemma, cf. the proof of
[
ACC+18
, Proposition 4.4.8]
).

Now our statement follows from Proposition
4.2.13
. This produces a lift of the map
R_{\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}|_{G_{F_{{v}}}}}^{\square}\to{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}},\{\bar{v}-\ord\}}(H^{q}(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}/\varpi^{m})_{\mathfrak{m}})/J
to a map with target a finite flat local
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra
R_{\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}|_{G_{F_{{v}}}}}^{\square}\to\widetilde{A}
. This map factors through the appropriate (crystalline or ordinary) quotient by the characterising property of this quotient.
∎

We also need a small refinement which will help us in a ‘fixed determinant’ setting:

Corollary 4.2.16
.

In the setting of Theorem
4.2.15
, assume moreover that
p\nmid n
and we have a quotient map
f:{\mathbb{T}}^{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\bar{v}},\{\bar{v}-\ord\}}(R\Gamma(X_{K},{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda})_{\mathfrak{m}})/J\to A
such that
\det(f_{\ast}(\rho_{\mathfrak{m}}))=\psi
for a character
\psi:G_{F,T}\to{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}
which is crystalline at all places in
S_{p}
with
\tau
-labelled Hodge–Tate weights
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{\tau,i}+(n-i)
for each
\tau:F\hookrightarrow E
.

Then for
v|\bar{v}
the induced map
R_{\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}|_{G_{F_{v}}}}^{\triangle,\lambda_{v}}\to A
or
R_{\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}|_{G_{F_{v}}}}^{\mathrm{cris},\lambda_{v}}\to A
factors through the appropriate fixed determinant
\psi
lifting ring (cf. §
3.3.5
).

Proof.

This follows from Lemma
3.3.6
.
∎

4.3.
The characteristic
0
case

For simplicity, we restrict to the crystalline case here. For this subsection, we drop our running assumption that
F
contains an imaginary quadratic field.

Theorem 4.3.1
.

Suppose
\pi
is cuspidal automorphic representation of
\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}}_{F})
, regular algebraic of weight
\lambda
, with
F
a totally real or CM field. Let
v
be a place of
F
dividing
p
and suppose
\pi^{\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{F_{v}})}
and
\pi^{\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{F_{v^{c}}})}
are both non-zero. (We allow the possibility that
v=v^{c}
, even in the CM case.) Let
\iota:\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}\to{\mathbb{C}}
be an isomorphism, and consider the continuous semisimple representation
r_{\iota}(\pi):G_{F}\to\GL_{n}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p})
constructed in
[
HLTT16
]
. Assume that:

(1)

\overline{r_{\iota}(\pi)}
is irreducible and decomposed generic.

Then
r_{\iota}(\pi)|_{G_{F_{v}}}
and
r_{\iota}(\pi)|_{G_{F_{v^{c}}}}
are crystalline with
\tau
-labelled Hodge–Tate weights
\lambda_{\iota\tau,n}<\cdots<\lambda_{\iota\tau,1}+n-1
for
\tau:F\to\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
inducing
v
or
v^{c}
respectively.

Proof.

Fix a prime
\ell
such that
\overline{r_{\iota}(\pi)}
satisfies the decomposed generic condition at
\ell
. Using cyclic base change and Theorem
4.2.15
, it suffices to find a cyclic CM extension
F^{\prime}/F
with the following properties:

(1)

F^{\prime}
is linearly disjoint from
\overline{F}^{\ker\overline{r_{\iota}(\pi)}}
over
F
.

(2)

F^{\prime}
contains an imaginary quadratic field.

(3)

Every
p
-adic place of
(F^{\prime})^{+}
splits in
F^{\prime}
.

(4)

\ell
splits completely in
F^{\prime}
.

(5)

The places
v,v^{c}
split completely in
F^{\prime}
.

(6)

There is a place
\bar{w}
of
(F^{\prime})^{+}
, lying over the place
\bar{v}|v
of
F^{+}
, and another
p
-adic place
\bar{w}^{\prime}
of
(F^{\prime})^{+}
such that

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bar{w}^{\prime\prime}|p\text{ in }(F^{\prime})^{+}\\
\bar{w}^{\prime\prime}\neq\bar{w},\bar{w}^{\prime}\end{subarray}}[(F^{\prime})^{+}_{\bar{w}^{\prime\prime}}:{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}]\geq\frac{1}{2}[(F^{\prime})^{+}:{\mathbb{Q}}].

We can achieve the final property by choosing
F^{\prime}
with
[(F^{\prime})^{+}:F^{+}]\geq 4
and with
\bar{v}
split completely in
(F^{\prime})^{+}
, and then choosing
\bar{w},\bar{w}^{\prime}
to be two distinct places of
(F^{\prime})^{+}
lying over
\bar{v}
. We conclude that it is possible to find such an extension
F^{\prime}
. ∎

5.
Automorphy lifting

5.1.
A potentially Barsotti–Tate modularity lifting theorem

We begin by stating the main theorem in this section. In order to get an optimal result for applications to modularity of elliptic curves, we only consider Galois representations with inverse-cyclotomic determinant.

Theorem 5.2
.

Let
F
be an imaginary CM field and let
p
be an odd prime. Suppose given a continuous representation
\rho:G_{F}\to\GL_{2}(\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
satisfying the following conditions:

(1)

\rho
is unramified almost everywhere and
\det(\rho)=\epsilon_{p}^{-1}
.

(2)

For each place
v|p
of
F
, the representation
\rho|_{G_{F_{v}}}
is potentially semistable with all labelled Hodge–Tate weights equal to
(0,1)
.

(3)

\overline{\rho}
is decomposed generic (Definition
2.1.27
) and
\overline{\rho}|_{G_{F(\zeta_{p})}}
is irreducible.

(4)

If
p=5
and the projective image of
\overline{\rho}(G_{F(\zeta_{5})})
is conjugate to
\PSL_{2}({\mathbb{F}}_{5})
, we assume further that the extension of
F
cut out by the projective image of
\overline{\rho}
does not contain
\zeta_{5}
.

(5)

There exists a cuspidal automorphic representation
\pi
of
\PGL_{2}(\mathbf{A}_{F})
and an isomorphism
\iota:\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}\cong{\mathbb{C}}
satisfying the following conditions:

(a)

\pi
is regular algebraic of weight
0
.

(b)

For each place
v|p
where
\rho|_{G_{F_{v}}}
is potentially crystalline,
r_{\iota}(\pi)|_{G_{F_{v}}}
is potentially ordinary of weight
0
(in the sense of
[
Ger19
, §5.2]
) if and only if
\rho|_{G_{F_{v}}}
is potentially ordinary of weight
0
. We moreover assume that
\mathrm{rec}_{F_{v}}(\pi_{v})
has monodromy operator
0
.

(c)

For each place
v|p
where
\rho|_{G_{F_{v}}}
is not potentially crystalline,
\pi
is
\iota
-ordinary of weight
0
at
v
and
r_{\iota}(\pi)|_{G_{F_{v}}}
is not potentially crystalline.

(d)

There is an isomorphism
\overline{\rho}\cong\overline{r}_{\iota}(\pi)
.

Then
\rho
is automorphic: there exists a cuspidal automorphic representation
\Pi
of
\PGL_{2}(\mathbf{A}_{F})
, regular algebraic of weight
0
, such that
\rho\cong r_{\iota}(\Pi)
.

Remark 5.2.1
.

Using Theorem
4.2.15
, we can replace the assumption that
r_{\iota}(\pi)|_{G_{F_{v}}}
is potentially ordinary of weight
0
at certain places
v
with an assumption on
\pi_{v}
.

Remark 5.2.2
.

We restrict to Galois representations with inverse-cyclotomic determinant, as in
[
AKT23
]
, so that we can handle the case where
p=3
and the image of
\overline{\rho}(G_{F(\zeta_{3})})
is
\SL_{2}({\mathbb{F}}_{3})
.

Remark 5.2.3
.

We could consider also the Fontaine–Laffaille case, for arbitrary dimension
n
Galois representations – namely, assume that

p
is unramified in
F
and that the weight
\lambda
satisfies the condition:

(5.2.1)

\lambda_{\tau,1}-\lambda_{\tau,n}<p-n\ \mathrm{for}\ \mathrm{all}\ \tau\in\Hom(F,E).

Using the local-global compatibility result given by Theorem
4.2.15
instead of Theorem
[
ACC+18
, Theorem 4.5.1]
, one could also prove an automorphy lifting theorem in this case that strenghtens
[
ACC+18
, Theorem 6.1.1]
. The restrictions on the Hodge–Tate weights in the automorphy lifting theorem in
loc. cit.
are stronger than the condition in (
5.2.1
) only because of restrictions in the corresponding result on local-global compatibility. The rest of the argument would go through verbatim.

5.3.
Galois deformation theory

To prove our automorphy lifting theorem we apply the patching method in
[
ACC+18
, §6]
, making modifications as in
[
AKT23
]
to avoid the assumption that there is a
\sigma\in G_{F}-G_{F(\zeta_{p})}
such that
\overline{\rho}(\sigma)
is scalar and to include cases with
p=3
or
5
where
\overline{\rho}|_{G_{F(\zeta_{p})}}
does not have enormous image. In addition, we work with local lifting rings at
p
that have two irreducible components (either ordinary/non-ordinary or crystalline ordinary/non-crystalline ordinary), which is why we need assumption (
5b
) in this theorem. The fact that these local lifting rings have generically reduced special fibre, which we will recall shortly, is important for implementing (derived) Ihara avoidance in a situation where local lifting rings have more than one component.

We adopt all the terminology and notation of
[
ACC+18
, §6.2.1]
, although the coefficient ring ‘
\Lambda
’ appearing there will always be
{\mathcal{O}}
for us, and our Galois representations will all be two-dimensional. So, we fix a continuous and absolutely irreducible
\overline{\rho}:G_{F}\to\GL_{2}(k)
, and let
S
be a finite set of finite places of
F
containing
S_{p}
and all the places where
\overline{\rho}
is ramified.

For each
v\in S
, we have a local lifting ring
R_{v}^{\square}
for
\overline{\rho}_{v}:=\overline{\rho}|_{G_{F_{v}}}
and the notion of a local deformation problem
{\mathcal{D}}_{v}
: a set valued functor on
\operatorname{CNL}_{{\mathcal{O}}}
satisfying some conditions which in particular imply that it is represented by a quotient
R_{v}
of
R_{v}^{\square}
.

A global deformation problem is a tuple

(\overline{\rho},S,\{R_{v}\}_{v\in S}),

where for each
v\in S
,
R_{v}
is a quotient of
R_{v}^{\square}
representing a local deformation problem for
\overline{\rho}_{v}
.

A global deformation problem with fixed determinant is a tuple

(\overline{\rho},\psi,S,\{R_{v}\}_{v\in S}),

where
\psi:G_{F,S}\to{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}
is a character which lifts
\det(\overline{\rho})
,
(\overline{\rho},S,\{R_{v}\}_{v\in S})
is a global deformation problem, and the lifts parameterized by each
R_{v}
have determinant
\psi|_{G_{F_{v}}}
.

If
{\mathcal{S}}
is a global deformation problem (with or without fixed determinant), and
T
is a subset of
S
, we have the functor
{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{S}}}^{T}
of
T
-framed deformations of type
{\mathcal{S}}
. When
T=\emptyset
, we denote the functor (of deformations of type
{\mathcal{S}}
) by
{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{S}}}
.

The functors
{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{S}}}
and
{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{S}}}^{T}
are represented by
\operatorname{CNL}_{{\mathcal{O}}}
-algebras
R_{\mathcal{S}}
and
R_{{\mathcal{S}}}^{T}
, respectively.

For a global deformation problem
{\mathcal{S}}=(\overline{\rho},S,\{R_{v}\}_{v\in S})
(or one with fixed determinant,
{\mathcal{S}}=(\overline{\rho},\psi,S,\{R_{v}\}_{v\in S})
) and
T\subset S
, we define
R_{{\mathcal{S}}}^{T,\mathrm{loc}}=\widehat{\otimes}_{v\in T}R_{v}
. There is a natural local
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra map
R_{{\mathcal{S}}}^{T,\mathrm{loc}}\to R_{{\mathcal{S}}}^{T}
.

We are assuming that
p
is odd. We will make use of the following local deformation problems (which we identify in terms of their representing ring), where
\psi
always denotes a fixed determinant character.

•

Fixed determinant lifting rings
R_{v}^{\psi}
, parameterizing lifts of
\overline{\rho}|_{G_{F_{v}}}
with determinant
\psi|_{G_{F_{v}}}
. We will make use of these rings when
v\notin S_{p}
and
H^{2}(F_{v},\mathrm{ad}^{0}\overline{\rho})=0
, in which case
R_{v}^{\psi}
is formally smooth over
{\mathcal{O}}
of relative dimension
3
.

•

Fixed determinant ‘level raising’ lifting rings
R_{v}^{\psi,\chi}
, for
v
with
q_{v}\equiv 1\mod p
and
\overline{\rho}|_{G_{F_{v}}}
trivial, and a character
\chi:{\mathcal{O}}_{F_{v}}^{\times}\to{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}
which is trivial modulo
\varpi
(cf.
[
AKT23
, §A.1.2]
). They classify lifts
\rho
with determinant
\psi
and characteristic polynomial

\mathrm{char}_{\rho(\sigma)}(X)=(X-\chi({\operatorname{Art}}_{F_{v}}^{-1}(\sigma)))(X-\chi^{-1}\psi({\operatorname{Art}}_{F_{v}}^{-1}(\sigma)))

for all
\sigma\in I_{F_{v}}
.

•

Barsotti–Tate lifting rings
R_{v}^{\psi,\mathrm{BT}}
for
v|p
. These are the rings
R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\mathrm{cris},(0,0)_{\tau\in\Hom(F_{v},E)},\psi}
in the notation of §
3.3
. Note that we assume that
\psi|_{G_{F_{v}}}
is crystalline with all labelled Hodge–Tate weights equal to
1
and that it lifts
\det\overline{\rho}_{v}
for this ring to be defined. In practice, we will take
\psi
to be the inverse of the cyclotomic character.

5.3.1.
Ordinary deformation rings

We will also use, when
v\mid p
, the ordinary lifting ring
R_{v}^{\triangle,(0,0)_{\tau\in\Hom(F_{v},E)},\psi}
with fixed determinant and Hodge–Tate weights
(0,1)
. For simplicity, we only consider the case
\psi=\epsilon_{p}^{-1}
, with
\overline{\rho}|_{G_{F_{v}}}
trivial and
\overline{\epsilon}_{p}
trivial on
G_{F_{v}}
, and set

R_{v}^{\triangle}:=R_{v}^{\triangle,(0,0)_{\tau\in\Hom(F_{v},E)},\psi}

in the notation of §
3.3
.

We recall some properties of
R_{v}^{\triangle}
, following
[
Sno18
]
. Note that twisting by the cyclotomic character and using the reducedness of
R_{v}^{\triangle}
(Theorem
3.3.3
) shows that our lifting ring can indeed be identified with the ring denoted by
R
in
[
Sno18
, Proposition 4.3.2]
.

Proposition 5.3.2
.

(1)

\Spec(R_{v}^{\triangle})
is equidimensional of dimension
[F_{v}:{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}]+4
, with two irreducible components
X^{cr}=\Spec(R_{v}^{\triangle,cr}),X^{st}=\Spec(R_{v}^{\triangle,st})
characterized by their points valued in finite extensions
E^{\prime}/E
:

•

x:R_{v}^{\triangle}\to E^{\prime}
factors through
R_{v}^{\triangle,cr}
if and only if
\rho_{x}
is crystalline.

•

x:R_{v}^{\triangle}\to E^{\prime}
factors through
R_{v}^{\triangle,st}
if and only if
\rho_{x}
is conjugate to a representation of the form
\begin{pmatrix}1&*\\
0&\epsilon_{p}^{-1}\end{pmatrix}.

(2)

Each generic point of
\Spec(R_{v}^{\triangle}/\varpi)
is the specialization of a unique generic point of
\Spec(R_{v}^{\triangle})
.

Proof.

The first part follows from
[
Sno18
, Proposition 4.3.2]
. The second part also follows from Snowden’s results, as we now explain. It suffices to show that the dimension of
X^{cr}\cap X^{st}\cap\Spec(R_{v}^{\triangle}/\varpi)
is
<[F_{v}:{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}]+3
, as this shows that there is no point of large enough dimension to be a generic point of
\Spec(R_{v}^{\triangle}/\varpi)
generalizing to both generic points of
\Spec(R_{v}^{\triangle})
.

Snowden defines another ring
\widetilde{R}_{v}^{\triangle}

[
Sno18
, Proposition 4.4.3]
. The ring
\widetilde{R}_{v}^{\triangle}
is a quotient (
{\mathcal{O}}
-flat and reduced) of the finite
R_{v}^{\square,\epsilon_{p}^{-1}}
-algebra given by adjoining a root of the characteristic polynomial of a lift of Frobenius under the universal lifting of
\overline{\rho}|_{G_{F_{v}}}
. It comes with a finite morphism
\pi:\Spec(\widetilde{R}_{v}^{\triangle})\to\Spec(R_{v}^{\triangle})
which is an isomorphism after inverting
p
. In particular,
\pi
is surjective and induces a bijection between irreducible components. We denote the irreducible components of
\Spec(\widetilde{R}_{v}^{\triangle})
lying over
X^{cr}
and
X^{st}
by
\widetilde{X}^{cr}
and
\widetilde{X}^{st}
respectively. The map
\pi
induces a finite surjective map
\widetilde{X}^{st}\cap\widetilde{X}^{cr}\cap\Spec(\widetilde{R}_{v}^{\triangle}/\varpi)\to X^{cr}\cap X^{st}\cap\Spec(R_{v}^{\triangle}/\varpi)
. Indeed, it follows from
[
Sno18
, Theorem 4.6.1, Lemma 4.6.4]
that a point of
\widetilde{X}^{cr}\cap\Spec(\widetilde{R}_{v}^{\triangle}/\varpi)
is contained in
\widetilde{X}^{st}
if and only if an element lifting Frobenius has unipotent image under the corresponding Galois representation. This condition holds for every element of
X^{st}\cap\Spec(R_{v}^{\triangle}/\varpi)
. We can now bound the dimension of the target of this map by bounding the dimension of the source. Snowden explicitly describes the mod
\varpi
fibre
\Spec(\widetilde{R}_{v}^{\triangle}/\varpi)

[
Sno18
, Lemma 4.6.4]
, and its two irreducible components intersect in a proper closed subset (of dimension
[F_{v}:{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}]+2
).
∎

Lemma 5.3.3
.

Each generic point of
\Spec(R_{v}^{\epsilon_{p}^{-1},\mathrm{BT}}/\varpi)
is the specialization
of a unique generic point of
\Spec(R_{v}^{\epsilon_{p}^{-1},\mathrm{BT}})
.

Proof.

We let
R=R_{v}^{\epsilon_{p}^{-1},\mathrm{BT}}
. The lemma follows from generic reducedness of
\Spec(R/\varpi)
and the fact that every generic point of
\Spec(R)
has characteristic
0
. The generic reducedness follows from
[
CEGS25
, Theorem 1.3]
. This proposition applies to the lifting ring
R_{v}^{\mathrm{BT}}=R_{v}^{\psi,(0,1)_{\tau\in\Hom(F_{v},\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p})}}
without fixed determinant, but since
p
is odd
R_{v}^{\mathrm{BT}}
is formally smooth over
R
. So we deduce that
R/\varpi
is also generically reduced. Every generic point of
\Spec(R)
has characteristic
0
because
R
is
{\mathcal{O}}
-flat. To deduce the claim about generic points of
\Spec(R/\varpi)
, let
\mathfrak{p}\in\Spec(R)
be the image of a generic point of
\Spec(R/\varpi)
. Since
\Spec(R/\varpi)
is generically reduced,
R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\varpi R_{\mathfrak{p}}=(R/\varpi)_{\mathfrak{p}}
is a field. Now we know that
R_{\mathfrak{p}}
is a Noetherian local ring with a principal maximal ideal; it is therefore a local principal ideal ring with non-zero ideals generated by powers of
\varpi
. Since
\mathfrak{p}
is not a generic point of
\Spec(R)
,
R_{\mathfrak{p}}
has dimension
1
and
(0)
is its unique minimal prime (in particular,
R_{\mathfrak{p}}
is a DVR).
∎

Lemma 5.3.4
.

Let
v
be a place of
F
with
v|p
. Suppose that
\overline{\rho}|_{G_{F_{v}}}
is trivial, the residue field
k_{v}
is not equal to
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
, and
R_{v}^{\epsilon_{p}^{-1},\mathrm{BT}}
is non-zero. Then
\Spec(R_{v}^{\epsilon_{p}^{-1},\mathrm{BT}})
has exactly two irreducible components, one whose points correspond to ordinary Galois representations and one whose points correspond to non-ordinary Galois representations.

Proof.

This follows from results of Kisin
[
Kis09
, Corollary 2.5.16]
and Gee
[
Gee06
, Proposition 2.3]
.
∎

Definition 5.3.5
.

A Taylor–Wiles datum for a global deformation problem
{\mathcal{S}}
is a tuple
(Q,N,(\alpha_{v,1},\alpha_{v,2})_{v\in Q})
consisting
of:

•

A finite set of finite places
Q
of
F
, disjoint from
S
, and a positive integer
N
such that
q_{v}\equiv 1\mod p^{N}
for each
v\in Q
.

•

For each
v\in Q
and
i\in\{1,2\}
, distinct unramified
k
-valued characters
\alpha_{v,1},\alpha_{v,2}
such that
\overline{\rho}|_{G_{F_{v}}}\cong\bigoplus_{i=1}^{2}\alpha_{v,i}
.

We call
N
the level of the Taylor–Wiles datum.

If
(Q,N,(\alpha_{v,1},\alpha_{v,2})_{v\in Q})
is a Taylor–Wiles datum for
{\mathcal{S}}
, then we define a new global deformation problem

{\mathcal{S}}_{Q}=(\overline{\rho},S\cup Q,\{R_{v}\}_{v\in S}\cup\{R_{v}^{\square}\}_{v\in Q})

(respectively,
{\mathcal{S}}_{Q}=(\overline{\rho},\psi,S\cup Q,\{R_{v}\}_{v\in S}\cup\{R_{v}^{\psi}\}_{v\in Q})
if
{\mathcal{S}}
has fixed determinant).

5.4.
Patching

Our set-up is very close to that of
[
ACC+18
, §6]
. First we give an axiomatic description of the kinds of objects which will be the output of the patching method, and deduce a formal modularity lifting result.

We assume given the following objects:

(1)

A power series ring
S_{\infty}={\mathcal{O}}[[X_{1},\cdots,X_{r}]]
with augmentation ideal
\mathfrak{a}_{\infty}=(X_{1},\dots,X_{r})
.

(2)

Perfect complexes
C_{\infty},C^{\prime}_{\infty}
of
S_{\infty}
-modules, and a fixed isomorphism

C_{\infty}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{S_{\infty}}S_{\infty}/\varpi\cong C^{\prime}_{\infty}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{S_{\infty}}S_{\infty}/\varpi

in
\mathbf{D}(S_{\infty}/\varpi)
.

(3)

Two
S_{\infty}
-subalgebras

T_{\infty}\subset\End_{\mathbf{D}(S_{\infty})}(C_{\infty})

and

T^{\prime}_{\infty}\subset\End_{\mathbf{D}(S_{\infty})}(C^{\prime}_{\infty}),

which have the same image in

\End_{\mathbf{D}(S_{\infty}/\varpi)}(C_{\infty}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{S_{\infty}}S_{\infty}/\varpi)=\End_{\mathbf{D}(S_{\infty}/\varpi)}(C^{\prime}_{\infty}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{S_{\infty}}S_{\infty}/\varpi),

where these endomorphism algebras are identified using the fixed isomorphism in (
2
). Call this common image

\overline{T}_{\infty}
. Note that
T_{\infty}
and
T^{\prime}_{\infty}
are finite

S_{\infty}
-algebras.

(4)

Two Noetherian complete local
S_{\infty}
-algebras
R_{\infty}
and
R^{\prime}_{\infty}
and
surjections
R_{\infty}\twoheadrightarrow T_{\infty}/I_{\infty}
,
R^{\prime}_{\infty}\twoheadrightarrow T^{\prime}_{\infty}/I^{\prime}_{\infty}
, where
I_{\infty}
and
I^{\prime}_{\infty}
are nilpotent ideals.
We write
\overline{I}_{\infty}
and
\overline{I}^{\prime}_{\infty}
for the image of these
ideals in
\overline{T}_{\infty}
. Note that it then makes sense to
talk about the support of
H^{*}(C_{\infty})
and
H^{*}(C^{\prime}_{\infty})
over
R_{\infty}
,

R^{\prime}_{\infty}
, even though they are not genuine modules over these rings. These
supports actually belong to the closed subsets of
\Spec R_{\infty}
,
\Spec R^{\prime}_{\infty}
given by
\Spec T_{\infty}
,
\Spec T^{\prime}_{\infty}
, and hence are finite
over
\Spec S_{\infty}
.

(5)

An isomorphism
R_{\infty}/\varpi\cong R^{\prime}_{\infty}/\varpi

compatible with the
S_{\infty}
-algebra structure and the actions
(induced from
T_{\infty}
and
T^{\prime}_{\infty}
) on

H^{*}(C_{\infty}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{S_{\infty}}S_{\infty}/\varpi)/(\overline{I}_{\infty}+\overline{I}^{\prime}_{\infty})=H^{*}(C^{\prime}_{\infty}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{S_{\infty}}S_{\infty}/\varpi)/(\overline{I}_{\infty}+\overline{I}^{\prime}_{\infty}),

where these cohomology groups are identified using the fixed isomorphism.

(6)

Integers
q_{0}\in\mathbf{Z}
and
l_{0}\in\mathbf{Z}_{\geq 0}
.

Assumption 5.4.1
.

Our set-up is assumed to satisfy the following:

(1)

\dim R_{\infty}=\dim R^{\prime}_{\infty}=\dim S_{\infty}-l_{0}
, and
\dim R_{\infty}/\varpi=\dim R^{\prime}_{\infty}/\varpi=\dim S_{\infty}-l_{0}-1
.

(2)

(Behavior of components) Assume that each generic point of
\Spec R_{\infty}/\varpi
is the specialization of unique generic points of
\Spec R_{\infty}
and
\Spec R^{\prime}_{\infty}
. Moreover, we assume that
\Spec R_{\infty}
and
\Spec R^{\prime}_{\infty}
are equidimensional and that all of their generic points have characteristic
0
. This implies that
\Spec R_{\infty}/\varpi
,
\Spec R^{\prime}_{\infty}/\varpi
are equidimensional of dimension one less (by the principal ideal theorem).

(3)

(Generic concentration)
We have

H^{\ast}(C_{\infty}\otimes_{S_{\infty}}^{\mathbf{L}}S_{\infty}/\mathfrak{a}_{\infty})[\frac{1}{p}]\neq 0,

and these groups are non-zero only for degrees in the interval
[q_{0},q_{0}+l_{0}]
.

(4)

(Automorphic point) We fix a characteristic
0
point
x\in\Spec(T_{\infty}/\mathfrak{a}_{\infty}T_{\infty})
.

Note that
\Supp_{R_{\infty}}(H^{*}(C_{\infty}))=\Spec T_{\infty}
and
